and hence

\sum_{i=1}^{N}[\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}_{E_{j,i}})(x)]^{q_{2}}\geq N\frac{1}{4^{q_{2}}}.

By the arbitrariness of
j\in[2^{N}-1]
,
we find that, for all
x\in(2^{-N},1]\times(0,1]^{n-1}
,

\sup_{j\in[2^{N}-1]}\Bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}[\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}_{E_{j,i}})(x)]^{q_{2}}\Bigg\}^{\frac{1}{q_{2}}}\geq N^{\frac{1}{q_{2}}}\frac{1}{4}.

Then, for any
x\in(2^{-N},1]^{n}\times(0,1]^{n-1}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{M}_{x}g_{N}(x,y,z)\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}(\Omega_{1})L^{q_{2}}_{z}(\Omega_{2})}

\displaystyle\quad=\mu_{2}(G_{1})^{\frac{1}{q_{2}}}\Bigg\|\Bigg[\sum_{j\in[2^{N}-1]}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}_{E_{j,i}})(x)]^{q_{2}}\mathbf{1}_{F_{j}}(y)\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{q_{2}}}\Bigg\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}(\Omega_{1})}

\displaystyle\quad=\mu_{2}(G_{1})^{\frac{1}{q_{2}}}\sup_{j\in[2^{N}-1]}\Bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}[\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{1}_{E_{j,i}})(x)]^{q_{2}}\Bigg\}^{\frac{1}{q_{2}}}\geq\mu_{2}(G_{1})^{\frac{1}{q_{2}}}N^{\frac{1}{q_{2}}}\frac{1}{4},

which further implies that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{M}_{x}g_{N}(x,y,z)\|_{L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{n})L^{\infty}_{y}(\Omega_{1})L^{q_{2}}_{z}(\Omega_{2})}\gtrsim\mu_{2}(G_{1})^{\frac{1}{q_{2}}}\frac{N^{\frac{1}{q_{2}}}}{4}.

This contradicts (
4.15
); hence
q_{2}=\infty
, which completes
the proof of (ii) and hence Theorem
4.2
.
∎

4.3.
Carleson embedding-type inequalities

In this subsection, aiming for a multi-parameter extension of the Carleson-embedding type inequalities that Frazier and Roudenko
[
67
, Theorem 3.7]
attribute to Fedor Nazarov, we look for conditions on a sequence of pointwise multipliers
\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
to obtain an inequality of the form

\big\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}\leq C\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}

(4.16)

for all function sequences of the type that each
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
is constant on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,
where
C
is a positive constant independent of

\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
. Certainly, we could always estimate with

C\leq\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{\infty}\ell^{\infty}},

which does not exploit the assumed piecewise constancy of
the functions
\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
and is much too crude for most applications
expect the case where
p=\infty
.
The following lemma can be proved by some simple computations;
we omit the details.

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
and
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
.
Then, for all measurable functions
\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
and
all
\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\in(L^{\vec{\infty}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}

with each
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
constant on every
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,

\big\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\vec{\infty}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}\leq\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{\infty}\ell^{\infty}}\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\vec{\infty}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}},

where this inequality is
sharp
in the sense that

\sup_{\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{\vec{\infty}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}\neq 0}\frac{\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{\vec{\infty}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}}{\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{\vec{\infty}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}}=\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{\infty}\ell^{\infty}}.

Now, we consider the case where
p\in(0,\infty)
.
Necessary conditions can be obtained by “testing” the inequality (
4.16
) with special choices of functions
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
.
Given
\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
and
\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
, we denote

\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}:=\bigcup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{R\in\mathscr D_{\vi}(\R^{\vn})}{R\subset\Omega}}R;

hence
\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}\subset\Omega
, and
\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}}
is piecewise constant on the rectangles
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.

By “testing” (
4.16
)
with
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=\delta_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}}
,
we obtain the necessary condition

\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\|\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{\textit{\L}^{p}(\Omega)}\leq C.

In particular, noting that
P_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}=P
for
P\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, we have

\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}\leq C.

(4.17)

In the special case of a Besov-type space
\ell^{\vec{q}}L^{p}
, this simple necessary condition turns out to be sufficient as well:

Proposition 4.13
.

If
p\in(0,\infty)
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
,
and
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
is constant on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,
then, for every measurable function
\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
and
every
\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\in\ell^{\vec{q}}L^{p}
,

\big\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\ell^{\vec{q}}L^{p}}\leq\bigg[\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}\bigg]\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\ell^{\vec{q}}L^{p}},

(4.18)

where this inequality is
sharp
in the sense that

\sup_{\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\ell^{\vec{q}}L^{p}}\neq 0}\frac{\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\ell^{\vec{q}}L^{p}}}{\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\ell^{\vec{q}}L^{p}}}=\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}.

Proof.

If the supremum is zero or infinity,
then (
4.18
) holds trivially;
otherwise we may assume by scaling that it is one.
Let
f_{R}
be the constant value of
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
on
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
. Then

\begin{split}\|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}^{p}&=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\|\mathbf{1}_{R}\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}^{p}=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\|\mathbf{1}_{R}\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}^{p}|f_{R}|^{p}\\
&\leq\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|R|\,|f_{R}|^{p}=\|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}^{p}.\end{split}

Taking the
\ell^{\vec{q}}
quasi-norm of both sides proves (
4.18
).
The sharpness follows from (
4.18
) and (
4.17
).
This finishes the proof of Proposition
4.13
.
∎

For all other spaces of type
(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}
,
the situation becomes more complicated.
The following lemma is a multi-parameter extension of

[
67
, Theorem 3.7(i)]
, which the authors of
[
67
]

attributed to Fedor Nazarov. The proof below
is a relatively straightforward modification,
only using the strong maximal operator in place of the classical one.

Lemma 4.14
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty)
,
\vec{0}<\vec{q}\leq\vec{p}:=p\cdot\vec{1}
,

s\in(p,\infty)
, and
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
.
Then there exists a positive constant
C
such that,
for all measurable functions
\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
and
all
\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\in(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}

with each
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
constant on every
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,

\big\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}\leq C\bigg[\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(R)}\bigg]\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}.

Proof.

If the supremum is infinite or zero, there is nothing to prove. Otherwise, we may assume by scaling that it is equal to one.

Let us begin by noting that, using the notation

\langle g\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}:=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\langle g\rangle_{R}\mathbf{1}_{R},

this rescaled assumption can be stated as
\langle|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{s}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\leq 1
.

Let
r\in(0,\min(\vec{p},\vec{q}))
so that

\frac{\vec{p}}{r},\frac{\vec{q}}{r}\in(1,\infty]^{k}
. Then we can dualise

\begin{split}\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}^{r}&=\big\|\{|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}/r}\ell^{\vec{q}/r})_{\pi}}\\
&=\sup\Bigg\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}:\|\{g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}\|_{(L^{(\vec{p}/r)^{\prime}}\ell^{(\vec{q}/r)^{\prime}})_{\pi}}\leq 1\Bigg\}.\end{split}

(4.19)

Recalling that
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
is constant on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
, it follows that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\langle|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}.

By Hölder’s inequality on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
and our assumption of
\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
,

\langle|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\leq\Big<|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r\frac{s}{r}}\Big>_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}^{\frac{r}{s}}\Big<|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{(\frac{s}{r})^{\prime}}\Big>_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}^{\frac{1}{(s/r)^{\prime}}}\lesssim\Big[\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}\Big(|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{(\frac{s}{r})^{\prime}}\Big)\Big]^{\frac{1}{(s/r)^{\prime}}}.

Hence

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\Big[\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}\Big(|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{(\frac{s}{r})^{\prime}}\Big)\Big]^{\frac{1}{(s/r)^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\{|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{(L^{\frac{\vec{p}}{r}}\ell^{\frac{\vec{q}}{r}})_{\pi}}\Big\|\Big\{\Big[\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}\Big(|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{(\frac{s}{r})^{\prime}}\Big)\Big]^{\frac{1}{(s/r)^{\prime}}}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{(L^{(\frac{\vec{p}}{r})^{\prime}}\ell^{(\frac{\vec{q}}{r})^{\prime}})_{\pi}}

\displaystyle=\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}^{r}\Big\|\Big\{\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}\Big(|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{(\frac{s}{r})^{\prime}}\Big)\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{(L^{\frac{(\vec{p}/r)^{\prime}}{(s/r)^{\prime}}}\ell^{\frac{(\vec{q}/r)^{\prime}}{(s/r)^{\prime}}})_{\pi}}^{\frac{1}{(s/r)^{\prime}}}.

Since
\infty>s>p\geq q_{i}>r>0
, it follows that

\infty>\frac{s}{r}>\frac{p}{r}\geq\frac{q_{i}}{r}>1
, hence

1<(\frac{s}{r})^{\prime}<(\frac{p}{r})^{\prime}\leq(\frac{q_{i}}{r})^{\prime}<\infty,

and thus

\vec{\infty}>\frac{(\vec{q}/r)^{\prime}}{(s/r)^{\prime}}\geq\frac{(\vec{p}/r)^{\prime}}{(s/r)^{\prime}}>\vec{1}.

Then, by Lemma
4.5
,
we find that
\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}
is bounded on
(L^{\frac{(\vec{p}/r)^{\prime}}{(s/r)^{\prime}}}\ell^{\frac{(\vec{q}/r)^{\prime}}{(s/r)^{\prime}}})_{\pi}
, and we conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}^{r}\Big\|\Big\{|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{(\frac{s}{r})^{\prime}}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{(L^{\frac{(\vec{p}/r)^{\prime}}{(s/r)^{\prime}}}\ell^{\frac{(\vec{q}/r)^{\prime}}{(s/r)^{\prime}}})_{\pi}}^{\frac{1}{(s/r)^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}^{r}\big\|\{g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{(\vec{p}/r)^{\prime}}\ell^{(\vec{q}/r)^{\prime}})_{\pi}}.

Substituting back to (
4.19
), we obtain

\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}^{r}\lesssim\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}^{r}.

This finishes the proof of Lemma
4.14
.
∎

Remark 4.15
.

In Lemma
4.14
,
the bound

\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(R)}

(4.20)

can be relaxed to

\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}

if
(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}
reduces to Besov-type.
However, if
(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}
is “truly” of Triebel–Lizorkin-type,
then the bound (
4.20
) is sharp;
see Proposition
4.23
.

In order to discuss versions of Lemma
4.14
without the restriction to
\vec{q}\leq p\cdot\vec{1}
, we will need some additional tools.

Definition 4.16
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty)
,
q\in(0,\infty]
, and
X
be a Banach space.
Let
f:\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\to X
be strongly measurable (i.e., a pointwise limit of simple functions, see
[
84
, Chapter 1]
).
Its Lorentz quasi-norm is defined as

\|f\|_{L^{p,q}(X)}:=\begin{cases}\displaystyle p^{\frac{1}{q}}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{q-1}|\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}:\ \|f(x)\|_{X}>s\}|^{\frac{q}{p}}\,ds\bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}},&\text{if }q\in(0,\infty),\\
\displaystyle\sup_{s\in(0,\infty)}s|\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}:\ \|f(x)\|_{X}>s\}|^{\frac{1}{p}},&\text{if }q=\infty.\end{cases}

Let us recall the following result concerning the real interpolation
of Banach space
X
-valued Lorentz spaces
L^{p,r}(X)
;
see
[
146
, Section 1.18.6, Theorem 2]
.

Lemma 4.17
.

Let
p_{0},p_{1}\in(1,\infty)
satisfy
p_{0}\neq p_{1}
,
and let
r_{0},r_{1},r\in[1,\infty]
.
Assume that
\theta\in(0,1)
and
\frac{1}{p}=\frac{1-\theta}{p_{0}}+\frac{\theta}{p_{1}}
.
Then, for any Banach space
X
,

(L^{p_{0},r_{0}}(X),L^{p_{1},r_{1}}(X))_{\theta,r}=L^{p,r}(X)

with equivalent norms.

Remark 4.18
.

In contrast to the clean statement of Lemma
4.17
,
interpolation of
L^{p_{i},r_{i}}(X_{i})
with two different
X_{i}

is more tricky; see
[
40
]
. This issue does not appear in
Lemma
4.17
, where only one
X
is involved throughout.

The following lemma is a multi-parameter extension of
[
67
, Theorem 3.7(ii)]
, which the authors of
[
67
]

attributed to Fedor Nazarov. The proofs of both
[
67
, Theorem 3.7(ii)]
and Lemma
4.19
below rely on interpolation; however, the details are substantially different. In
[
67
, Theorem 3.7(ii)]
, where the sequence space has just one exponent
q
in place of our
\vec{q}
, the interpolation is done between the end-points
q\in\{1,\infty\}
, for a fixed
p
. In contrast, we work with a fixed
\vec{q}
, and interpolate with respect to the parameter
p
, for two exponent
p_{i}
close to the desired
p
. This is inspired by a similar interpolation strategy used in
[
83
, Section 8]
.

On the level of the actual result, we will now need to deal with general open sets
\Omega
, in contrast to dyadic rectangles that were enough in Lemma
4.14
.

Lemma 4.19
.

If
p\in(1,\infty)
,
\vec{q}\in(1,\infty)^{k}
, and
s\in(p,\infty)
,
then there exists a positive constant
C
such that,
for every sequence
\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\in L^{p}\ell^{\vec{q}}
and
all measurable functions
\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
,

\big\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\langle f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{\vec{q}}}\leq C\bigg[\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Big\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(\Omega)}\bigg]\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{\vec{q}}}.

(4.21)

Proof.

We first assume that
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=\langle f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
takes a constant value
f_{R}
on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
. Let us also write
\gamma_{R}:=\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|_{R}
for
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, although this need not be a constant. If the supremum in the claim is zero or infinity, there is nothing to prove; otherwise we may assume by scaling that it is one. We also assume that
\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{\vec{q}}}<\infty
, for otherwise there is nothing to prove.

For every
\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}
, let

\Omega_{\kappa}:=\Big\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}:\ \|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x)\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\ell^{\vec{q}}}>2^{\kappa}\Big\}

and

\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}:=\Big\{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}):\ |R\cap\Omega_{\kappa}|>\frac{1}{2}|R|\Big\}.

We claim that, for each
R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
with
f_{R}\neq 0
, there is a maximal
\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
R\in\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}
: On the one hand, for all
x\in R
, we have
\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x)\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\ell^{\vec{q}}}\geq|f_{R}|
; hence
R\subset\Omega_{\kappa}
and thus
R\in\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}
for all
\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}
with
2^{\kappa}<|f_{R}|
. On the other hand,
if
R\in\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}
, then

\infty>\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}}}\geq 2^{\kappa}|\Omega_{\kappa}|^{\frac{1}{p}}\geq 2^{\kappa}|\Omega_{\kappa}\cap R|^{\frac{1}{p}}\geq 2^{\kappa}(2^{-1}|R|)^{\frac{1}{p}},

which cannot hold for arbitrarily large
\kappa
.
Thus,
R\in\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}
for all small enough
\kappa
, and
R\notin\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}
for all large enough
\kappa
. This confirms the existence of a maximal
\kappa
with
R\in\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}
, and hence
R\in\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}\setminus\mathscr{R}_{\kappa+1}

and
R\notin\mathscr{R}_{\widetilde{\kappa}}\setminus\mathscr{R}_{\widetilde{\kappa}+1}

for all
\widetilde{\kappa}\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{\kappa\}
.
By this and Minkowski’s inequality, we conclude that,
for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
,

\displaystyle\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x)f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x)\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\ell^{\vec{q}}}

\displaystyle=\|\{\gamma_{R}(x)f_{R}\mathbf{1}_{R}(x)\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\|_{\ell^{\vec{q}}}

\displaystyle=\Bigg\|\Bigg\{\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}\setminus\mathscr{R}_{\kappa+1}}(R)\gamma_{R}(x)f_{R}\mathbf{1}_{R}(x)\Bigg\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Bigg\|_{\ell^{\vec{q}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}\setminus\mathscr{R}_{\kappa+1}}(R)\gamma_{R}(x)f_{R}\mathbf{1}_{R}(x)\Big\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|_{\ell^{\vec{q}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big[\sup_{P\in\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}}|\gamma_{P}(x)|\mathbf{1}_{P}(x)\Big]\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}\setminus\mathscr{R}_{\kappa+1}}(R)f_{R}\mathbf{1}_{R}(x)\Big\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|_{\ell^{\vec{q}}}.

Taking
L^{p}
norms and using Minkowski’s inequality again
and then using Hölder’s inequality with
\frac{1}{p}=\frac{1}{s}+\frac{1}{r}
[where, since
s\in(p,\infty)
, also
r=\frac{s}{s-p}p\in(p,\infty)
], we obtain

\displaystyle\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{\vec{q}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|x\mapsto\Big[\sup_{P\in\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}}|\gamma_{P}(x)|\mathbf{1}_{P}(x)\Big]\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}\setminus\mathscr{R}_{\kappa+1}}(R)f_{R}\mathbf{1}_{R}(x)\Big\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|_{\ell^{\vec{q}}}\Big\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|x\mapsto\sup_{P\in\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}}|\gamma_{P}(x)|\mathbf{1}_{P}(x)\Big\|_{L^{s}}\Big\|\Big\{x\mapsto\mathbf{1}_{\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}\setminus\mathscr{R}_{\kappa+1}}(R)f_{R}\mathbf{1}_{R}(x)\Big\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|_{L^{r}\ell^{\vec{q}}}

\displaystyle=:\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}}I_{\kappa}\times II_{\kappa}.

To proceed, we note that every
P\in\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}
satisfies
\langle\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\kappa}}\rangle_{P}>\frac{1}{2}
by definition, and hence

P\subset\Omega_{\kappa}^{*}:=\Big\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}:\ \mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\kappa}}(x)>\frac{1}{2}\Big\}.

The lower semicontinuity of
\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\kappa}}
implies that
\Omega_{\kappa}^{*}
is open
and the strong maximal inequality (Lemma
4.5
) implies that

|\Omega_{\kappa}^{*}|\lesssim|\Omega_{\kappa}|<\infty.

(4.22)

Hence

\begin{split}I_{\kappa}&\leq\bigg\|x\mapsto\sup_{P\subset\Omega_{\kappa}^{*}}|\gamma_{P}(x)|\mathbf{1}_{P}(x)\bigg\|_{L^{s}}=\bigg\|\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\mathbf{1}_{(\Omega_{\kappa}^{*})_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\bigg\|_{L^{s}}\\
&\leq|\Omega_{\kappa}^{*}|^{\frac{1}{s}}\quad\text{by \eqref{carl >1eq}, where we assumed that the supremum is $1$}\\
&\lesssim|\Omega_{\kappa}|^{\frac{1}{s}}\quad\text{by \eqref{Omega*}}.\end{split}

Concerning
II_{\kappa}
, for
R\notin\mathscr{R}_{\kappa+1}
,
we have
\langle\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\kappa+1}^{c}}\rangle_{R}\geq\frac{1}{2}
by definition,
while
R\in\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}
implies
R\subset\Omega_{\kappa}^{*}
as before. Hence

\displaystyle II_{\kappa}

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\Big\{x\mapsto\mathbf{1}_{\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}\setminus\mathscr{R}_{\kappa+1}}(R)f_{R}\cdot 2\langle\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\kappa+1}^{c}}\rangle_{R}\mathbf{1}_{R}(x)\Big\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|_{L^{r}\ell^{\vec{q}}}

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\Big\{\langle 2\cdot f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\kappa}^{*}\setminus\Omega_{\kappa+1}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{L^{r}\ell^{\vec{q}}}\leq\Big\|\Big\{\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}(2\cdot f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\kappa}^{*}\setminus\Omega_{\kappa+1}})\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{L^{r}\ell^{\vec{q}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big\|\Big\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\kappa}^{*}\setminus\Omega_{\kappa+1}}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{L^{r}\ell^{\vec{q}}}=\Big\|x\mapsto\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\kappa}^{*}\setminus\Omega_{\kappa+1}}(x)\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x)\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\ell^{\vec{q}}}\Big\|_{L^{r}}.

For
x\notin\Omega_{\kappa+1}
, we have
\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x)\}\|_{\ell^{\vec{q}}}\leq 2^{\kappa+1}
by definition, and hence

II_{\kappa}\lesssim\Big\|x\mapsto\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\kappa}^{*}\setminus\Omega_{\kappa+1}}(x)2^{\kappa+1}\Big\|_{L^{r}}\lesssim|\Omega_{\kappa}^{*}|^{\frac{1}{r}}2^{\kappa}\lesssim|\Omega_{\kappa}|^{\frac{1}{r}}2^{\kappa},

again by (
4.22
) in the last step.

Combining the obtained estimates, we deduce that

\begin{split}\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{\vec{q}}}&\lesssim\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}}I_{\kappa}\times II_{\kappa}\lesssim\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}}|\Omega_{\kappa}|^{\frac{1}{s}}\times|\Omega_{\kappa}|^{\frac{1}{r}}2^{\kappa}=\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}}|\Omega_{\kappa}|^{\frac{1}{p}}2^{\kappa}\\
&=\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}}|\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}:\ \|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x)\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\ell^{\vec{q}}}>2^{\kappa}\}|^{\frac{1}{p}}2^{\kappa}\sim\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{p,1}\ell^{\vec{q}}},\end{split}

(4.23)

where
L^{p,1}
is the Lorentz space.

The obtained estimate deviates from that claimed in the lemma in two ways: by having the Lorentz
L^{p,1}
norm in place of
L^{p}
on the right,
and by being only established under the additional assumption that
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=\langle f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
. Both shortcomings may be lifted with the help of the interpolation result stated in Lemma
4.17
. Given
1<p<s<\infty
as in the assumptions of the lemma that we are proving, we may clearly choose
p_{0},p_{1},\theta
as in Lemma
4.17
so that in addition
1<p_{0}<p<p_{1}<s<\infty
. By the strong maximal inequality (Lemma
4.5
), we find that

\|\{\langle f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{p_{i}}\ell^{\vec{q}}}\leq\|\{\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}(f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}})\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{p_{i}}\ell^{\vec{q}}}\lesssim\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{p_{i}}\ell^{\vec{q}}},\quad i=0,1.

Applying Lemma
4.17
with
X=\ell^{\vec{q}}
,
L^{p_{i},r_{i}}=L^{p_{i},p_{i}}=L^{p_{i}}
, and
L^{p,r}=L^{p,1}
, we deduce for the bounded linear operator
\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\mapsto\{\langle f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}:\ L^{p_{i}}\ell^{\vec{q}}\to L^{p_{i}}\ell^{\vec{q}}
the estimate

\begin{split}\|\{\langle f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{p,1}\ell^{\vec{q}}}&\sim\|\{\langle f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{p_{0}}\ell^{\vec{q}},L^{p_{1}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\theta,1}}\\
&\lesssim\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{p_{0}}\ell^{\vec{q}},L^{p_{1}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\theta,1}}\sim\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{p,1}\ell^{\vec{q}}}.\end{split}

(4.24)

Recalling that (
4.23
) was proved under the assumption that
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=\langle f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
, if we now apply this bound to
\langle f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
in place of
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
, and combine this with (
4.24
), we obtain

\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\langle f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{\vec{q}}}\lesssim\|\{\langle f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{p,1}\ell^{\vec{q}}}\lesssim\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{p,1}\ell^{\vec{q}}}.

(4.25)

Since (
4.25
) is valid for any exponents as in the assumptions, it is also valid for each
p_{i}
in place of
p
:

\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\langle f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{p_{i}}\ell^{\vec{q}}}\lesssim\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{p_{i},1}\ell^{\vec{q}}},\quad i=0,1.

Applying Lemma
4.17
with
X=\ell^{\vec{q}}
,
r_{i}\in\{p_{i},1\}
, and
r=p
, we deduce for the bounded linear operator
\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\mapsto\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\langle f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}:\ L^{p_{i},1}\ell^{\vec{q}}\to L^{p_{i}}\ell^{\vec{q}}
, the estimate

\begin{split}\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\langle f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{\vec{q}}}&\sim\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\langle f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{p_{0}}\ell^{\vec{q}},L^{p_{1}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\theta,p}}\\
&\lesssim\|\{\langle f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{p_{0},1}\ell^{\vec{q}},L^{p_{1},1}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\theta,p}}\sim\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{\vec{q}}},\end{split}

which is the assertion of Lemma
4.19
.
∎

Remark 4.20
.

In the one-parameter case of Lemma
4.19
,
the bound

\mathrm{I}:=\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\bigg\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{j}}\gamma_{j}\bigg\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(\Omega)}

can be replaced by

\mathrm{II}:=\sup_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\bigg\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z},\,j\geq j_{Q}}\gamma_{j}\bigg\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(Q)};

see
[
67
, Theorem 3.7(ii)]
.
Indeed,
\mathrm{I}=\mathrm{II}
for all
s\in(0,\infty)
.
The inequality
\mathrm{II}\leq\mathrm{I}
is obvious.
Denoting by
\mathscr{D}^{*}(\Omega)
the collection of maximal dyadic cubes

P\subset\Omega
, these cubes are disjoint, and we have

\bigg\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{j}}\gamma_{j}\bigg\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(\Omega)}^{s}=\frac{1}{|\Omega|}\sum_{P\in\mathscr{D}^{*}(\Omega)}\int_{P}\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z},j\geq j_{P}}|\gamma_{j}|^{s}\leq\frac{1}{|\Omega|}\sum_{P\in\mathscr{D}^{*}(\Omega)}|P|\cdot\mathrm{II}^{s}=\mathrm{II}^{s}

and hence
\mathrm{I}\leq\mathrm{II}
.
Thus,
\mathrm{I}=\mathrm{II}
and Lemma
4.19
in the case
k=1

coincides with
[
67
, Theorem 3.7(ii)]
in the case
q\in(1,\infty)
.

Finally, by specialising to sequences
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
that are constant on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, we can push the lower bound of the exponents from one to zero.

Lemma 4.21
.

If
0<p<s<\infty
and
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
then there exists a positive constant
C
such that,
for all measurable functions
\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
and
every sequence
\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\in L^{p}\ell^{\vec{q}}

with each
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
constant on every
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,

\big\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{\vec{q}}}\lesssim\bigg[\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Big\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(\Omega)}\bigg]\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{\vec{q}}}.

Proof.

Pick some
r\in(0,\min(p,\min\vec{q}))
so that
1<p/r<s/r<\infty
and
\vec{1}<\vec{q}/r<\vec{\infty}
. An application of Lemma
4.19
with these rescaled exponents then shows that

\displaystyle\big\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{\vec{q}}}^{r}

\displaystyle=\big\|\{|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p/r}\ell^{\vec{q}/r}}

\displaystyle=\big\|\{|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\langle|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p/r}\ell^{\vec{q}/r}}\quad\text{since $f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}$
is constant on $R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})$},

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\Big\|_{\textit{\L}^{s/r}(\Omega)}\big\|\{|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p/r}\ell^{\vec{q}/r}}\quad\text{by Lemma \ref{carl >1}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Big\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(\Omega)}^{r}\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{\vec{q}}}^{r},

which is the claim of the present lemma.
∎

Let us summarise the findings of this subsection in the following:

Theorem 4.22
.

Let
0<p<s<\infty
,
\vec{p}:=p\cdot\vec{1}
, and
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
.
Let
\mathscr{X}
be as in one of the cases (
i
) through (
iii
) below.
Then, for all measurable functions
\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
and
every
\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\in\mathscr{X}

with each
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
constant on every
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,

\big\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\mathscr{X}}\lesssim\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\mathscr{X}}

(4.26)

holds, with the implicit positive constants independent of
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
and
\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
, in each of the following cases:

(i)

\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}\lesssim 1
and
\mathscr{X}=\ell^{\vec{q}}L^{p}
is of Besov-type,

(ii)

\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(R)}\lesssim 1
and
\mathscr{X}=(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}
is of arbitrary type with
\vec{q}\leq\vec{p}
,

(iii)

\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\|\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(\Omega)}\lesssim 1
and
\mathscr{X}=L^{p}\ell^{\vec{q}}
is of Triebel–Lizorkin-type.

Proof.

The inequality (
4.26
) in cases (
i
) through (
iii
) follows from
Proposition
4.13
, Lemma
4.14
,
and Lemma
4.21
, respectively.
∎

As we saw in Proposition
4.13
, the condition of case (
i
) of Theorem
4.22
is not only sufficient but also necessary for the conclusion.
Also the assumptions on
\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
in cases (
ii
) and (
iii
) of Theorem
4.22
are sharp, in a sense to be made precise in Propositions
4.23
,
4.24
, and
4.25
below. Moreover, the assumption
\vec{q}\leq\vec{p}
in (
ii
) is necessary, as we will show in Propositions
4.26
and
4.27
.

4.4.
Sharpness and necessity of the Carleson conditions

In this subsection, we establish the sharpness of the assumptions of Theorem
4.22
.
We first prove the sharpness of the assumptions on
\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
in cases
(
ii
) and (
iii
) of Theorem
4.22
,
with case (
ii
) handled in the lemma below.

Proposition 4.23
.

Let
0<q<p<s<\infty
.
Then there exists a positive constant
C
such that,
for all measurable functions
\{\gamma_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}
and
all
\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\in L^{p}\ell^{q}
,
where each
f_{j}
constant on every
R\in\mathscr{D}_{j}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,

\big\|\{\gamma_{j}f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}\leq C\bigg[\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{j}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\|\gamma_{j}\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(R)}\bigg]\big\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}},

(4.27)

where this inequality is sharp in the sense that
(
4.27
) no longer holds when
s=p
.

Proof.

Inequality (
4.27
) is a special case of Lemma
4.14
.
Now, we prove the sharpness.
For any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, let

\displaystyle\gamma_{-j}:=\begin{cases}2^{\frac{jn}{p}}\mathbf{1}_{[0,1)^{n}},&\text{if }j\geq 0,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(4.28)

Then

\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}\|\gamma_{j}\|_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}=1
.
Next, we show that

\displaystyle\sup_{\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}\neq 0}\frac{\|\{\gamma_{j}f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}}{\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}}=\infty.

(4.29)

For any
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, let

f_{-j}^{(N)}:=\begin{cases}2^{-\frac{jn}{p}}\mathbf{1}_{[0,2^{j})^{n}},&\text{if }j\in[N],\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Then
\|\{\gamma_{j}f_{j}^{(N)}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}=N^{\frac{1}{q}}
and

\displaystyle\Big\|\Big\{f_{j}^{(N)}\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}^{p}

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1)^{n}}\Bigg\{\sum_{j=1}^{N}\Big[f_{-j}^{(N)}\Big]^{q}\Bigg\}^{\frac{p}{q}}+\sum_{l=1}^{N}\int_{[0,2^{l})^{n}\setminus[0,2^{l-1})^{n}}\cdots

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{N}2^{-jn\frac{q}{p}}\Bigg)^{\frac{p}{q}}+\sum_{l=1}^{N}\Big[2^{ln}-2^{(l-1)n}\Big]\Bigg(\sum_{j=l}^{N}2^{-jn\frac{q}{p}}\Bigg)^{\frac{p}{q}}

\displaystyle\sim 1+\sum_{l=1}^{N}2^{ln}2^{-ln}\sim N.

Therefore,

\frac{\|\{\gamma_{j}f_{j}^{(N)}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}}{\|\{f_{j}^{(N)}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}}\gtrsim N^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{p}}\to\infty\quad\text{as}\quad N\to\infty.

This finishes the proof of (
4.29
).
Hence (
4.27
) no longer holds when
s=p
,
which completes the proof of Proposition
4.23
.
∎

In Theorem
4.22
(iii), the essential range of
p
,
q
, and
s

is
0<p<s<q<\infty
because

•

the case
q\leq p
is already covered by Theorem
4.22
(ii), and

•

when both
p<q
and
p<s
, we may assume that
p<s<q
, since the smaller the
s
, the better the bound.

Proposition 4.24
.

If
0<p<s<q<\infty
,
then there exists a positive constant
C
such that,
for all measurable functions
\{\gamma_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}
and
every sequence
\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\in L^{p}\ell^{q}

with each
f_{j}
constant on every
R\in\mathscr{D}_{j}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,

\big\|\{\gamma_{j}f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}\leq C\bigg[\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{j}}\gamma_{j}\Big\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(\Omega)}\bigg]\big\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}.

(4.30)

Moreover, this inequality is sharp in the sense that
(
4.30
) no longer holds if
the bound

\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{j}}\gamma_{j}\Big\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(\Omega)}

is replaced by either

\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{j}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\|\gamma_{j}\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(R)}\quad\text{or}\quad\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{j}}\gamma_{j}\Big\|_{\textit{\L}^{p}(\Omega)}.

Proof.

Inequality (
4.30
) is a special case of Lemma
4.21
.
Now, we prove the sharpness.
We first show that

\big\|\{\gamma_{j}f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}\lesssim\Big[\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{j}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\|\gamma_{j}\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(R)}\Big]\big\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}

(4.31)

fails to hold. For any
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, let

\displaystyle\gamma_{j}^{(N)}:=\begin{cases}N^{\frac{1}{s}}\mathbf{1}_{F_{j}},&\text{if }j\in[N],\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

where
\{F_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N}
are pairwise disjoint subsets
of
[0,1)^{n}
and,
for every
j\in[N]
,
we have
|F_{j}|=\frac{1}{N}
and

\{F_{j}\cap Q\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}_{j}([0,1)^{n})}

have equal measure.
Let
e:=(1,0,\ldots,0)\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and, for each
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\gamma_{j}(\cdot):=\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}\gamma_{j}^{(N)}(\cdot-Ne).

Then
\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{j}}\|\gamma_{j}\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(R)}=1
.
For each
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, let

f_{j}^{(N)}:=\begin{cases}\mathbf{1}_{[0,1)^{n}+Ne},&\text{if }j\in[N],\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Then

\begin{split}\|\{\gamma_{j}f_{j}^{(N)}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}&=\|\{\gamma_{j}^{(N)}(\cdot-Ne)f_{j}^{(N)}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}\\
&=\|\{N^{\frac{1}{s}}\mathbf{1}_{F_{j}+Ne}\}_{j\in[N]}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}=\|N^{\frac{1}{s}}\mathbf{1}_{[0,1)^{n}+Ne}\|_{L^{p}}=N^{\frac{1}{s}}\end{split}

and
\|\{f_{j}^{(N)}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}=\|\{\mathbf{1}_{[0,1)^{n}+Ne}\}_{j\in[N]}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}=N^{\frac{1}{q}}
.

Therefore,

\frac{\|\{\gamma_{j}f_{j}^{(N)}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}}{\|\{f_{j}^{(N)}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}}=N^{\frac{1}{s}-\frac{1}{q}}\to\infty\quad\text{as}\quad N\to\infty

and hence (
4.31
) fails to hold.

Next, we prove that

\big\|\{\gamma_{j}f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}\lesssim\Bigg[\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{j}}\gamma_{j}\Big\|_{\textit{\L}^{p}(\Omega)}\Bigg]\big\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}

(4.32)

fails to hold.
Let
\{\gamma_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}
be as in (
4.28
).
Thus,
\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{-j}}\gamma_{-j}=\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{N}}(j)2^{\frac{jn}{p}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{-j}}\mathbf{1}_{[0,1)^{n}}
. For
j\geq 0
, the set
\Omega_{-j}
is a union of the translates of
[0,2^{j})^{n}\supseteq[0,1)^{n}
contained in
\Omega
. Hence it follows that

\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{-j}}\gamma_{-j}=\begin{cases}2^{\frac{jn}{p}}\mathbf{1}_{[0,1)^{n}},&\text{if }j\geq 0\text{ and }[0,2^{j})^{n}\subset\Omega,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Note that a necessary condition for
[0,2^{j})^{n}\subseteq\Omega
is
2^{jn}\leq|\Omega|
. Hence

\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{j}}\gamma_{j}\leq\sup_{j:1\leq 2^{jn}\leq|\Omega|}2^{\frac{jn}{p}}\mathbf{1}_{[0,1)^{n}}\lesssim|\Omega|^{\frac{1}{p}}\mathbf{1}_{[0,1)^{n}},

and thus

\Big\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{j}}\gamma_{j}\Big\|_{\textit{\L}^{p}(\Omega)}\lesssim|\Omega|^{\frac{1}{p}}\big\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,1)^{n}}\big\|_{\textit{\L}^{p}(\Omega)}=|\Omega|^{\frac{1}{p}}|\Omega|^{-\frac{1}{p}}\big\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,1)^{n}}\big\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}=1.

Hence (
4.32
) would imply that

\|\{\gamma_{j}f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}\lesssim\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}
.
But we know from (
4.29
) that this is not the case, and hence (
4.32
) fails to hold.
This finishes the proof of Proposition
4.24
.
∎

In the one-parameter case of Theorem
4.22
(
iii
),
the bound

\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\Big\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{j}}\gamma_{j}\Big\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(\Omega)}

can be equivalently replaced by

\sup_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\|\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z},\,j\geq j_{Q}}\gamma_{j}\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(Q)}
;
see Remark
4.20
.
However, this replacement is not valid in the multi-parameter case.

Proposition 4.25
.

If
0<p<s<q<\infty
,
then there exists a positive constant
C
such that,
for all measurable functions
\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
and
every sequence
\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\in L^{p}\ell^{q}

with each
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
constant on every
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,

\big\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}\leq C\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Big\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(\Omega)}\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}.

(4.33)

Moreover, this inequality is sharp in the sense that
(
4.33
) no longer holds if
k\geq 2
and
the bound
\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\|\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(\Omega)}

is replaced by

\sup_{P\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k},\,\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\geq\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}_{P}}\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Big\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(P)}.

Proof.

Inequality (
4.33
) is a special case of Lemma
4.21
.
Next, we show the sharpness, that is,

\big\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}\lesssim\sup_{P\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k},\,\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\geq\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}_{P}}\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Big\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(P)}\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}

(4.34)

fails to hold.

By an elaboration of Carleson’s counterexample
[
23
]
given in Lemma
A.12
, we find that, for every
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
there exists a finite collection
\{R_{i}\}_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N}}
of
pairwise distinct dyadic rectangles in
[0,1)^{\vec{n}}
such that

(i)

\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N}}|R_{i}|=1
,

(ii)

|\bigcup_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N}}R_{i}|<\frac{1}{N}
, and

(iii)

\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N},\,R_{i}\subset P}|R_{i}|\leq|P|
for every dyadic rectangle
P\subset[0,1)^{\vec{n}}
,

(iv)

|\bigcup_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N}}R_{i}|^{-\frac{s}{p}}\|\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N}}\mathbf{1}_{R_{i}}\|_{\frac{p}{s}}>N
.

We note that properties (
i
) through (
iii
) consist of Carleson’s original counterexample (or more precisely, its simple extension from two to higher dimensions, as given in Lemma
A.11
). If
p=s
, property (
iv
) would be immediate from (
i
) and (
ii
). However, now that
p<s
, we see from Hölder’s inequality that property (
iv
) is more difficult to satisfy, which requires an elaboration of Carleson’s counterexample given in Lemma
A.12
, based on Proposition
A.3
.

Let
g^{(N)}:=\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N}}\mathbf{1}_{R_{i}}
.
For every
N,\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, define

\Omega^{(N,\kappa)}:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}:\ g^{(N)}=\kappa\}.

Then

\bigcup_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\Omega^{(N,\kappa)}=\bigcup_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N}}R_{i}=:\Omega^{(N)}.

For every
N,\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and
i\in\mathscr{I}_{N}
, let

E^{(N)}_{i,\kappa}\subset R_{i}\cap\Omega^{(N,\kappa)}

be sets such that
\{E_{i,\kappa}^{(N)}\}_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N},\,\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
forms a partition of
\Omega^{(N)}
and

|E^{(N)}_{i,\kappa}|=\frac{1}{\kappa}|R_{i}\cap\Omega^{(N,\kappa)}|;

such sets can be chosen, since each point of
\Omega^{(N,\kappa)}
belongs to exactly
\kappa
different
R_{i}
. (Indeed, for every non-empty intersection of the form
R_{i_{1}}\cap\cdots\cap R_{i_{\kappa}}\cap\Omega^{(N,\kappa)}
, we divide it to
\kappa
equal parts and give one of the parts to each
E_{i_{j}}^{(N,\kappa)}
; each final
E_{i}^{(N,\kappa)}
will be the union of all such parts when we go through all such intersections, where
R_{i}
is included.)
Thus

g^{(N)}=\sum_{\kappa=1}^{\infty}\kappa\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{(N,\kappa)}}=\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N}}\kappa\mathbf{1}_{E^{(N,\kappa)}_{i}}.

(4.35)

Then, let

\gamma_{R_{i}}^{(N)}:=\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\kappa^{\frac{1}{s}}\mathbf{1}_{E_{i,\kappa}^{(N)}},

and, for every
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
,

\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}^{(N)}:=\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N},\,R_{i}\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\gamma_{R_{i}}^{(N)}.

Let
e:=(1,0,\ldots,0)\in\mathbb{Z}^{\vec{n}}
and

\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\cdot):=\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}^{(N)}(\cdot-Ne).

Then, for every
P\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,

\begin{split}\Bigg\|\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k},\,\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\geq\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}_{P}}\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Bigg\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(P)}^{s}&=\fint_{P}\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N},\,\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}_{R_{i}}\geq\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}_{P}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\kappa\mathbf{1}_{E_{i,\kappa}^{(N)}}(\cdot-Ne)\\
&=\frac{1}{|P|}\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N},\,\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}_{R_{i}}\geq\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}_{P}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\kappa|E_{i,\kappa}^{(N)}\cap(P-Ne)|\\
&=\frac{1}{|P|}\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N},\,R_{i}\subset(P-Ne)}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}|R_{i}\cap\Omega^{(N,\kappa)}|\\
&=\frac{1}{|P|}\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N},\,R_{i}\subset(P-Ne)}|R_{i}|\leq\frac{1}{|P|}\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}|(P-Ne)\cap[0,1)^{\vec{n}}|\leq 1.\end{split}

For every
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
, let

f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}^{(N)}:=\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N},\,R_{i}\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\mathbf{1}_{R_{i}+Ne}.

If (
4.34
) holds, then, for every
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,

\big\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}^{(N)}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}\lesssim\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}^{(N)}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}.

However,

\begin{split}\big\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}^{(N)}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}^{p}&=\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}^{(N)}(\cdot-Ne)f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}^{(N)}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}^{p}\\
&=\Bigg\|\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\kappa^{\frac{1}{s}}\mathbf{1}_{E_{i,\kappa}^{(N)}}(\cdot-Ne)\Bigg\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}^{p}=\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\kappa^{\frac{p}{s}}|E_{i,\kappa}^{(N)}|=\|g^{(N)}\|_{L^{\frac{p}{s}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}^{\frac{p}{s}}\end{split}

by (
4.35
) in the last step.
From Hölder’s inequality, we infer that

\begin{split}\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}^{(N)}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}^{p}=\Bigg\|\Bigg(\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N}}\mathbf{1}_{R_{i}+Ne}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{q}}\Bigg\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}^{p}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{(N)}}[g^{(N)}]^{\frac{p}{q}}&\leq|\Omega^{(N)}|^{\frac{1}{(q/s)^{\prime}}}\|[g^{(N)}]^{\frac{p}{q}}\|_{L^{\frac{q}{s}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\\
&=|\Omega^{(N)}|^{1-\frac{s}{q}}\|g^{(N)}\|_{L^{\frac{p}{s}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}^{\frac{p}{q}}.\end{split}

Therefore,

\frac{\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}^{(N)}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}^{p}}{\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}^{(N)}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}^{p}}\geq|\Omega^{(N)}|^{\frac{s}{q}-1}\|g^{(N)}\|_{L^{\frac{p}{s}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}^{\frac{p}{s}-\frac{p}{q}}=\Big(|\Omega^{(N)}|^{-\frac{s}{p}}\|g^{(N)}\|_{L^{\frac{p}{s}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big)^{\frac{p}{s}-\frac{p}{q}}>N^{\frac{p}{s}-\frac{p}{q}}

using, in the last step, (
iv
) and the fact that the exponent is positive, since
s<q
.
As
N\to\infty
, we see that (
4.34
) fails to hold.
This finishes the proof of Proposition
4.25
.
∎

The following result shows that if the assumption
\vec{q}\leq\vec{p}
in Theorem
4.22
(
ii
) is removed,
then even under a stronger assumption on
\gamma_{j}
,
the desired inequality

\big\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}\lesssim\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}

fails to hold.

Proposition 4.26
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty)
,
q_{1}\in(0,\infty)
,
q_{2}\in(p,\infty)
, and
s\in(p,q_{2})
.
Then there exist measurable functions

\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}
satisfying

\displaystyle\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Bigg\|\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Bigg\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(\Omega)}\leq 1

(4.36)

and
\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\in\ell^{q_{1}}L_{x}^{p}\ell^{q_{2}}L_{y}^{p}

with each
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
constant on every
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
such that

\big\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\big\|_{\ell^{q_{1}}L_{x}^{p}\ell^{q_{2}}L_{y}^{p}}=\infty\quad\text{and}\quad\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\big\|_{\ell^{q_{1}}L_{x}^{p}\ell^{q_{2}}L_{y}^{p}}<\infty.

(4.37)

Proof.

For any
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and
t\in(0,\infty)
,
let
S_{t}(j):=\sum_{i=1}^{j}i^{-t}
.
Assume that
t\in(1,\frac{pq_{2}^{-1}-1}{ps^{-1}-1})

and
d\in(pq_{2}^{-1},tps^{-1}-t+1)
.
For any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
, let

\displaystyle f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x,y):=\begin{cases}j_{2}^{-\frac{d}{p}}\mathbf{1}_{(j_{2},j_{2}+1]}(y)\mathbf{1}_{(0,1]}(x),&\text{if }j_{2}>0\text{ and }j_{1}=0,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x,y):=\begin{cases}[j_{2}^{t}\zeta(t)]^{s^{-1}}\mathbf{1}_{(j_{2},j_{2}+1]}(y)\mathbf{1}_{(S_{t}(j_{2}-1),S_{t}(j_{2})]}[\zeta(t)x],&\text{if }j_{2}>0\text{ and }j_{1}=0,\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

where
\zeta(t):=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}i^{-t}
.
For
j_{1}=0
, any
j_{2}>0
, and
Q:=Q_{1}\times Q_{2}\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R})
,
we obviously have

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(Q)}\leq\bigg[\fint_{Q_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}j_{2}^{t}\zeta(t)\mathbf{1}_{(j_{2},j_{2}+1]}(y)\mathbf{1}_{(S_{t}(j_{2}-1),S_{t}(j_{2})]}[\zeta(t)x]\,dx\,dy\bigg]^{\frac{1}{s}}=1.

It is also obvious that, for any
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}_{1}\neq\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}_{2}
,

\operatorname{supp}\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}_{1}}\cap\operatorname{supp}\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}_{2}}=\varnothing
.
Then

\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\bigg\|\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\bigg\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(\Omega)}\leq\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\sup_{Q\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R})}\|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(Q)}\leq 1,

which shows that the assumption (
4.36
) holds.
Now, we verify that (
4.37
) holds.
Indeed,

\displaystyle\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\big\|_{\ell^{q_{1}}L_{x}^{p}\ell^{q_{2}}L_{y}^{p}}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Bigg\{\sum_{j_{2}=1}^{\infty}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}}j_{2}^{-d}\mathbf{1}_{(j_{2},j_{2}+1]}(y)\mathbf{1}_{(0,1]}(x)\,dy\bigg]^{\frac{q_{2}}{p}}\Bigg\}^{\frac{p}{q_{2}}}\,dx\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\sum_{j_{2}=1}^{\infty}j_{2}^{-\frac{q_{2}d}{p}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{q_{2}}}\bigg\{\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big[\mathbf{1}_{(0,1]}(x)\Big]^{p}\,dx\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{p}}<\infty

and

\displaystyle\big\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\big\|_{\ell^{q_{1}}L_{x}^{p}\ell^{q_{2}}L_{y}^{p}}

\displaystyle\quad=\bigg[\int_{0}^{1}\bigg\{\sum_{j_{2}=1}^{\infty}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}}j_{2}^{-d}[j_{2}^{t}\zeta(t)]^{ps^{-1}}\mathbf{1}_{(j_{2},j_{2}+1]}(y)\mathbf{1}_{(S_{t}(j_{2}-1),S_{t}(j_{2})]}[\zeta(t)x]\,dy\bigg]^{\frac{q_{2}}{p}}\bigg\}^{\frac{p}{q_{2}}}\,dx\bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\quad\sim\bigg[\sum_{j_{2}=1}^{\infty}\int_{S_{t}(j_{2}-1)}^{S_{t}(j_{2})}j_{2}^{-d+tps^{-1}}\,dx\bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}}=\infty.

This finishes the proof of Proposition
4.26
.
∎

Indeed, if
(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}
looks like

\cdots L^{p}\cdots\ell^{q}\cdots L^{p}\cdots
with
q>p
,
then the estimate (
4.26
) also fails.
This further implies that condition
\vec{q}\leq\vec{p}
in (
6.4
)
is necessary in some sense.
More precisely, we have the following proposition.

Proposition 4.27
.

Let
0<p<s<\infty
,
\vec{p}:=p\cdot\vec{1}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
and
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
.
Assume that there exist

0<\gamma_{1}<\gamma_{2}<\gamma_{3}\leq 2k

such that

\pi(\gamma_{1}),\pi(\gamma_{3})\in[k]
,

\pi(\gamma_{2})\in[2k]\setminus[k]
, and

q_{\pi(\gamma_{2})-k}>s.

Then there exist measurable functions

\{\{\gamma^{(N)}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\}_{N\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
satisfying (
4.36
)
and

\{\{f^{(N)}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\}_{N\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\subset(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}

with each
f^{(N)}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
constant on every
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
such that

\left\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}^{(N)}f^{(N)}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\right\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}\left\|\{f^{(N)}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\right\|^{-1}_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}\to\infty\quad\text{as}\quad N\to\infty.

Proof.

Let
a:=\pi(\gamma_{1})
,

b:=\pi(\gamma_{2})-k
,

c:=\pi(\gamma_{3})
,
and
a,b,c\in[k]
with
q_{b}>s
.
Let
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. For
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}=(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
, define

f^{(N)}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x):=\begin{cases}\mathbf{1}_{(j_{b},j_{b}+1]\times(0,1]^{n_{c}-1}}(x_{c})\,\mathbf{1}_{(0,1]^{n-n_{c}}}(x\ominus x_{c}),&\text{if }0<j_{b}\leq N\text{ and }\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\ominus j_{b}=\vec{0},\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

We partition the interval
(0,1)
into
2^{N}
disjoint intervals

I_{l}:=\Big(l2^{-N},(l+1)2^{-N}\Big),\qquad\text{for }l\in\{0,\cdots,2^{N}-1\}.

Each
I_{l}
is further subdivided into
N
subintervals

I_{l,i}:=\Bigg(l2^{-N}+(i-1)\frac{2^{-N}}{N},\;l2^{-N}+i\frac{2^{-N}}{N}\Bigg),\qquad\text{for }i\in[N].

Let

A_{i}:=\bigcup_{l=0}^{2^{N}-1}I_{l,i},\qquad\text{for }i\in[N]

and then
|A_{i}|=\frac{1}{N}
.
Moreover, let

\gamma^{(N)}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x):=\begin{cases}N\,\mathbf{1}_{(j_{b},j_{b}+1]\times(0,1]^{n_{c}-1}}(x_{c})\,\mathbf{1}_{A_{j_{b}}\times(0,1]^{n_{a}-1}}(x_{a})\,\mathbf{1}_{(0,1]^{n-n_{a}-n_{c}}}(x\ominus x_{a}\ominus x_{c}),\\
\phantom{0,}\qquad\text{if }0<j_{b}\leq N\text{ and }\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\ominus j_{b}=\vec{0},\\
0,\qquad\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

It is straightforward to verify that

\{\gamma^{(N)}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
satisfies (
4.36
).
By a direct computation, we conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|\{f^{(N)}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\right\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}=N^{\frac{1}{q_{b}}}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}^{(N)}f^{(N)}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\right\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}=N^{\frac{1}{s}}.

This, combined with
q_{b}>s
, finishes the proof of Proposition
4.27
.
∎

4.5.
Carleson conditions related to matrix weights

As in the one-parameter theory developed by Frazier and Roudenko
[
67
, Section 3]
, the key example in the context of matrix weights to which we wish to apply the general Theorem
4.22
involves multipliers of the form

\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x):=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\gamma_{R}(x)\mathbf{1}_{R}(x),\quad\gamma_{R}(x):=|V(x)[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}^{-1}|,

(4.38)

where
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
. These are easily seen to be compatible with cases (
i
) and (
ii
) of Theorem
4.22
:

Lemma 4.28
.

For
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, there exists
s\in(p,\infty)
such that

\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\bigg[\fint_{R}|V(x)[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}^{-1}|^{s}dx\bigg]^{1/s}\lesssim 1,

uniformly over bounded subsets of
\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.

Proof.

By Corollary
2.27
, we can pick an
s\in(p,\infty)
, uniformly over bounded subsets of
\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, such that
V\in\mathrm{RHI}_{p,s}(\mathrm{Rect}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}))
, and thus

\displaystyle\bigg[\fint_{R}|V(x)[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}^{-1}|^{s}dx\bigg]^{\frac{1}{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg[\fint_{R}|V(x)[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}^{-1}|^{p}dx\bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\sim|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}^{-1}|=1.

This finishes the proof of Lemma
4.28
.
∎

Let us then discuss case (
iii
) of Theorem
4.22
. In order to facilitate the discussion, we give this condition a name in the special case of functions
of the form (
4.38
):

Definition 4.29
.

For
0<p<s<\infty
, we say that
V\in\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{Carl}}_{p,s}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
if
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
and

\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\fint_{\Omega}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}),\,x\in R\subset\Omega}|V(x)[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}^{-1}|^{s}\,dx\lesssim 1.

(4.39)

We say that
V\in\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{Carl}}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
if
V\in\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{Carl}}_{p,s}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
for some
s\in(p,\infty)
.

Lemma 4.30
.

In the one-parameter case,
\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{Carl}}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})=\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for all
p\in(0,\infty)
. Moreover, bounded subsets of
\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
are bounded in
\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{Carl}}_{p,s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for some
s\in(p,\infty)
uniformly over such subsets.

Proof.

This is essentially
[
73
, Lemma 3.3]
for
p\in(1,\infty)
and
[
67
, Lemma 3.3]
for
p\in(0,1]
; an inspection of both arguments shows that they give (
4.39
) with the same
s\in(p,\infty)
as that in the reverse Hölder class
\mathrm{RHI}_{p,s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
containing a given
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
; by Lemma
2.13
, this is uniform over bounded subsets of
\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

More precisely, both
[
73
, Lemma 3.3]
and
[
67
, Lemma 3.3]
deal with (
4.39
) for
\Omega=P\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
; however, the estimate as stated is a quick consequence: Denoting by
\mathscr{D}^{*}(\Omega)
the collection of maximal dyadic cubes
P\subset\Omega
, these cubes are disjoint, and we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{R\in\D(\R^n)}{x\in R\subset\Omega}}|V(x)[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}^{-1}|^{s}\,dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{P\in\mathscr{D}^{*}(\Omega)}\int_{P}\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{R\in\D(\R^n)}{x\in R\subset P}}|V(x)[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}^{-1}|^{s}\,dx

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{P\in\mathscr{D}^{*}(\Omega)}|P|=|\Omega|,

where the inequality for each
P
is
[
73
, Lemma 3.3]
or
[
67
, Lemma 3.3]
, depending on the value of
p
.
This finishes the proof of Lemma
4.30
.
∎

The original motivation of
[
73
, Lemma 3.3]
was to prove the boundedness of the matrix-weighted maximal operator

\mathcal{M}_{V}f(x):=\sup_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),\,x\in Q}\fint_{Q}|V(x)V^{-1}(y)f(y)|dy

(4.40)

on
L^{u}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for all
u
in some neighbourhood of
p\in(1,\infty)
, provided that
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
; this is
[
73
, Theorem 3.2]
.
(The definition of
\mathcal{M}_{V}f(x)
as stated in (
4.40
) is from
[
29
, p. 1996]
, where case
p=2
of the just mentioned result was proved, but actually an equivalent definition and case
u=p\in(1,\infty)
of the theorem already appeared in
[
122
, p. 121]
.)

The following lemma shows that the reasoning of
[
73
]
works in both directions, and also in the multi-parameter case. Similar statements about the equivalence of maximal and Carleson embedding-type inequalities are known in different situations, see e.g.
[
82
]
.

Lemma 4.31
.

Let
0<v<p\leq 1
or
v=1<p<\infty
, and let
p<s<\infty
. For
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, consider the maximal operator

\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n},V}^{(v)}f(x):=\bigg[\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}),\,x\in R}\fint_{R}|V(x)V^{-1}(y)f(y)|^{v}dy\bigg]^{\frac{1}{v}}.

Then the following conditions are equivalent:

(i)

V\in\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{Carl}}_{p,s}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
;

(ii)

the operator
\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n},V}^{(v)}
is bounded on
L^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
;

(iii)

for some
r\in(0,p)
, whose choice is uniform over bounded subsets of
\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, the operator
\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n},V}^{(v)}
is bounded on
L^{u}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
for all
u\in(r,s]
.

Proof.

(
iii
)
\Rightarrow
(
ii
) is obvious.

(
ii
)
\Rightarrow
(
i
):
Let
\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
and
R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
be such that
R\subset\Omega
.
By Lemma
2.8
, we conclude that

\begin{split}|V(x)[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}^{-1}|&=|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}^{-1}V(x)|\lesssim|[V^{-1}]_{\textit{\L}^{v}(R)}V(x)|\\
&\sim\bigg[\fint_{R}|V^{-1}(y)V(x)|^{v}\,dy\bigg]^{\frac{1}{v}}=\bigg[\fint_{R}|V(x)V^{-1}(y)|^{v}\,dy\bigg]^{\frac{1}{v}}\\
&\sim\sum_{i=1}^{m}\bigg[\fint_{R}|V(x)V^{-1}(y)e_{i}|^{v}\,dy\bigg]^{\frac{1}{v}},\end{split}

(4.41)

where
(e_{i})_{i=1}^{m}
is a fixed orthonormal basis of
\mathbb{F}^{m}
.
Since
R\subset\Omega
, we can insert
\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}(y)
inside the integrand on the right-hand side of (
4.41
).
Hence

\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}),\,x\in R\subset\Omega}|V(x)[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}^{-1}|\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n},V}^{(v)}(\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}e_{i})(x).

An application of assumption (
ii
) to
f=\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}e_{i}\in L^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{F}^{m})
for each
i\in[m]
shows that

\bigg\{\int_{\Omega}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}),\,x\in R\subset\Omega}|V(x)[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}^{-1}|^{s}dx\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{s}}\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{m}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}|\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}(y)e_{i}|^{s}dy\bigg]^{\frac{1}{s}}\sim|\Omega|^{\frac{1}{s}}.

Valid for every
\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, this is the claimed property
V\in\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{Carl}}_{p,s}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.

(
i
)
\Rightarrow
(
iii
):
For each
R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, we have

\begin{split}&\bigg[\fint_{R}|V(x)V^{-1}(y)f(y)|^{v}\,dy\bigg]^{\frac{1}{v}}\\
&\quad\leq|V(x)[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}^{-1}|\bigg\{\fint_{R}|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}V^{-1}(y)f(y)|^{v}\,dy\bigg\}^{\frac{1}{v}}=:I_{R}(x)II_{R}.\end{split}

(4.42)

Here, from Hölder’s inequality, it follows that

II_{R}\leq\|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}V^{-1}\|_{\textit{\L}^{q}(R)}\|f\|_{\textit{\L}^{r}(R)},\qquad\frac{1}{v}=\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{r}.

If
p\in(0,1]
, we take
q=p^{\prime}=\infty
, hence
r=v\in(0,p)
, and the definition of
\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
implies that

\|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}V^{-1}\|_{\textit{\L}^{\infty}(R)}\lesssim 1.

If
p\in(1,\infty)
, then
V^{-1}\in\mathscr{A}_{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, and we choose
q\in(p^{\prime},\infty)
so that
V^{-1}\in\mathrm{RHI}_{p^{\prime},q}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, which is possible by Corollary
2.27
. Then

\|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}V^{-1}\|_{\textit{\L}^{q}(R)}=\|V^{-1}[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}\|_{\textit{\L}^{q}(R)}\sim\|V^{-1}[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}\|_{\textit{\L}^{p^{\prime}}(R)}\lesssim 1.

Thus, when
p\in(1,\infty)
, we have
v=1
by assumption and
q\in(p^{\prime},\infty)
by the choice just made; hence

\frac{1}{r}=1-\frac{1}{q}>1-\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{p}

and thus
r\in(0,p)
. Therefore, in both cases
p\in(0,1]
and
p\in(1,\infty)
, we obtain

II_{R}\lesssim\|f\|_{\textit{\L}^{r}(R)}\leq\inf_{z\in R}\Big[\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}(|f|^{r})(z)\Big]^{\frac{1}{r}}

for some
r\in(0,p)
. If
f\in L^{u}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
for some
u>r
, the boundedness of
\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}
on
L^{u/r}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
shows that
\|f\|_{\textit{\L}^{r}(R)}<\infty
for every
R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.

For
\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}
, let

\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}:=\{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}):\ \|f\|_{\textit{\L}^{r}(R)}>2^{\kappa}\}.

If
R
is not in any
\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}
, then
II_{R}\lesssim\|f\|_{\textit{\L}^{r}(R)}=0
, and (
4.42
) shows that such rectangles
R
do not contribute to the supremum defining the maximal function
\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n},V}^{(v)}f
. If
R
is in every
\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}
, then
\|f\|_{\textit{\L}^{r}(R)}=\infty
, and this we have already excluded. Thus, all relevant
R
belong to some but not all
\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}
, and hence to some
\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}\setminus\mathscr{R}_{\kappa+1}
. Therefore

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n},V}^{(v)}f(x)

\displaystyle=\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\bigg[\fint_{R}|V(x)V^{-1}(y)f(y)|^{v}\,dy\bigg]^{\frac{1}{v}}\mathbf{1}_{R}(x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}I_{R}(x)\|f\|_{\textit{\L}^{r}(R)}\mathbf{1}_{R}(x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{R}_{\kappa}\setminus\mathscr{R}_{\kappa+1}}I_{R}(x)2^{\kappa}\mathbf{1}_{R}(x)\leq\sup_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{\kappa}\sup_{R\subset\Omega^{\kappa},\,x\in R}I_{R}(x),

where

\displaystyle\Omega^{\kappa}:=\Big\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}:\ [\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}(|f|^{r})(x)]^{\frac{1}{r}}>2^{\kappa}\Big\}.

Recalling the definition of
I_{R}(x)
, for
V\in\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{Carl}}_{p,s}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
and
u\in(r,s]
, it then follows that

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n},V}^{(v)}f\big\|_{L^{u}}^{u}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{\kappa u}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\sup_{R\subset\Omega^{\kappa},\,x\in R}|V(x)[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}^{-1}|^{u}dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{\kappa u}|\Omega^{\kappa}|\fint_{\Omega_{\kappa}}\sup_{R\subset\Omega^{\kappa},\,x\in R}|V(x)[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}^{-1}|^{u}dx

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{\kappa u}|\Omega^{\kappa}|\bigg[\fint_{\Omega_{\kappa}}\sup_{R\subset\Omega^{\kappa},\,x\in R}|V(x)[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}^{-1}|^{s}\,dx\bigg]^{\frac{u}{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\kappa\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{\kappa u}|\Omega^{\kappa}|\sim\Big\|(\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}|f|^{r})^{\frac{1}{r}}\Big\|_{L^{u}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}^{u}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{u}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}^{u}.

This finishes the proof of Lemma
4.31
.
∎

The following corollary is a restatement of
[
73
, Theorem 3.2]
when
p\in(1,\infty)
, but may be new for
p\in(0,1]
.

Corollary 4.32
.

Let
0<v<p\leq 1
or
v=1<p<\infty
. If
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is a one-parameter weight, then the one-parameter maximal operator
\mathcal{M}_{n,V}^{(v)}
is bounded on
L^{u}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for all
u
in some neighbourhood of
p
, uniformly over bounded subsets of
\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.30
, we conclude that

\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})=\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{Carl}}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
in the one-parameter situation.
From Lemma
4.31
, it follows that
the latter property is equivalent to the claimed maximal inequality.

Moreover, by Lemma
4.30
, bounded subsets of
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
are bounded in
\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{Carl}}_{p,s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for some
s\in(p,\infty)
uniformly over such subsets. By Lemma
4.31
, this gives the boundedness of
\mathcal{M}_{n,V}^{(v)}
for all such
V
on
L^{u}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, for all
u\in(r,s]
, where
r\in(0,p)
. An inspection of the proof shows that one can take
r=v
for
p\in(0,1]
and
r=q^{\prime}
for
p\in(1,\infty)
, provided that
V^{-1}\in\mathscr{A}_{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
belongs to
\mathrm{RHI}_{p^{\prime},q}
. Since
V\mapsto V^{-1}
maps bounded subsets of
\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
into bounded subsets of
\mathscr{A}_{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, and such subsets are bounded in
\mathrm{RHI}_{p^{\prime},q}
for some common
q\in(p^{\prime},\infty)
by Lemma
2.13
, it follows that one can always choose a common
r\in(0,p)
for all
V
in bounded subsets of
\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, as claimed.
This finishes the proof of Corollary
4.32
.
∎

To give an informal summary of our observations about the
\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{Carl}}_{p}
condition so far, we have seen that:

•

in general,
V\in\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{Carl}}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
if and only if a
V
-weighted maximal operator satisfies certain inequalities (Lemma
4.31
);

•

in the one-parameter case, both
V\in\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{Carl}}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and the said maximal inequalities hold unconditionally (Lemma
4.30
and Corollary
4.32
).

What remains, then, to complete our picture of the
\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{Carl}}_{p}
condition, is to understand the boundedness of the matrix-weighted maximal operator in the general multi-parameter setting. In a recent work of
[
151
]
, this has been accomplished for
p\in(1,\infty)
, but his method does not seem to extend to
p\in(0,1]
. For this reason, we next develop a new framework for vector-valued maximal operators, which may have independent interest.

4.6.
Reducing operator-valued maximal functions

In this subsection, we are going to establish the boundedness of
the matrix-weighted strong maximal operator.
To this end, we introduce the reducing operator-valued maximal functions.

4.6.1.
The one-parameter case

Given
F\in L^{v}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{F}^{m\times\ell})
, we consider the following function of variables
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
e\in\mathbb{F}^{m}
:

r(x,e):=(\mathcal{M}^{(v)}|F(\cdot)^{*}e|)(x):=\sup_{Q\in\mathrm{Cubes}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),x\in Q}\bigg[\fint_{Q}|F(y)^{*}e|^{v}\,dy\bigg]^{\frac{1}{v}}.

Note that
F(y)^{*}\in\mathbb{F}^{\ell\times m}
, so that
F(y)^{*}e\in\mathbb{F}^{\ell}
is well defined.

For each fixed
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, the mapping
e\mapsto r(x,e)
defines a
\min\{1,v\}
-seminorm on
\mathbb{F}^{m}
.
Noting that the same supremum is achieved over a fixed countable family of cubes,
say all those with rational corners, it follows that

x\mapsto r(x,e)
is measurable for each
e\in\mathbb{F}^{m}
.
Hence Theorem
3.1
guarantees a measurable selection of
reducing operators inducing equivalent Euclidean seminorms;
we denote one such choice by
\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}F(x)
. Hence

|[\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}F(x)]e|\sim\mathcal{M}^{(v)}(|F(\cdot)^{*}e|).

Using the definition of the induced matrix norm
|A|:=\sup_{|e|\leq 1}|Ae|
, this easily extends to any matrix
A\in\mathbb{F}^{m\times m}
in place of the vector
e\in\mathbb{F}^{m}
:

|[\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}F(x)]A|\sim\mathcal{M}^{(v)}(|F(\cdot)^{*}A|).

(4.43)

Our main application deals with vector-valued functions
f\in L^{v}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{F}^{m})
. With the usual identification of
\mathbb{F}^{m}=\mathbb{F}^{m\times 1}
as the space of column vectors, the expressions
f(x)^{*}e\in\mathbb{F}
, hence
\mathcal{M}^{(v)}(f(\cdot)^{*}e)
, and thus
