\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}f
are well defined.

Nevertheless, even to make estimates with such vector-valued functions only, we will also need to deal with matrix-valued functions as intermediate steps. Extending the definition of the maximal operator
\mathcal{M}_{V}^{(v)}
from (
4.40
) to such functions is immediate:

\mathcal{M}_{V}^{(v)}F(x):=\sup_{Q\in\mathrm{Cubes}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),x\in Q}\bigg[\fint_{Q}|V(x)V(y)^{-1}F(y)|^{v}\,dy\bigg]^{\frac{1}{v}}.

Lemma 4.33
.

For a matrix weight
V
and
F\in L^{v}_{\mathrm{loc}}(V;\mathbb{F}^{m\times\ell})
,

\mathcal{M}_{V}^{(v)}F(x)\sim|V(x)\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}(V(\cdot)^{-1}F(\cdot))(x)|.

Proof.

Recall that
|AB|=|B^{*}A^{*}|
, and that both matrix weights
V(x)
and reducing operators are self-adjoint by definition.
Applying these and (
4.43
) with
A=V(x)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{V}^{(v)}F(x)

\displaystyle=\sup_{Q\ni x}\bigg[\fint_{Q}\Big|[V(y)^{-1}F(y)]^{*}V(x)\Big|^{v}\,dy\bigg]^{\frac{1}{v}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}^{(v)}\Big(|[V(\cdot)^{-1}F(\cdot)]^{*}V(x)|\Big)(x)

\displaystyle\sim|\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}(V(\cdot)^{-1}F(\cdot))(x)V(x)|=|V(x)\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}(V(\cdot)^{-1}F(\cdot))(x)|.

This finishes the proof of Lemma
4.33
.
∎

Proposition 4.34
.

Let
0<v<p\leq 1
or
v=1<p<\infty
. If
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, then for all
u
in some neighbourhood of
p
, and all
F\in L^{u}(V;\mathbb{F}^{m\times\ell})
,

\|\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}F\|_{L^{u}(V;\mathbb{F}^{m\times m})}\lesssim\|F\|_{L^{u}(V;\mathbb{F}^{m\times\ell})}

with the implicit positive constant independent of
F
.

Proof.

By Corollary
4.32
,
\mathcal{M}_{V}^{(v)}
is bounded on
L^{u}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{F}^{m})
under the assumptions of the present proposition.
Each
F\in L^{u}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{F}^{m\times\ell})
can be written as
F=[f_{1},\ldots,f_{\ell}]
with each
f_{i}\in L^{u}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{F}^{m})
, and there is the identity

V(x)V(y)^{-1}F(y)=\Big[V(x)V(y)^{-1}f_{1}(y),\ldots,V(x)V(y)^{-1}f_{\ell}(y)\Big].

Thus,
\mathcal{M}_{V}^{(v)}F(x)\sim\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\mathcal{M}_{V}^{(v)}f_{i}(x)
and from this, the boundedness of
\mathcal{M}_{V}^{(v)}
on
L^{u}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{F}^{m\times\ell})
readily follows.

By Lemma
4.33
, the claimed inequality is equivalent to the said boundedness, and hence also follows.
This finishes the proof of Proposition
4.34
.
∎

4.6.2.
The multi-parameter case

For functions on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
, definitions completely analogous to the one-parameter case above can be made. The following two versions are particularly relevant. On the one hand, there is the strong maximal operator

(\mathcal{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n}}|F(\cdot)^{*}e|)(x):=\sup_{R\in\mathrm{Rect}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\mathbf{1}_{R}(x)\bigg[\fint_{R}|F(y)^{*}e|^{v}\,dy\bigg]^{\frac{1}{v}}.

On the other hand, for each
i\in[k]
, we can consider the one-parameter maximal operator in the
i
-th variable,

(\mathcal{M}^{(v)}_{i}|F(\cdot)^{*}e|)(x):=\sup_{Q\in\mathrm{Cubes}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{i}})}\mathbf{1}_{Q}(x_{i})\bigg[\fint_{Q}|F(x^{\prime}\oplus y_{i})^{*}e|^{v}\,dy_{i}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{v}},

where
x=x^{\prime}\oplus x_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}-n_{i}}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{n_{i}}
.

In the scalar-valued case, there is a well-known trick, going back to
[
95
]
,
to dominate
\mathcal{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n}}
by the composition of
\mathcal{M}^{(v)}_{i}
for all
i\in[k]
. This also leads to a quick proof of the weighted
L^{p}
boundedness of

\mathcal{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n}}
for product Muckenhoupt weights, as noted in
[
62
, (3.4)]
. In the vector-valued, matrix-weighted situation,
a simple adaptation of this idea runs into trouble, since the maximal operator of a vector-valued function is a scalar-valued one, preventing an efficient iteration. For
v=1
, this obstacle was overcome in
[
151
]
with the help of the set-valued
maximal operator introduced in
[
9
]
. However, the framework of
[
9
]
seems to rely somewhat heavily on convexity, and it seems unclear how to define a meaningful set-valued counterpart of
\mathcal{M}^{(v)}
for
v\in(0,1)
. Our substitute is the reducing operator-valued maximal operator, which remains compatible with this quasi-Banach regime.

With considerations as in the one-parameter case, Theorem
3.1
guarantees a measurable selection of reducing operators inducing Euclidean seminorms equivalent to those defined by setting

e\mapsto(\mathcal{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n}}|F(\cdot)^{*}e|)(x)\ \ \text{and}\ \ e\mapsto(\mathcal{M}^{(v)}_{i}|F(\cdot)^{*}e|)(x);

we denote some such choices by
\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n}}F(x)
and
\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{i}F(x)
. Hence

|(\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n}}F(x))e|\sim\mathcal{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n}}|F(\cdot)^{*}e|,\qquad|(\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{i}F(x))e|\sim\mathcal{M}^{(v)}_{i}|F(\cdot)^{*}e|.

As before, these easily extend to any matrix
A
in place of the vector
e
:

|(\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n}}F(x))A|\sim\mathcal{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n}}|F(\cdot)^{*}A|,\qquad|(\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{i}F(x))A|\sim\mathcal{M}^{(v)}_{i}|F(\cdot)^{*}A|.

(4.44)

Let us now extend the classical domination of
\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}^{(v)}
by
\mathcal{M}_{1}^{(v)}\circ\cdots\circ\mathcal{M}_{k}^{(v)}
to the reducing operator-valued maximal operators.

Lemma 4.35
.

Let
v\in(0,1]
. For all functions
F\in L^{v}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{F}^{m\times\ell})
, constant matrices
A\in\mathbb{F}^{m\times\ell^{\prime}}
, and points
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

|(\mathfrak{M}_{\vec{n}}^{(v)}F)(x)A|\lesssim|((\mathfrak{M}_{1}^{(v)}\circ\cdots\circ\mathfrak{M}_{k}^{(v)})F)(x)A|,

with the implicit positive constant independent of
F
,
A,
and
x
.

Proof.

By the first equivalence in (
4.44
) and the classical domination of the scalar-valued function
|F(\cdot)^{*}A|
, we have

|(\mathfrak{M}_{\vec{n}}^{(v)}F)(\cdot)A|\sim\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}^{(v)}(F(\cdot)^{*}A)\leq\mathcal{M}_{1}^{(v)}\circ\cdots\circ\mathcal{M}_{k}^{(v)}(F(\cdot)^{*}A).

(4.45)

Let
F_{k+1}:=F
. Then
F_{k}:=\mathfrak{M}_{k}^{(v)}F
is another matrix-valued function, and thus
F_{k-1}:=\mathfrak{M}_{k-1}^{(v)}F_{k}
is well defined. In this way, we may recursively define
F_{i}:=\mathfrak{M}_{i}^{(v)}F_{i+1}
for all
i\in[k]
. Applying the second equivalence in (
4.44
) with
F_{i+1}
in place of
F
, we find the pointwise equivalence

\mathcal{M}_{i}^{(v)}(F_{i+1}(\cdot)^{*}A)\sim|(\mathfrak{M}_{i}^{(v)}F_{i+1}(\cdot))A|=|F_{i}(\cdot)A|=|F_{i}(\cdot)^{*}A|,

(4.46)

noting in the last step that
F_{i}(\cdot):=\mathfrak{M}_{i}^{(v)}F_{i+1}(\cdot)
is self-adjoint-valued by the definition of reducing operators. Applying
\mathcal{M}_{1}^{(v)}\circ\cdots\circ\mathcal{M}_{i-1}^{(v)}
(interpreted as
|\ |
if
i=1
) to both sides of (
4.46
), it follows that

(\mathcal{M}_{1}^{(v)}\circ\cdots\circ\mathcal{M}_{i}^{(v)})(F_{i+1}(\cdot)^{*}A)\sim(\mathcal{M}_{1}^{(v)}\circ\cdots\circ\mathcal{M}_{i-1}^{(v)})(F_{i}(\cdot)^{*}A).

Concatenating these equivalences for all
i\in[k]
and recalling that
F_{k+1}:=F
, we obtain

\displaystyle(\mathcal{M}_{1}^{(v)}\circ\cdots\circ\mathcal{M}_{k}^{(v)})(F(\cdot)^{*}A)

\displaystyle\sim|F_{1}(\cdot)^{*}A|=|F_{1}(\cdot)A|

\displaystyle=|((\mathfrak{M}_{1}^{(v)}\circ\cdots\circ\mathfrak{M}_{k}^{(v)})F(\cdot))A|.

In combination with (
4.45
),
this finishes the proof of Lemma
4.35
.
∎

We are now ready to prove the boundedness of the matrix-weighted strong maximal operator
\mathcal{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n},V}
. For
p\in(1,\infty)
and
k=2
(the bi-parameter case), the following result was previously obtained in
[
151
, Theorem 3.12]
, and indeed this was a key inspiration for the present generalisation. For
p\in(1,\infty)
and arbitrary
k
, the result could also be obtained by a routine extension of the
considerations in
[
151
]
, but the extension to
p\in(0,1]
seems to require the new framework that we have introduced in this section. The proof of
[
151
, Theorem 3.12]
uses the set-valued maximal function of
[
9
]
and its natural product-space extension; our argument is modelled after this,
but using our reducing operator-valued maximal function instead.

Theorem 4.36
.

Let
0<v<p\leq 1
or
v=1<p<\infty
, and
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.
Then
\mathcal{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n},V}
is bounded on
L^{u}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
for all
u
in a neighbourhood of
p
, uniformly over bounded subsets of
\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.

Proof.

The proof of Lemma
4.33
extends to the multi-parameter case
verbatim
to give the pointwise identity

\mathcal{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n},V}f(x)=|V(x)\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n},V}(V(\cdot)^{-1}f(\cdot))(x)|.

Hence

\|\mathcal{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n},V}f\|_{L^{u}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}=\|\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n},V}(V(\cdot)^{-1}f(\cdot))\|_{L^{u}(V;\mathbb{F}^{m\times m})}.

Let
G:=V(\cdot)^{-1}f(\cdot)\in L^{u}(V;\mathbb{F}^{m})=L^{u}(V;\mathbb{F}^{m\times 1})
. It suffices to prove that
\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n}}
is bounded from
L^{u}(V;\mathbb{F}^{m\times 1})
to
L^{u}(V;\mathbb{F}^{m\times m})
.

Using Lemma
4.35
with
A=V(x)
, and the identity
|AB|=|B^{*}A^{*}|=|BA|
for self-adjoint matrices we see that

\displaystyle|V(x)(\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n}}G)(x)|

\displaystyle=|(\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n}}G)(x)V(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim|(\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{k}G)(x)V(x)|

\displaystyle=|V(x)(\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{k}G)(x)|.

From Lemma
2.11
, we deduce that each
x_{i}\mapsto V(x^{\prime}\oplus x_{i})
belongs to
\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{i}})
, uniformly in
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\ominus\mathbb{R}^{n_{i}}
. Let

G_{i}:=(\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{i}\circ\cdots\circ\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{k})G,\quad i\in[k],\qquad G_{k+1}:=G.

Then

\|\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n}}G\|_{L^{u}(V;\mathbb{F}^{m\times m})}\lesssim\|(\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{k})G\|_{L^{u}(V;\mathbb{F}^{m\times m})}=\|G_{1}\|_{L^{p}(V;\mathbb{F}^{m\times m})}

and, for each
i\in[k]
,

\displaystyle\|G_{i}\|_{L^{p}(V;\mathbb{F}^{m\times m})}

\displaystyle=\|\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{i}G_{i+1}\|_{L^{p}(V;\mathbb{F}^{m\times m})}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\ominus\mathbb{R}^{n_{i}}}\|x_{i}\mapsto\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}\big(G_{i+1}(x^{\prime}\oplus\cdot)\big)(x_{i})\|_{L^{u}(dx_{i},x_{i}\mapsto V(x^{\prime}\oplus x_{i});\mathbb{F}^{m\times m})}^{u}\,dx^{\prime}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{u}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\ominus\mathbb{R}^{n_{i}}}\|x_{i}\mapsto G_{i+1}(x^{\prime}\oplus x_{i})\|_{L^{u}(dx_{i},x_{i}\mapsto V(x^{\prime}\oplus x_{i});\mathbb{F}^{m\times\ell})}^{u}\,dx^{\prime}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{u}}

\displaystyle=\|G_{i+1}\|_{L^{u}(V;\mathbb{F}^{m\times\ell})},

where
\ell=m
for
i<k
and
\ell=1
for
i=k
, and we used Fubini’s theorem for the identities, and Proposition
4.34
, with the
\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{i}})
weights
x_{i}\mapsto V(x^{\prime}\oplus x_{i})
in place of
V
, for the inequality. (Note that, even if
G_{k+1}=G=V(\cdot)^{-1}f(\cdot)\in L^{u}(V;\mathbb{F}^{m\times 1})
is vector-valued, all other
G_{i+1}
in the argument above will be
m\times m
-matrix-valued, which is why we need Proposition
4.34
in the stated generality.)

Concatenating these estimates, we find that

\|\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n}}G\|_{L^{u}(V;\mathbb{F}^{m\times m})}\lesssim\|G_{1}\|_{L^{u}(V;\mathbb{F}^{m\times m})}\lesssim\|G_{k+1}\|_{L^{u}(V;\mathbb{F}^{m})}=\|G\|_{L^{u}(V;\mathbb{F}^{m})},

which is the required boundedness of
\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}_{\vec{n}}
, and completes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.37
.

In the framework of set-valued maximal operators in
[
9
,
151
]
, a vector-valued function
f(x)
is identified with the set-valued function
F(x):=\{\alpha f(x):\alpha\in\bar{B}_{\mathbb{F}}\}
. In our framework, where we can allow matrix-valued functions of any number of columns as input for our
\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}
, we may simply identify
\mathbb{F}^{m}
with
\mathbb{F}^{m\times 1}
in the most obvious way. If one prefers matrices of same dimensions
m\times m
as both input and output of
\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}
, then one could equally well identify
f(x)
with the rank-one matrix
F(x)=[f(x),0,\ldots,0]
, or even the positive semidefinite rank-one matrix

F(x)=\frac{f(x)f(x)^{*}}{|f(x)|}

(4.47)

instead. The only thing that really matters is the property
|F(x)^{*}e|=|f(x)^{*}e|
for all
e\in\mathbb{F}^{m}
. Note that
F(x)
in (
4.47
) is an example of a measurable choice of the reducing operator for the seminorms
e\mapsto|f(x)^{*}e|
. However, through the application of Lemma
4.33
, we also need to apply
\mathfrak{M}^{(v)}
to functions like
V(\cdot)^{-1}F(\cdot)
, which need not be self-adjoint-valued even if both
V
and
F
are; hence it does not seem purposeful to restrict the treatment to self-adjoint-valued functions only.

4.7.
Carleson estimates related to matrix weights

With the results concerning maximal operators from the previous subsection, we can now complete our discussion of Carleson embedding-type inequalities related to matrix weights.

Corollary 4.38
.

For all
p\in(0,\infty)
, we have
\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})=\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{Carl}}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.

Proof.

This is immediate from the combination of the
V
-weighted maximal inequalities for
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
(Theorem
4.36
) and the equivalence of these maximal inequalities with
V\in\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{Carl}}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
(Lemma
4.31
).
∎

Corollary 4.39
.

Let
\vec{p}=p\cdot\vec{1}
with
p\in(0,\infty)
, let
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
, and
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
.
Let
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
and
\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x):=V(x)[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}^{-1}
for all
x\in R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
and
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
.
Let
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
be constant on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
, for each
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
.
Then the inequality

\big\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\mathscr{X}}\lesssim\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\mathscr{X}}

holds, with the implicit positive constants independent of
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
, in each of the following cases:

\mathscr{X}=\ell^{\vec{q}}L^{p},\quad\text{or}\quad\mathscr{X}=L^{p}\ell^{\vec{q}},\quad\text{or}\quad\mathscr{X}=(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}\quad\text{with}\quad\vec{q}\leq\vec{p}.

Proof.

By Corollary
4.38
, we have
V\in\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{Carl}}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, thus
V\in\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{Carl}}_{p,s}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
for some
s\in(p,\infty)
by Definition
4.29
. Written out with the notation
\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
and making elementary estimates, this means that

1\gtrsim\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Big\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(\Omega)}\geq\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{\textit{\L}^{s}(R)}\geq\sup_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\sup_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\|\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}.

Thus, the assumptions of Theorem
4.22
are satisfied in each case under consideration, and the said theorem implies the claimed inequality.
∎

We will also need a certain dual version of the results just discussed, dealing with
[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}V(x)^{-1}
in place of
V(x)[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}^{-1}
:

Proposition 4.40
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty)
and
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.

(i)

For some
s>p^{\prime}
if
p\in(1,\infty)
, or all
s\in(0,\infty)
if
p\in(0,1]
, we have

\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\bigg[\fint_{\Omega}\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{R\in\D(\R^{\vn})}{x\in R\subset\Omega}}|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}V(x)^{-1}|^{s}\,dx\bigg]^{\frac{1}{s}}\lesssim 1.

(4.48)

(ii)

Let moreover
\vec{r}=r\cdot\vec{1}
with

r\in\begin{cases}(0,p^{\prime}],&\text{if }p\in(1,\infty),\\
(0,\infty),&\text{if }p\in(0,1],\end{cases}

(4.49)

let
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
and
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
.
Let
\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x):=[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}V(x)^{-1}
for all
x\in R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
and
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
.
Let
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
be constant on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, for each
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
.
Then the inequality

\big\|\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\mathscr{Y}}\lesssim\big\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\mathscr{Y}}

holds, with the implicit positive constants independent of
\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
, in each of the following cases:

\mathscr{Y}=\ell^{\vec{q}}L^{r},\quad\text{or}\quad\mathscr{Y}=L^{r}\ell^{\vec{q}},\quad\text{or}\quad\mathscr{Y}=(L^{\vec{r}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}\quad\text{with}\quad\vec{q}\leq\vec{r}.

Proof.

(
i
):
If
p\in(0,1]
, then, for almost all
x\in R
,

|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}V(x)^{-1}|\leq\|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}V^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(R)}\sim|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}[V^{-1}]_{L^{\infty}(R)}|\lesssim 1

by the assumption that
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
. From this, (
i
) is immediate.

If
p\in(1,\infty)
, then
U:=V^{-1}\in\mathscr{A}_{q}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
for
q:=p^{\prime}\in(1,\infty)
, and the quantity of interest may be written as

\begin{split}|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}V(x)^{-1}|&\leq|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}[V^{-1}]_{\textit{\L}^{p^{\prime}}(R)}|\cdot\Big|[V^{-1}]_{\textit{\L}^{p^{\prime}}(R)}^{-1}V(x)^{-1}\Big|\\
&\lesssim|[V^{-1}]_{\textit{\L}^{p^{\prime}}(R)}^{-1}V(x)^{-1}|\qquad\text{since }V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})\\
&=|U(x)[U]_{\textit{\L}^{q}(R)}^{-1}|\qquad\text{by definition of $U:=V^{-1}$ and $q:=p^{\prime}$}.\end{split}

(4.50)

From Corollary
4.38
, it follows that
U\in\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{Carl}}_{q}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, and hence
U\in\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{Carl}}_{q,s}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
for some
s>q=p^{\prime}
by Definition
4.29
. This means that

\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\bigg[\fint_{\Omega}\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{R\in\D(\R^{\vn})}{x\in R\subset\Omega}}|U(x)[U]_{\textit{\L}^{q}(R)}^{-1}|^{s}dx\bigg]^{\frac{1}{s}}\lesssim 1.

By (
4.50
), this implies the claim (
4.48
).

(
ii
): In either case of (
4.49
), part (
i
) provides us with
s>r
such that (
4.48
) holds. This allows us to apply Theorem
4.22
with
r
in place of
p
to obtain the claimed conclusions.
∎

5.
Some Fourier analysis

In this section, we recall some basic concepts and properties of multi-parameter Fourier analysis.
Let
\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
be the space of all Schwartz functions on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
equipped with the well-known topology determined by a countable family of norms,
and let
\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
be the set of all continuous linear functionals on
\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
equipped with the weak-
*
topology.
For any
f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, let

\widehat{f}(\xi):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f(x)e^{-\mathrm{i}x\cdot\xi}dx

denote the
Fourier transform
of
f
.
This agrees with the normalisation of the Fourier transform used,
for instance, in
[
64
, p. 4]
and
[
159
, p. 452]
,
and allows us to quote some lemmas from these works directly,
whereas using any other normalisation (such as with
2\pi
in the exponent)
would also necessitate slight adjustments here and there in several other formulas.

On the product space
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
, we define

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}):=\bigg\{\phi\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})

\displaystyle:\ \forall\,i\in[k],\ \forall\,\alpha_{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{n_{i}},\ \forall\,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\ominus\mathbb{R}^{n_{i}},

\displaystyle\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n_{i}}}x_{i}^{\alpha_{i}}\phi(x^{\prime}\oplus x_{i})dx_{i}=0\bigg\}.

Definition 5.1
.

We say that
\varphi
is a
Littlewood–Paley function
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
if
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfies

\widehat{\varphi}(\xi)\neq 0\quad\text{ only if }\quad\frac{1}{2}\leq|\xi|\leq 2

and

\big|\widehat{\varphi}(\xi)\big|\geq c>0\quad\text{ if }\quad\frac{3}{5}\leq|\xi|\leq\frac{5}{3},

where
c
is a positive constant independent of
\xi
.
We say that
\varphi
is a
Littlewood–Paley function
on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
if
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
satisfies

\varphi(x)=\varphi(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})=\prod_{i=1}^{k}\varphi^{(i)}(x_{i}),

where each
\varphi^{(i)}
is a Littlewood–Paley function on
\mathbb{R}^{n_{i}}
. These
\varphi^{(i)}
are called the component functions of
\varphi
.

We say that
(\varphi,\psi)
is a
Littlewood–Paley pair
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
if both
\varphi
and
\psi
are Littlewood–Paley functions on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and, in addition, they satisfy

\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\widehat{\varphi}\Big(2^{j}\xi\Big)}\widehat{\psi}\Big(2^{j}\xi\Big)=1\quad\text{ if }\quad\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\{\vec{0}\}.

We say that
(\varphi,\psi)
is a
Littlewood–Paley pair
on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
if both
\varphi
and
\psi
are Littlewood–Paley functions on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
and, in addition, their component functions
\varphi^{(i)}
and
\psi^{(i)}
are Littlewood–Paley pairs on
\mathbb{R}^{n_{i}}
for each
i\in[k]
.

If
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}=(j_{1},\ldots,j_{k})\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
and
\xi=(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k})\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
, we denote

2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\xi:=(2^{j_{1}}\xi_{1},\ldots,2^{j_{k}}\xi_{k}).

If
(\varphi,\psi)
is a Littlewood–Paley pair on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
, it follows from the definition that

\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\overline{\widehat{\varphi}\Big(2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\xi\Big)}\widehat{\psi}\Big(2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\xi\Big)=1\quad\text{ if }\quad\xi\in\dot{\mathbb{R}}^{\vec{n}}:=(\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\})\times\cdots\times(\mathbb{R}^{n_{k}}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}).

(5.1)

Let
\varphi
be a function on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
.
For each
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
, let

\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x):=2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}}\varphi(2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}x),

where
\vec{n}:=(n_{1},\ldots,n_{k})
.
For each
R=2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}([0,1)^{\vec{n}}+h)\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, where
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
and
h\in\mathbb{Z}^{\vec{n}}
,
and for each
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
, let

\varphi_{R}(x):=2^{\frac{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}}{2}}\varphi\Big(2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}x-h\Big)=|R|^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-a_{R}),

where
a_{R}:=2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}h
is the “lower left” corner of
R
.
We also write
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}_{R}:=\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}
to denote the “
level
” of
R
.

5.1.
Calderón reproducing formulas

Lemma 5.2
.

If
(\varphi,\psi)
is a Littlewood–Paley pair on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
, then

f=\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f,\qquad\widetilde{\varphi}(x):=\overline{\varphi(-x)},

for all
f\in\mathscr{F}\in\{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}),\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}),\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})\}
, in each case in the sense of convergence in the usual topology of the respective space.

Proof.

For both
\mathscr{F}\in\{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}),\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})\}
, this is most conveniently checked on the Fourier transform side, recalling that the Fourier transform is an isomorphism on both
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
and
\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
. From (
5.1
) we obtain that the Fourier transforms of both sides agree pointwise on
\dot{\mathbb{R}}^{\vec{n}}
, and the convergence in the relevant topologies follows by some routine computations, noting that

\begin{split}\widehat{\mathscr{S}_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})&:=\Big\{\widehat{f}:\ f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})\Big\}\\
&\phantom{:}=\Big\{f\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}):\ \partial^{\alpha}f(\xi)=0\text{ for all }\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\setminus\dot{\mathbb{R}}^{\vec{n}}\text{ and }\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{\vec{n}}\Big\}.\end{split}

(5.2)

For
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, the convergence then follows by dualising against each
g\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
and using the convergence in this space.
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

If
(\varphi,\psi)
is a Littlewood–Paley pair on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
, then

f=\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}}\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}^{\vec{n}}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f(2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}h)\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\cdot-2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}h)=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\langle f,\varphi_{R}\rangle\psi_{R}

(5.3)

for all
f\in\mathscr{F}\in\{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}),\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}),\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})\}
, in each case in the sense of convergence of the usual topology of the respective space.

Proof.

Case
k=1
can be found in
[
159
, Lemma 2.1]
; the general case can be proved similarly.
∎

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
\varphi
be a Littlewood–Paley function on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
, and let
\gamma\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
satisfy

\widehat{\gamma}(\xi)=1\quad\text{if}\quad\xi\in[-2,2]^{\vec{n}},\qquad\operatorname{supp}\widehat{\gamma}\subset(-\pi,\pi)^{\vec{n}}.

If
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, then,
for every
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
,

(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f)(x)=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f)(a_{R}+y)\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-a_{R}-y).

(5.4)

Proof.

Case
k=1
can be found in
[
12
, Remark 3.16]
;
the general case can be proved similarly.
∎

We also record from
[
12
, Lemma 3.7]
the following elementary inequality,
which is often convenient for handling the full scale of
q\in(0,\infty]
in a unified way.
We typically apply this to situations, where
\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}}a_{i}\lesssim 1
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
q\in(0,\infty]
and
a_{i},b_{i}\in[0,\infty)
for all
i\in\mathscr{I}

be numbers with some countable index set
\mathscr{I}
. Then

\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}}a_{i}b_{i}\leq\Bigg[\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}}a_{i}\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}\Bigg[\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}}a_{i}^{\min(1,q)}b_{i}^{q}\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}},

where the interpretation of the second factor is
\|b\|_{\infty}:=\sup_{i\in\mathscr{I}}b_{i}
for
q=\infty
.

We now obtain a useful estimate:

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
\varphi
be a Littlewood–Paley function on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
.
For every
M,r\in(0,\infty)
, there exists a positive constant
C

such that, for every
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
,

R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, and
x\in R
,

\sup_{y\in R}|\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f(y)|^{r}\leq C(\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*|\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f|^{r})(x),

where, for any
y\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
,

\phi(y):=(1+|y|)^{-M},\quad\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(y):=2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}}\phi(2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}y).

(5.5)

Proof.

Fix
M,r\in(0,\infty)
and let
\gamma\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
be as in Lemma
5.4
.
Using the fast decay of
\gamma

and the identity of Lemma
5.4
,
we obtain the following estimate, for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
,

|\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f(x)|\lesssim\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f)(a_{R}+y)|[1+|2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-a_{R}-y)|]^{-M}.

(5.6)

For
M>n
, we have

\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}[1+|2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-a_{R}-y)|]^{-M}=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}[1+|2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-y)-m|]^{-M}\lesssim 1

and hence, by Lemma
5.5
with
q=r
, we conclude that

|\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f(x)|^{r}\lesssim\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f)(a_{R}+y)|^{r}[1+|2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-a_{R}-y)|]^{-M\min(1,r)}.

An integration over
y\in 2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}[0,1)^{\vec{n}}
, in which case
a_{R}+y
ranges over
R
, gives

|\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f(x)|^{r}\lesssim(\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*|\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f|^{r})(x),

where
\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
is as in the statement of the lemma.
Then also

|\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f(x+z)|^{r}\lesssim(\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\cdot+z)*|\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f|^{r})(x).

If
|2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}z|\lesssim 1
, then
\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(y+z)\sim\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(y)
, and hence

\sup_{\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}:\ |2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}z|\leq c\}}|\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f(x+z)|^{r}\lesssim(\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*|\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f|^{r})(x).

For
x\in R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, all
y\in R
can be written as
x+z
with
|2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}z|\leq c
, and hence the bound just written also gives the bound claimed in the lemma. Since
M
can be chosen as large as we like, we can also make
M\min\{1,r\}
as large as we like, for any given
r\in(0,\infty)
.
This finishes the proof of Lemma
5.6
.
∎

Remark 5.7
.

On the product space
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}=\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{R}^{n_{k}}
, it depends a bit on the context whether it is more convenient to deal with isotropic decay functions
\phi^{N}(x):=(1+|x|)^{-N}
as in Lemma
5.6

or product-type decay functions
\omega^{\vec{N}}(x):=\prod_{i=1}^{k}(1+|x_{i}|)^{-N_{i}}
. In situations, like above, where we can choose the decay rate as large as we like, there is no essential difference. Indeed, if we denote the reciprocals by
\Phi^{N}(x):=(1+|x|)^{N}
and
\Omega^{\vec{N}}(x):=\prod_{i=1}^{k}(1+|x_{i}|)^{N_{i}}
, then

\prod_{i=1}^{k}(1+|x_{i}|)\geq 1+\sum_{i=1}^{k}|x_{i}|\geq 1+|x|,

hence
\Phi^{N}(x)\leq\Omega^{N\cdot\vec{1}}(x)
and thus
\omega^{N\cdot\vec{1}}(x)\leq\phi^{N}(x)
. On the other hand, we also have
1+|x_{i}|\leq 1+|x|
, hence
\Omega^{\vec{N}}(x)\leq\Phi^{|\vec{N}|}(x)
, and thus
\phi^{N}(x)\leq\omega^{\vec{N}}(x)
for any
\vec{N}
with
|\vec{N}|=N
.

Let us also record the following variant of Lemma
5.6
.

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
\varphi
be a Littlewood–Paley function on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
, and
\chi\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
be another test function.
For every
M,r\in(0,\infty)
, there is a constant
C\in(0,\infty)
such that, for every
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
,
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, and
x\in R
,

\sup_{y\in R}|\chi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f(y)|^{r}\leq C(\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*|\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f|^{r})(x),

where
\phi
is as in (
5.5
).

Proof.

Starting as in the proof of Lemma
5.6
, we fix some
\gamma\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
as in Lemma
5.4
. But then, instead of directly estimating as in the proof of Lemma
5.6
, we first take the convolution with
\chi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
of both sides of the identity (
5.4
) guaranteed by Lemma
5.4
, thus obtaining

(\chi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f)(x)=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f)(a_{R}+y)(\chi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}})(x-a_{R}-y).

(5.7)

But
\chi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=(\chi*\gamma)_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
, and the new test function
\chi*\gamma\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, like
\gamma
, also has polynomial decay of any desired order. Hence

2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}}|(\chi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}})(x-a_{R}-y)|\lesssim(1+|2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-a_{R}-y)|)^{-M}.

Substituting this into (
5.7
), we obtain

|(\chi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f)(x)|\lesssim\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f)(a_{R}+y)|(1+|2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-a_{R}-y)|)^{-M},

which is the same upper bound as that obtained for
|(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f)(x)|
in (
5.6
) in the proof of Lemma
5.6
. From this point on, one can repeat the same proof as for Lemma
5.6
.
This finishes the proof of Lemma
5.8
.
∎

5.2.
Decay of Schwartz pairings

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
\varphi,\psi\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.
For every
M,N\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a positive constant
C
,
depending only on
M,N
and
\varphi,\psi
, such that,
for all
\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}},\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
,

|(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}})(x)|\leq C2^{-|\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}|M}\omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\wedge\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}^{(N)}(x)\leq C2^{-|\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}|M}\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\wedge\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}^{(N)}(x),

where

\omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}^{(N)}(x):=2^{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}}\omega^{(N)}(2^{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}x),\quad\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}^{(N)}(x):=2^{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}}\phi(2^{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}x),

and

\omega^{(N)}(x):=\prod_{h=1}^{k}(1+|x_{h}|)^{-N},\quad\phi^{(N)}(x):=(1+|x|)^{-N}.

Proof.

For any multi-index
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{\vec{n}}
, we have

(ix)^{\alpha}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}})(x)=(2\pi)^{-n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\widehat{\varphi}(2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}\xi)\widehat{\psi}(2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\xi)(ix)^{\alpha}e^{ix\cdot\xi}\,d\xi.

Since
(ix)^{\alpha}e^{ix\cdot\xi}=\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha}e^{ix\cdot\xi}
, we may integrate by parts to obtain

(ix)^{\alpha}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}})(x)=\frac{(-1)^{|\alpha|}}{(2\pi)^{n}}\sum_{\beta+\gamma=\alpha}\binom{\alpha}{\beta}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\cdot\beta}(\partial^{\beta}\widehat{\varphi})(2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}\xi)2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\gamma}(\partial^{\gamma}\widehat{\psi})(2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\xi)e^{ix\cdot\xi}\,d\xi,

where, with slight misuse of notation, the dot product
\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\cdot\beta
of
\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
and
\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{\vec{n}}
is interpreted as

\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\cdot\beta:=\sum_{h=1}^{k}i_{h}|\beta_{h}|,

and similarly with
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\gamma
.

Since
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, its Fourier transform
\widehat{\varphi}
vanishes to arbitrary order on the coordinate hyperplanes, i.e., whenever for some
h\in[k]
we have
\xi_{h}=\mathbf{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{h}}
for
\xi=(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k})\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
. And of course
\widehat{\varphi}
and all its derivatives decay faster than any polynomial at infinity. Hence, for any
M\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\big|(\partial^{\beta}\widehat{\varphi})(2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}\xi)\big|\lesssim\prod_{h=1}^{k}\min\Big\{|2^{-i_{h}}\xi_{h}|^{M},|2^{-i_{h}}\xi_{h}|^{-M}\Big\},

and a similar bound is valid for
(\partial^{\gamma}\widehat{\psi})(2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\xi)
. Writing
\vec{\mu}:=\min(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}},\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}})
,
\vec{\nu}:=\max(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}},\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}})
(componentwise), it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\big|(\partial^{\beta}\widehat{\varphi})(2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}\xi)\big|\big|(\partial^{\gamma}\widehat{\psi})(2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\xi)\big|d\xi

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\prod_{h=1}^{k}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n_{h}}}\min\Big\{|2^{-i_{h}}\xi_{h}|^{M},|2^{-i_{h}}\xi_{h}|^{-M}\Big\}\min\Big\{|2^{-j_{h}}\xi_{h}|^{M},|2^{-j_{h}}\xi_{h}|^{-M}\Big\}d\xi_{h}

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{h=1}^{k}\bigg(\int_{|\xi_{h}|<2^{\mu_{h}}}|2^{-\mu_{h}}\xi_{h}|^{M}|2^{-\nu_{h}}\xi_{h}|^{M}d\xi_{h}+\int_{2^{\mu_{h}}<|\xi_{h}|<2^{\nu_{h}}}|2^{-\mu_{h}}\xi_{h}|^{-M}|2^{-\nu_{h}}\xi_{h}|^{M}d\xi_{h}\Big.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\Big.+\int_{|\xi_{h}|>2^{\nu_{h}}}|2^{-\mu_{h}}\xi_{h}|^{-M}|2^{-\nu_{h}}\xi_{h}|^{-M}d\xi_{h}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\prod_{h=1}^{k}\Big[2^{-(\mu_{h}+\nu_{h})M}2^{\mu_{h}(2M+n_{h})}+2^{(\mu_{h}-\nu_{h})M}2^{\nu_{h}n_{h}}+2^{(\mu_{h}+\nu_{h})M}2^{\nu_{h}(n_{h}-2M)}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\sim\prod_{h=1}^{k}2^{(\mu_{h}-\nu_{h})M}2^{\nu_{h}n_{h}}=2^{-|\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}|M}2^{\vec{\nu}\cdot\vec{n}}=2^{-|\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}|M}2^{(\vec{\nu}-\vec{\mu})\cdot\vec{n}}2^{\vec{\mu}\cdot\vec{n}}\leq 2^{-|\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}|(M-|\vec{n}|)}2^{\vec{\mu}\cdot\vec{n}}.

Above, we can pick
M
as large as we like, hence also
M-|\vec{n}|
as large as we like. Thus, redefining
M
, we can simply write the result as

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\big|(\partial^{\beta}\widehat{\varphi})(2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}\xi)\big|\big|(\partial^{\gamma}\widehat{\psi})(2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\xi)\big|d\xi\lesssim 2^{-|\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}|M}2^{\min\{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}},\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\}\cdot\vec{n}}.

Substituting back, it follows that

|(ix)^{\alpha}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}})(x)|\lesssim 2^{-|\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}|M}2^{\min\{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}},\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\}\cdot\vec{n}}\sum_{\beta+\gamma=\alpha}\binom{\alpha}{\beta}2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\cdot\beta}2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\gamma},

where

\begin{split}\sum_{\beta+\gamma=\alpha}\binom{\alpha}{\beta}2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\cdot\beta}2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\gamma}&=\prod_{h=1}^{k}\prod_{l=1}^{n_{h}}\sum_{\beta_{h,l}=0}^{\alpha_{h,l}}\binom{\alpha_{h,l}}{\beta_{h,l}}2^{-i_{h}\beta_{h,l}}2^{-j_{h}(\alpha_{h,l}-\beta_{h,l})}\\
&=\prod_{h=1}^{k}\prod_{l=1}^{n_{h}}(2^{-i_{h}}+2^{-j_{h}})^{\alpha_{h,l}}\sim\prod_{h=1}^{k}\prod_{l=1}^{n_{h}}(2^{-\mu_{h}})^{\alpha_{h,l}}=2^{-\vec{\mu}\cdot\alpha}.\end{split}

Hence, for every
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{\vec{n}}
,

|(2^{\vec{\mu}}x)^{\alpha}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}})(x)|\lesssim 2^{-|\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}|M}2^{\vec{\mu}\cdot\vec{n}}.

We now make a choice of
\alpha
depending on
x
. If
|2^{\mu_{h}}x_{h}|\leq 1
, we take
\alpha_{h}:=0
so that
|(2^{\mu_{h}}x_{h})^{\alpha_{h}}|=1
. If
|2^{\mu_{h}}x_{h}|>1
, let
l
be a coordinate with
|2^{\mu_{h}}x_{h,l}|\sim|2^{\mu_{h}}x_{h}|
. Then we take
\alpha_{h}:=Ne_{h,l}
, where
e_{h,l}
is the
l
-th unit vector in
\mathbb{R}^{n_{h}}
. It follows that
|(2^{\mu_{h}}x_{h})^{\alpha_{h}}|=|2^{\mu_{h}}x_{h,l}|^{N}\sim|2^{\mu_{h}}x_{h}|^{N}
. Hence, in either case, we obtain

|(2^{\mu_{h}}x_{h})^{\alpha_{h}}|\gtrsim(1+|2^{\mu_{h}}x_{h}|)^{N},

and thus

\begin{split}|(2^{\vec{\mu}}x)^{\alpha}|=\Bigg|\prod_{h=1}^{k}(2^{\mu_{h}}x_{h})^{\alpha_{h}}\Bigg|&\gtrsim\prod_{h=1}^{k}(1+|2^{\mu_{h}}x_{h}|)^{N}=\frac{1}{\omega^{(N)}(2^{\vec{\mu}}x)}\\
&\geq\Bigg(1+\sum_{h=1}^{k}|2^{\mu_{h}}x_{h}|\Bigg)^{N}\geq(1+|2^{\vec{\mu}}x|)^{N}=\frac{1}{\phi^{(N)}(2^{\vec{\mu}}x)}.\end{split}

Hence we have proved that

\frac{1}{\phi^{(N)}(2^{\vec{\mu}}x)}|(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}})(x)|\leq\frac{1}{\omega^{(N)}(2^{\vec{\mu}}x)}|(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}})(x)|\lesssim 2^{-|\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}|M}2^{\vec{\mu}\cdot\vec{n}}.

This finishes the proof of Lemma
5.9
.
∎

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
\varphi,\psi\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.
For every
M,N\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a positive constant
C
,
depending only on
M,N
and
\varphi,\psi
, such that,
for all
\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}},\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
,
P\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, and
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,

|\langle\varphi_{P},\psi_{R}\rangle|\lesssim|P|^{\frac{1}{2}}|R|^{\frac{1}{2}}2^{-|\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}|M}\omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\wedge\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}^{(N)}(a_{P}-a_{R}),

where
\omega^{(N)}
is as in Lemma
5.9
.

Proof.

Recall that
\varphi_{P}(x)=|P|^{1/2}\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(x-a_{P})
, and
\psi_{R}
has a similar definition. Then

\langle\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(\cdot-a_{P}),\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\cdot-a_{R})\rangle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(y)\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(y+a_{P}-a_{R})dy=(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*\widetilde{\psi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}})(a_{R}-a_{P}),

where
\widetilde{\psi}(y):=\psi(-y)
satisfies the same assumptions as
\psi
. The claim is then immediate from Lemma
5.9
.
This finishes the proof of Lemma
5.10
.
∎

6.
Multi-parameter Besov–Triebel–Lizorkin-type spaces

In this section, we introduce the matrix-weighted
multi-parameter Besov-type and Triebel–Lizorkin-type spaces
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})

and establish their
\varphi
-transform characterization.
To this end, we first prove the equivalent relations among three quasi-norms
associated with
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})
in Subsection
6.1
.
Using these equivalent relations, we then obtain the

\varphi
-transform characterization of
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})
in Subsection
6.2
.

Although the one-parameter case has been well studied in
[
12
]
,
the situation is different in the multi-parameter case,
since the definition of
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})
involves
taking the supremum over all open sets rather than over all rectangles.
Hence, we need to find some new methods.

6.1.
Different quasi-norms and their relations

It is customary in the theory of Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces to give two parallel sets of definitions, one of function
(actually: distribution) spaces, and another one of sequence spaces. Both of them are given in terms of a quasi-norm of a
sequence of functions that are derived from the original objects. For an efficient discussion avoiding unnecessary repetition, we give a third (or first, to emphasise its fundamental
role in generating the other two) definition of quasi-norms on sequences of functions. This is particularly useful in the present discussion of matrix-weighted spaces, where these weights may be
inserted into the quasi-norms in a variety of ways. Here is the formal definition:

Definition 6.1
.

Let
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,
\vec{p}:=p\cdot\vec{1}
with
p\in(0,\infty)
, and
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
.
Let
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be an admissible permutation of
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
in the sense of Definition
4.6
.

For each
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
, let
V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m\times m})
, and
\mathbb{V}:=\{V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
.
For
\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, let

\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\Omega):=\{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}):\ R\subset\Omega\}\quad\text{and}\quad\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}:=\bigcup_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\Omega)}R\subset\Omega.

(i)

For any sequence of
\mathbb{C}^{m}
-valued functions
\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
, let

\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}:=\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|\Omega|^{-\tau}\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}.

(6.1)

Here and below, we adopt the convention that

|\Omega|^{-\tau}=\infty
whenever
|\Omega|=0
.

(ii)

Let
\varphi
be a Littlewood–Paley function on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
.
For each distribution
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, let

\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}^{\varphi}:=\|\{\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}.

(iii)

For each sequence
t:=\{t_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
of vectors
t_{Q}\in\mathbb{C}^{m}
, let

\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}:=\|\{t_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})},\qquad t_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}:=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}{t}_{R}\widetilde{\mathbf{1}}_{R},\quad\widetilde{\mathbf{1}}_{R}:=|R|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{1}_{R}.

(6.2)

If
V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\equiv V
is the same function for all
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
, we write just
V
instead of
\mathbb{V}
in these quasi-norms.
If
V\equiv 1
, we write
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
instead of
V
,
and in this case, the assumption
\vec{p}=p\cdot 1
can be relaxed to
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
.

Remark 6.2
.

Even when specialised to the one-parameter case
k=1
,
this definition differs slightly from previous definitions of
Besov–Triebel–Lizorkin-type spaces (see
[
158
,
159
,
162
]
for the unweighted and
[
12
]
for the matrix-weighted case).
That is, we take the supremum in (
6.1
) over all open sets, in contrast to the much smaller collection of dyadic cubes in
[
12
,
158
,
159
,
162
]
.
(While dyadic cubes, strictly speaking, are not open sets, estimating them by slightly larger open sets containing them, it is easy to see that the supremum over open sets in (
6.1
) always dominates the supremum over dyadic cubes. We will frequently use this fact in what follows.)
As a consequence of this difference, the two definitions differ in many
cases (see Theorem
9.12
).
The reasons for taking the supremum over open sets
rather than rectangles in the definition of
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
are two-fold.
It is well known that, when
k\geq 2
, the most natural multi-parameter BMO space
was introduced by Chang and Fefferman
[
25
]
in terms of
taking the supremum over all open sets, which is the dual
of the multi-parameter Hardy space.
Note that when
k\geq 2
the space
\dot{F}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}_{2,2}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
in this case coincides with
Chang–Fefferman BMO (see Theorem
9.10
), which differs from the corresponding
rectangular version (see Theorem
9.18
).
Another reason is that the dual space of the multi-parameter
Triebel–Lizorkin sequence space is precisely
\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})

instead of their rectangular version (see Corollary
9.19
).
The key factor behind these is that although any open set in

\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
can be decomposed into the union of maximal dyadic
rectangles, they are not nested and hence may have too much
overlaps, which leads to their futility.
Thus, to capture the whole information of functions or distributions
on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
, we need to take the supremum over all open sets
which can cover all regions of arbitrary shape,
including collections of rectangles at
different scales, avoiding the loss
of control caused by the non-nestedness of rectangles.

Nevertheless, the two definitions coincide in important special cases. First, when
\tau=0
, corresponding to usual Besov–Triebel–Lizorkin (instead of Besov–Triebel–Lizorkin-
type
) spaces, the supremum is obviously achieved by taking a sequence of sets approaching the whole
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
(or, in the case of dyadic cubes, the quadrant that gives the maximal value), which amounts to formally substituting
\Omega=\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
.

Another important case consists of Triebel–Lizorkin-type spaces with
(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}=L^{p}\ell^{\vec{q}}
at the critical value
\tau=\frac{1}{p}
. When
k=1
, letting
\mathscr{D}^{*}(\Omega)
be the collection of maximal dyadic cubes
P\subset\Omega
, it follows from their disjointness that

\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{j}}2^{js}V_{j}f_{j}\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}^{p}=\sum_{P\in\mathscr{D}^{*}(\Omega)}\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{P}2^{js}V_{j}f_{j}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}^{p}\leq K^{p}\sum_{P\in\mathscr{D}^{*}(\Omega)}|P|=K^{p}|\Omega|,

where

K:=\sup_{P\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}|P|^{-\frac{1}{p}}\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{P}2^{js}V_{j}f_{j}\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}.

Hence the supremum over all open
\Omega
is the same as the supremum over all
P\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
also in this case.

Definition 6.3
.

For every
p\in(0,\infty]
and
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
,
we define two versions of local
L^{p}
averaging operators over the dyadic rectangles
\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
as follows:

\langle f\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}:=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\mathbf{1}_{R}\|f\|_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)},\qquad[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}:=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\mathbf{1}_{R}[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}.

We denote by
[V]_{p}:=\{[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
the sequence of functions obtained by the second averaging method. In particular,
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}([V]_{p})
and
\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}([V]_{p})
will denote, respectively,
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})
and
\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})
as in Definition
6.1
for
\mathbb{V}=\{V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
with
V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}
.

We will also identify
[V]_{p}
with the sequence
\{[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
or with the diagonal matrix
\{[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}\delta_{P,R}\}_{P,R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
, both of which carry the same information as the sequence of piecewise constant functions
\{[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
.

The following theorem relating the three types of quasi-norms is the main result of this subsection.

Theorem 6.4
.

Let
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
, and
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
.
Assume that
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
is admissible for
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
.
For each
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
, let either
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f
for some Littlewood–Paley function
\varphi
and
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, or
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=t_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
be as in (
6.2
).
Then we have the following conclusions:

(i)

Under the above assumptions,

\big\|\big\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}\sim\Big\|\Big\{\langle f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}

with the positive equivalence constants independent of

\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
.

(ii)

If we further assume that
\vec{p}=p\cdot\vec{1}
for some
p\in(0,\infty)
and
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, then

\big\|\big\{[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}\sim\Big\|\Big\{\langle[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}\lesssim\big\|\big\{Vf_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}

(6.3)

with the implicit positive constants independent of

\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
.

(iii)

The final
\lesssim
in (
6.3
) may be replaced by
\sim
under any of the following additional assumptions:

(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}=\ell^{\vec{q}}L^{p},\quad\text{or}\quad(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}=L^{p}\ell^{\vec{q}},\quad\text{or}\quad\vec{q}\leq\vec{p}.

(6.4)

Corollary 6.5
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
6.4
,
including (
6.4
) in the weighted case,
for every
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
,
the following equivalences hold:

\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}([V]_{p})}^{\varphi}\sim\Big\|\Big\{\langle[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f)\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}\sim\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}^{\varphi}\qquad

(6.5)

where the positive equivalence constants are independent of
f
.
Moreover, for every
t\in(\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
,

\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}([V]_{p})}\sim\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}

(6.6)

where the positive equivalence constants are independent of
t
.

Proof of Corollary
6.5
assuming Theorem
6.4
.

The first chain of quasi-norm equivalences corresponds to the case of Theorem
6.4
with
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f
, and the second to the case where
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=t_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
is as in (
6.2
), thus constant on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
. [The equivalence of the other quasi-norms to the one involving
\langle\ \rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}
is also true but uninteresting in the second case, since
\langle[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}=|[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|
, when the function inside
\langle\ \rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}
is already constant on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.]
This finishes the proof of Corollary
6.5
.
∎

Remark 6.6
.

To the best of our knowledge, Theorem
6.4
and Corollary
6.5
appear to be new in the multi-parameter case (i.e.,
k>1
), even for scalar weights. For
k=1
, (
6.5
) and (
6.6
) roughly correspond to
[
12
, Theorems 3.9 and 3.27]
, but recall from Remark
6.2
that the definitions here and in
[
12
]
differ even in this case.

The first “
\lesssim
” (actually, “
\leq
”) in (
6.3
) is essentially trivial, and holds for arbitrary functions
V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
in place of
[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}
: indeed,
|g|\leq\langle g\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}
, and hence

\Big\|\big\{V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}\leq\Big\|\big\{\langle V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}.

(6.7)

However, the other bounds will require more work, and the following lemma will play an important role:

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
,
and
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be an admissible permutation of
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
.
Suppose that two sequences
\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
and
\{g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}

of measurable functions on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
satisfy:
there exist
r\in(0,\min(\vec{p},\vec{q}))
,

C\in(0,\infty)
, and
M\in(n(1+\tau),\infty)
such that,
for every
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
,

|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x)|^{r}\leq C\Big(\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\Big)(x),

(6.8)

where
\phi(\cdot):=(1+|\cdot|)^{-M}
.
Then there exists a positive constant
\widetilde{C}
, depending only on the parameters of the space and on
C,M,r
, such that

\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}\leq\widetilde{C}\|\{g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}.

Proof.

By considering
2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
and
2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
in place of
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
and
g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
, the general case follows from the special case
\vec{s}=\vec{0}
; thus, we will assume that
\vec{s}=\vec{0}
in the rest of the proof.

Recall that

\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\Omega)=\{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}):\ R\subset\Omega\}\ \ \text{and}\ \ \Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=\bigcup_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\Omega)}R\subset\Omega.

We introduce the auxiliary sets

A_{0}:=[-1,1]^{\vec{n}},\qquad A_{h}:=[-2^{h},2^{h}]^{\vec{n}}\setminus[-2^{h-1},2^{h-1}]^{\vec{n}},\quad h\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}.

These lead to the splitting

\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=\sum_{h=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}A_{h}}\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}},\qquad\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\leq\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\sum_{h=0}^{\infty}(\mathbf{1}_{2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}A_{h}}\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}})*|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}.

For
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\Omega)
, we investigate

\mathbf{1}_{R}(x)(\mathbf{1}_{2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}A_{h}}\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}})*|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}(x)=\mathbf{1}_{R}(x)\int_{2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}A_{h}}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}}\phi(2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}y)|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-y)|^{r}dy.

If
x\in R
and
y\in 2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}A_{h}
, then
x-y\in 3R^{(h)}
, where
R^{(h)}\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}-h\vec{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
is the
h
-th dyadic ancestor of
R
in the dyadic system
\{\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}+i\vec{1}}\}_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
.
Since
R\subset\Omega
, every
z\in 3R^{(h)}
satisfies

\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}(z)\geq\fint_{3R^{(h)}}\mathbf{1}_{R}=\frac{|R|}{|3R^{(h)}|}=3^{-n}2^{-hn}.

Thus,

3R^{(h)}\subset\Omega^{h}:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}:\ (\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega})(x)\geq 3^{-n}2^{-hn}\}.

By the strong maximal inequality (see
[
34
]
),
we conclude that, for all
\lambda\in(0,\infty)
,

|\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}:\ (\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega})(x)\geq\lambda\}|\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}}{\lambda}\bigg(1+\log_{+}^{k-1}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}}{\lambda}\bigg)=\frac{|\Omega|}{\lambda}\bigg(1+\log_{+}^{k-1}\frac{1}{\lambda}\bigg),

and hence

|\Omega^{h}|\lesssim 2^{hn}(1+h^{k-1})|\Omega|.

(6.9)

Since
3R^{(h)}
is a union of
3^{n}2^{hn}
rectangles of
\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, we even have

3R^{(h)}\subset\Omega^{h}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}:=(\Omega^{h})_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}},

where the latter set has the same relation to
\Omega^{h}
as
\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
has to the original
\Omega
. Altogether, we observed that

x-y\in 3R^{(h)}\subset\Omega^{h}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}},

and hence

\mathbf{1}_{R}(x)\Big((\mathbf{1}_{2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}A_{h}}\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}})*|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\Big)(x)=\mathbf{1}_{R}(x)\Big((\mathbf{1}_{2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}A_{h}}\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}})*(\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{h}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}^{r}|)\Big)(x).

Summing over all
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\Omega)
, we deduce that

\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\Big((\mathbf{1}_{2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}A_{h}}\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}})*|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\Big)=\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\Big((\mathbf{1}_{2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}A_{h}}\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}})*(\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{h}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r})\Big).

(6.10)

We then investigate, for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
,

(\mathbf{1}_{2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}A_{h}}\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}})(x)=\mathbf{1}_{A_{h}}(2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}x)2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}}\phi(2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}x)=(\mathbf{1}_{A_{h}}\phi)_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x),

where

(\mathbf{1}_{A_{h}}\phi)(x)=\mathbf{1}_{A_{h}}(x)(1+|x|)^{-M}\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{A_{h}}(x)2^{-hM}\leq 2^{-hn}\mathbf{1}_{[-2^{h},2^{h}]^{\vec{n}}}(x)2^{h(n-M)}.

Since

(2^{-hn}\mathbf{1}_{[-2^{h},2^{h}]^{\vec{n}}})_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*g(x)\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}g(x),

it follows that

(\mathbf{1}_{2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}A_{h}}\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}})*\Big(\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{h}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\Big)\lesssim 2^{h(n-M)}\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}\Big(\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{h}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\Big).

Substituting this into (
6.10
), we obtain

\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\Big((\mathbf{1}_{2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}A_{h}}\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}})*|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\Big)\lesssim 2^{h(n-M)}\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}\Big(\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{h}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\Big).

Summing over
h
then gives

\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}(\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r})\lesssim\sum_{h=0}^{\infty}2^{h(n-M)}\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}\Big(\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{h}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\Big).

(6.11)

We will use this estimate to bound the quasi-norm

\begin{split}\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{0},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}^{r}&=\bigg[\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|\Omega|^{-\tau}\|\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}\bigg]^{r}\\
&=\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|\Omega|^{-\tau r}\|\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{\frac{\vec{p}}{r}}\ell^{\frac{\vec{q}}{r}})_{\pi}}.\end{split}

Here
\frac{\vec{p}}{r},\frac{\vec{q}}{r}\in(1,\infty]^{k}
and
\pi
is an admissible permutation of
(\frac{\vec{p}}{r},\frac{\vec{q}}{r})
; hence we have access to the triangle inequality and the strong maximal inequality (Lemma
4.5
) in the following computation:

\displaystyle\Big\|\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{h}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{(L^{\frac{\vec{p}}{r}}\ell^{\frac{\vec{q}}{r}})_{\pi}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\frac{\vec{p}}{r}}\ell^{\frac{\vec{q}}{r}})_{\pi}}\quad\text{by assumption \eqref{143}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigg\|\Bigg\{\sum_{h=0}^{\infty}2^{h(n-M)}\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}\Big(\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{h}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\Big)\Bigg\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Bigg\|_{(L^{\frac{\vec{p}}{r}}\ell^{\frac{\vec{q}}{r}})_{\pi}}\quad\text{by \eqref{insert Ohj 2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=0}^{\infty}2^{h(n-M)}\big\|\Big\{\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}\Big(\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{h}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\Big)\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\frac{\vec{p}}{r}}\ell^{\frac{\vec{q}}{r}})_{\pi}}\quad\text{by the triangle inequality}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{h=0}^{\infty}2^{h(n-M)}\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{h}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{(L^{\frac{\vec{p}}{r}}\ell^{\frac{\vec{q}}{r}})_{\pi}}\quad\text{by Lemma \ref{FS strong}}.

Here

\displaystyle\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{h}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{(L^{\frac{\vec{p}}{r}}\ell^{\frac{\vec{q}}{r}})_{\pi}}^{\frac{1}{r}}

\displaystyle=\|\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{h}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}\leq|\Omega^{h}|^{\tau}\|\{g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{0},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big[2^{hn}(1+h^{k-1})|\Omega|\Big]^{\tau}\|\{g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{0},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}\quad\text{by \eqref{Oh bd}}.

Substituting back,

\displaystyle\|\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{\frac{\vec{p}}{r}}\ell^{\frac{\vec{q}}{r}})_{\pi}}\lesssim\Bigg[\sum_{h=0}^{\infty}2^{h(n-M)}2^{hn\tau}(1+h^{k-1})^{\tau}\Bigg]|\Omega|^{\tau r}\|\{g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{0},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}^{r}\sim|\Omega|^{\tau r}\|\{g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{0},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}^{r}

for
M>n(1+\tau)
. Thus,

\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{0},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}^{r}=\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|\Omega|^{-\tau r}\|\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{\frac{\vec{p}}{r}}\ell^{\frac{\vec{q}}{r}})_{\pi}}\lesssim\|\{g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{0},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}^{r},

which completes the proof of Lemma
6.7
.
∎

The following lemma allows us to apply Lemma
6.7
to relevant concrete cases.

Lemma 6.8
.

For any
r,M\in(0,\infty)
and
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, there is a constant
C\in(0,\infty)
such that the following holds: If either

f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=\langle\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty},\qquad g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=|\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f|,

(6.12)

or
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=|t_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|
where
t_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
is constant on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, then
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
and
g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
satisfy the assumptions of Lemma
6.7
with parameters
r,M,C
.

Proof.

Case (
6.12
) is just a restatement of Lemma
5.6
.

For the other case, if
\phi
as in Lemma
6.7
, we observe that
\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-y)\sim|R|^{-1}
if
x,y\in R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.
If
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=|t_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|
and
t_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
takes a constant value
t_{R}
on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, it is then immediate that, for
x\in R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,

\displaystyle|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}(x)=|t_{R}|^{r}=\fint_{R}|t_{R}|^{r}dy\sim\int_{R}\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-y)|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(y)|^{r}dy\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-y)|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(y)|^{r}dy.

This finishes the proof of the second case
and hence Lemma
6.8
.
∎

We can now prove the first equivalence of Theorem
6.4
, even under somewhat more general assumptions:

Proposition 6.9
.

Let
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
,
and
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be an admissible permutation of
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
.
Let each
V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
take a constant value
V_{R}
on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, where the sequence
\{V_{R}\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
is weakly doubling (Definition
2.23
).
Let
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
be either constant on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, or
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f
for some Littlewood–Paley function
\varphi
and
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
.
Then

\big\|\big\{\langle V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}\sim\big\|\big\{V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}

with the positive equivalence constants independent of

\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
.

Proof.

We observed “
\geq
” in (
6.7
). Turning to the main case “
\lesssim
”, Lemma
6.8
says that,
for every
x\in R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,

\sup_{y\in R}|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(y)|^{r}\lesssim\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}(x)=\sum_{P\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\int_{P}\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-z)|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(z)|^{r}dz.

With
V_{R}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
in place of
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
(noting that it satisfies the same assumptions), expanding
V_{R}=V_{R}{V_{P}}^{-1}V_{P}
on the right, this gives

\sup_{y\in R}|V_{R}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(y)|^{r}\lesssim\sum_{P\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\int_{P}\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-z)|V_{R}V_{P}^{-1}|^{r}|V_{P}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(z)|^{r}dz.

(6.13)

By the assumption that
\{V_{P}\}_{P\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
is weakly doubling (Definition
2.23
), say of order
d
, it follows that

|V_{R}V_{P}^{-1}|\lesssim(1+|2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(c_{R}-c_{P})|)^{d}\sim(1+|2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-z)|)^{d},

when
x\in R
and
z\in P
. Thus, in the integrand in (
6.13
),

\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-z)|V_{R}V_{P}^{-1}|^{r}\lesssim\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-z)\Big[1+|2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-z)|\Big]^{dr}=\widetilde{\phi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-z),

where
\widetilde{\phi}
is another function like
\phi
, but with decay
M-dr
in place of
M
.
Noting also that
V_{P}=V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(z)
for
z\in P\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
and
V_{R}=V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(y)
for
y\in R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, we deduce that

\sup_{y\in R}|V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(y)f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(y)|^{r}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\widetilde{\phi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-z)|V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(z)|^{r}dz,\quad\text{for all }x\in R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}),

or in other words,

\langle V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}^{r}\lesssim\widetilde{\phi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*|V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r}.

This agrees with the assumption of Lemma
6.7
with
\langle V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}
in place of
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
and
|V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|
in place of
g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
, as well as
\widetilde{\phi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
in place of
\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
. Since we can choose the decay
M
of
\phi
as large as we like to begin with, we can also make
M-dr
as big as we want, and thus this
\widetilde{\phi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
is just as good. Hence an application of Lemma
6.7
completes the proof of Proposition
6.9
.
∎

A variation of the same argument also yields another comparison from Theorem
6.4
:

Proposition 6.10
.

Let
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,
p\in(0,\infty)
,
\vec{p}:=p\cdot\vec{1}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
,
and
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be an admissible permutation of
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
.
Assume that
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})

and
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
is either constant on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, or
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f
for some Littlewood–Paley function
\varphi
and
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
.
Then

\big\|\big\{\langle[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}\lesssim\big\|\big\{Vf_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}

with the implicit positive constant independent of

\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
.

Proof.

Repeating the beginning of the proof of Proposition
6.9
, but now with the specific coefficients
V_{R}=[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}
, and expanding further with
V_{R}=V_{R}V_{P}^{-1}V_{P}V(z)^{-1}V(z)
, we obtain, in place of (
6.13
),

\begin{split}\sup_{y\in R}|V_{R}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(y)|^{r}&\lesssim\sum_{P\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\int_{P}\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-z)|V_{R}V_{P}^{-1}|^{r}|V_{P}V(z)^{-1}|^{r}|V(z)f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(z)|^{r}dz\\
&\lesssim\sum_{P\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\int_{P}\widetilde{\phi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-c_{P})|V_{P}V(z)^{-1}|^{r}|V(z)f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(z)|^{r}dz,\end{split}

(6.14)

where
\widetilde{\phi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
is another function like
\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
, just like in the proof of Proposition
6.9
, and we used the observation that this function is almost constant on
P\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
. If
p\in(0,1]
and
z\in P
, then
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
guarantees that

|V_{P}V(z)^{-1}|\leq\|V_{P}V^{-1}\|_{\textit{\L}^{\infty}(P)}\lesssim 1.

Thus, we directly deduce that

\langle[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}^{r}\lesssim\widetilde{\phi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*|Vf_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{r},\quad\widetilde{\phi}(y):=(1+|y|)^{-(M-nr)},

(6.15)

noting that
([V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(P)})_{P\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
is weakly doubling of order
n
by Lemma
2.22
.

If
p\in(1,\infty)
, we use Hölder’s inequality with exponent
p^{\prime}/r
and its conjugate, to the result that

\displaystyle\int_{P}|V_{P}V(z)^{-1}|^{r}|V(z)f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(z)|^{r}dz
