\displaystyle\quad\leq\bigg[\int_{P}|V_{P}V(z)^{-1}|^{p^{\prime}}dz\bigg]^{\frac{r}{p^{\prime}}}\bigg[\int_{P}|V(z)f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(z)|^{u}dz\bigg]^{1-\frac{r}{p^{\prime}}},\quad u:=\frac{rp^{\prime}}{p^{\prime}-r},

where
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
guarantees that

\bigg[\int_{P}|V_{P}V(z)^{-1}|^{p^{\prime}}dz\bigg]^{\frac{r}{p^{\prime}}}=|P|^{\frac{r}{p^{\prime}}}\bigg[\fint_{P}|V_{P}V(z)^{-1}|^{p^{\prime}}dz\bigg]^{\frac{r}{p^{\prime}}}\lesssim|P|^{\frac{r}{p^{\prime}}}.

Another Hölder’s inequality with the same exponents then allows us to continue the estimate (
6.14
) with

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in R}|V_{R}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(y)|^{r}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{P\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\widetilde{\phi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-c_{P})|P|^{\frac{r}{p^{\prime}}}\bigg[\int_{P}|V(z)f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(z)|^{u}dz\bigg]^{1-\frac{r}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigg[\sum_{P\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\widetilde{\phi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-c_{P})|P|\Bigg]^{\frac{r}{p^{\prime}}}\Bigg[\sum_{P\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\widetilde{\phi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-c_{P})\int_{P}|V(z)f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(z)|^{u}dz\Bigg]^{1-\frac{r}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\sim\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\widetilde{\phi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-z)dz\bigg]^{\frac{r}{p^{\prime}}}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\widetilde{\phi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-z)|V(z)f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(z)|^{u}dz\bigg]^{1-\frac{r}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\widetilde{\phi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-z)|V(z)f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(z)|^{u}dz\bigg]^{1-\frac{r}{p^{\prime}}}.

Raising to power
\frac{p^{\prime}}{p^{\prime}-r}
, we obtain

\langle[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}^{u}\lesssim\widetilde{\phi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*|Vf_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{u},\quad u=\frac{rp^{\prime}}{p^{\prime}-r},

(6.16)

where
\widetilde{\phi}
is as in (
6.15
). We proved (
6.16
) under the assumption that
p\in(1,\infty)
, but we see that it also agrees with (
6.15
) when
p\in(0,1]
, with the usual interpretations, so we have (
6.16
) for all
p\in(0,\infty)
. As before, we can choose
r
as small and
M
as large as we like; hence we can also make
u
as small and
M-nr
as large as we like. Thus, the assumptions of Lemma
6.7
are satisfied with
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=\langle[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}
and
g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=|Vf_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|
, and an application of that lemma shows that

\big\|\big\{\langle[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}\lesssim\big\|\big\{Vf_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}.

This is what we wanted to prove.
∎

Remark 6.11
.

For the case that
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
is constant on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, a different (perhaps easier) proof could be given, along the lines of the corresponding argument in the one-parameter case, see
[
67
, Theorem 3.4]
and
[
12
, Theorem 3.28]
. Since we want to deal with both cases anyway, we prefer the proof above that covers both of them simultaneously. This is in contrast to
[
12
,
67
]
, where separate arguments were used for function and sequence spaces.

It remains to prove the last claim in Theorem
6.4
, i.e., the estimate of
\|\{Vf_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}
by the other quasi-norms under the additional assumption (
6.4
).

Proposition 6.12
.

Let
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,
p\in(1,\infty)
,
\vec{p}:=p\cdot\vec{1}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
, and
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be admissible for
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
.
Suppose that
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
and that we are in one of the cases (
6.4
). Then

\big\|\{Vf_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}\lesssim\big\|\big\{[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}

with the implicit positive constant independent of

\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
.

Proof.

By definition,

\Big\|\Big\{Vf_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}=\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|\Omega|^{-\tau}\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}Vf_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}.

Here

\displaystyle\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}Vf_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}=\Big\|\Big\{V[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}^{-1}\big(\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big)\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}\leq\big\|\big\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}},

where

\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}:=|V[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}^{-1}|,\quad g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}:=\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}\langle[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f)\big)\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}.

These satisfy the relevant assumptions of Corollary
4.39
with
g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
in place of
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
of the corollary:
\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
is exactly as in the corollary, and
g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
is constant on
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
. Hence the said corollary applies to show that

\begin{split}\big\|\big\{\gamma_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}&\lesssim\big\|\big\{g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}=\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}\langle[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}\\
&\leq|\Omega|^{\tau}\Big\|\Big\{\langle[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}.\end{split}

Substituting back, we obtain

\Big\|\Big\{Vf_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}\lesssim\Big\|\Big\{\langle[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}.

Finally, for
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
as in the assumptions, the related
sequence
\{[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
of reducing operators
is weakly doubling by Remark
2.24
, and hence

\Big\|\Big\{\langle[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}\lesssim\Big\|\Big\{[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}

by Proposition
6.9
.
This finishes the proof Proposition
6.12
.
∎

The proof of Theorem
6.4
now consists of collecting the results of the various propositions:

Proof of Theorem
6.4
.

Claim (
i
) is Proposition
6.9
with
V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\equiv 1
.

Claim (
ii
) follows by combining Propositions
6.9
and
6.10
, noting that, by Remark
2.24
, the assumptions of Proposition
6.9
are implied by those of Theorem
6.4
.

Claim (
iii
) is Proposition
6.12
.
∎

6.2.
The
\varphi
-transform characterization

In Theorem
6.4
, we proved the equivalence of several different norms of sequences of functions
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
, either arising from the Littlewood–Paley components
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f
of a distribution, or from discrete coefficient sequences indexed by the dyadic rectangles; however, we always compared norms of two object of the same type. The aim of this subsection is to complement these results by comparing the norm of a distribution with the norm of a related sequence of coefficients, thereby obtaining equivalences between objects of two seemingly different types. Such equivalences play a key role in the subsequent sections, where operators on function or distribution spaces are studied via their discrete counterparts on sequence spaces. The identification of the function and sequences spaces is obtained via the so-called
\varphi
-transform, first introduced by Frazier and Jawerth
[
63
]
.

Our main result concerning its mapping properties of the matrix-weighted spaces is Theorem
6.16
below. Before getting there, we need some preparations, and we begin with the following elementary bound for individual entries
t_{R}
of
t\in\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})
:

Lemma 6.13
.

Let
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
, and
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
.
Let
\mathbb{V}=\{V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
be a sequence of
\mathbb{C}^{m\times m}
-valued functions,
where each
V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
takes the constant value
V_{R}
on
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
. For
t\in(\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
, we have

|V_{R}t_{R}|\leq 2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}(\tau+\frac{1}{2})}2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot(\vec{s}-\vec{n}/\vec{p})}\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})},

where
\vec{n}/\vec{p}:=(n_{1}/p_{1},\ldots,n_{k}/p_{k}).

Proof.

A lower bound for the supremum defining
\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}
is obtained by taking
\Omega=R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
(Although
R
is not an open set,
a simple modification shows that this choice is still valid.) and dropping all functions except the
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}
-th for this fixed
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
. Thus,

\begin{split}\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}&\geq|R|^{-\tau}\big\|\mathbf{1}_{R}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}|V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|\big\|_{L^{\vec{p}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}=|R|^{-\tau}\big\|\mathbf{1}_{R}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}|R|^{-\frac{1}{2}}|V_{R}t_{R}|\big\|_{L^{\vec{p}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\\
&=|R|^{-\tau-\frac{1}{2}}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}|V_{R}t_{R}|\,\|\mathbf{1}_{R}\|_{L^{\vec{p}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}=2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}(\tau+\frac{1}{2})}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}|V_{R}t_{R}|2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}/\vec{p}}.\end{split}

This finishes the proof of Lemma
6.13
.
∎

Lemma 6.14
.

Let
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
, and
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
.
Let
\mathbb{V}=\{V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
be a sequence of
\mathbb{C}^{m\times m}
-valued functions, where each
V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
takes a constant value
V_{R}
on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, and the sequence
\{V_{R}\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
is strongly doubling (Definition
2.23
). If
t\in\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})
and
\psi\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, then

T_{\psi}t:=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}t_{R}\psi_{R}

converges in
\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
. More precisely, for each
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,

\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|t_{R}\langle\psi_{R},\varphi\rangle|\lesssim\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})},

where the implicit positive constant may depend on the space
\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})
and
\varphi
but is independent of
t
.
In particular, these estimates hold
if
\mathbb{V}\equiv 1
or
\vec{p}=p\cdot\vec{1}
for some
p\in(0,\infty)
,
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,
and
\mathbb{V}=[V]_{p}
(i.e.,
V_{R}=[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}
).

Proof.

Once the other claims are proved, the last claim is immediate from the strong doubling property of the reducing operators of
\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
weights (Remark
2.24
).

Let
P:=[0,1)^{n}
, so that
\varphi=\varphi_{P}
. Inserting the identity as
V_{P}V_{P}^{-1}V_{R}V_{R}^{-1}
, we can estimate

\begin{split}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|t_{R}\langle\psi_{R},\varphi\rangle|&\leq|V_{P}^{-1}|\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|V_{P}V_{R}^{-1}|\,|V_{R}t_{R}\langle\psi_{R},\varphi\rangle|\\
&\sim\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|V_{P}V_{R}^{-1}|\,|V_{R}t_{R}\langle\psi_{R},\varphi_{P}\rangle|=:\mathrm{I},\end{split}

since
|V_{P}^{-1}|=|V_{[0,1)^{n}}^{-1}|
is just a positive number depending on the quantities admissible in the implicit positive constants.

Since
\{V_{R}\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
is strongly doubling, say of order
(\vec{a},\vec{b},\vec{c})\in[0,\infty)^{3k}
, Remark
2.25
shows that

|V_{P}V_{R}^{-1}|\lesssim 2^{|\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}|A}\Omega^{\vec{c}}(2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\wedge\vec{0}}a_{R}),\qquad\Omega^{\vec{c}}(x):=\prod_{i=1}^{k}(1+|x_{i}|)^{c_{i}},

where
A:=\max(|\vec{a}|,|\vec{b}|)
.

On the other hand, Lemma
6.13
guarantees that

|V_{R}t_{R}|\lesssim 2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}(\tau+\frac{1}{2})}2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot(\vec{s}-\vec{n}/\vec{p})}\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}.

Finally, Lemma
5.10
says that

|\langle\psi_{R},\varphi_{P}\rangle|\lesssim 2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}/2}2^{-|\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}|M}\omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\wedge\vec{0}}^{\vec{N}}(a_{R}),

where
\omega^{\vec{N}}=\Omega^{-\vec{N}}
. Hence

\begin{split}|V_{P}V_{R}^{-1}|\,|V_{R}t_{R}\langle\psi_{R},\varphi_{P}\rangle|&\lesssim 2^{|\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}|(A+B-M)}\omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\wedge\vec{0}}^{\vec{N}-\vec{c}}(a_{R})\quad\text{with}\quad B:=n|\tau+1|+|\vec{s}-\vec{n}/\vec{p}|\\
&=2^{-|\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}|M^{\prime}}\omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\wedge\vec{0}}^{\vec{N}^{\prime}}(a_{R})\quad\text{with}\quad\begin{cases}M^{\prime}:=M-A-B\\
\vec{N}^{\prime}:=\vec{N}-\vec{c}.\end{cases}\end{split}

Since
M
and the components of
\vec{N}
can be chosen as large as we like, the same is true of
M^{\prime}
and
\vec{N}^{\prime}
. Now

\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\wedge\vec{0}}^{\vec{N}^{\prime}}(a_{R})=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\vec{n}}}\omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\wedge\vec{0}}^{\vec{N}^{\prime}}(2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\vec{k})=\prod_{h=1}^{k}\sum_{k_{h}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n_{h}}}\omega_{j_{h}\wedge 0}^{N_{h}^{\prime}}(2^{-j_{h}}k_{h}).

Here, for
N_{h}^{\prime}>n_{h}
,

\begin{split}\sum_{k_{h}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n_{h}}}\omega_{j_{h}\wedge 0}^{N_{h}^{\prime}}(2^{-j_{h}}k_{h})&=2^{(j_{h}\wedge 0)n_{h}}\sum_{k_{h}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n_{h}}}(1+2^{-(j_{h})_{+}}|k_{h}|)^{-N_{h}^{\prime}}\\
&\sim 2^{(j_{h}\wedge 0)n_{h}}2^{(j_{h})_{+}n_{h}}=2^{j_{h}n_{h}}.\end{split}

Thus,

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}\lesssim\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}2^{-|\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}|M^{\prime}}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\wedge\vec{0}}^{\vec{N}^{\prime}}(a_{R})\lesssim\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}2^{-|\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}|M^{\prime}}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}}\lesssim 1,\end{split}

if
M^{\prime}>|\vec{n}|
.
This finishes the proof of Lemma
6.14
.
∎

Lemma 6.15
.

Let
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
,
and
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be a permutation of
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
.
Then, for every sequence of
\mathbb{C}^{m}
-valued functions
\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}

and every
\vec{h}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
with
|\vec{h}|\lesssim 1
,

\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\sim\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})},

where the positive equivalence constants are independent of
\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
.

Proof.

By definition,

\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}=\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|\Omega|^{-\tau}\big\|\big\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}.

The quasi-norm of
(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}
is obviously invariant under translations. Hence, making the change of variable
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}:=\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}
, we obtain

\begin{split}\big\|\big\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}&=\big\|\big\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}-\vec{h}}}2^{(\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}-\vec{h})\cdot\vec{s}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}\\
&=2^{-\vec{h}\cdot\vec{s}}\big\|\big\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}-\vec{h}}}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}},\end{split}

(6.17)

where
2^{-\vec{h}\cdot\vec{s}}\sim 1
for
|\vec{h}|\lesssim 1
and a fixed
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
.

Next, we aim at showing that

\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}-\vec{h}}\subset(\Omega^{\vec{h}})_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}

(6.18)

for a suitable set
\Omega^{\vec{h}}
. Recall that

\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}-\vec{h}}:=\bigcup_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}-\vec{h}}(\Omega)}R,\qquad\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}-\vec{h}}(\Omega):=\Big\{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}-\vec{h}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}):\ R\subset\Omega\Big\}.

Consider one of these rectangles
R=R_{1}\times\cdots\times R_{k}\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}-\vec{h}}(\Omega)
. We are going to cover
R
by a disjoint union of rectangles
P=P_{1}\times\cdots\times P_{k}\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
as follows: If
h_{l}\geq 0
, then
R_{l}\in\mathscr{D}_{j_{l}-h_{l}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
is partitioned by cubes of
\mathscr{D}_{j_{l}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, and we let
P_{l}
run through each of them. If
h_{l}<0
, then
R_{l}\in\mathscr{D}_{j_{l}-h_{l}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
is contained in a unique
P_{l}\in\mathscr{D}_{j_{l}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, and this is our choice of
P_{l}
. It is clear that
R
is contained in the union of all
P
formed like this. On the other hand, if
P
is one of these rectangles, then

\frac{|P\cap R|}{|P|}=\prod_{l=1}^{k}\frac{|P_{l}\cap R_{l}|}{|P_{l}|}=\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{l=1}{h_l<0}}^{k}\frac{|R_{l}|}{|P_{l}|}=\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{l=1}{h_l<0}}^{k}2^{h_{l}n_{l}}=2^{-\vec{h}_{-}\cdot\vec{n}}>2^{-\vec{h}_{-}\cdot\vec{n}-1}.

Since
R\subset\Omega
, this shows that

P\subset\Big\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}:\ (\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega})(x)>2^{-\vec{h}_{-}\cdot\vec{n}-1}\Big\}=:\Omega^{\vec{h}},

where
\Omega^{\vec{h}}
is open. Since
P\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, it also follows that
P\subset(\Omega^{\vec{h}})_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
. Since
R
is contained in the union of such
P
, we have
R\subset(\Omega^{\vec{h}})_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
, and since this holds for every
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}-\vec{h}}(\Omega)
, we obtain (
6.18
), as desired. Moreover, the strong maximal inequality guarantees that

|\Omega^{\vec{h}}|\lesssim|\Omega|

for
|\vec{h}|\lesssim 1
.

We can now return to the computation (
6.17
):

\begin{split}\big\|\big\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}-\vec{h}}}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}&\leq\big\|\big\{\mathbf{1}_{(\Omega^{\vec{h}})_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}\\
&\leq|\Omega^{\vec{h}}|^{\tau}\big\|\big\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}\lesssim|\Omega|^{\tau}\big\|\big\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}.\end{split}

Substituting this into (
6.17
), we obtain

\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}\lesssim\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}.

If we apply this to
g_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}:=f_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}
in place of
f_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}
and the shift
-\vec{h}
in place of
+\vec{h}
, we obtain the reverse inequality.
This finishes the proof of Lemma
6.15
.
∎

The following theorem is the
\varphi
-transform characterisation of matrix-weighted multi-parameter Triebel–Lizorkin-type spaces, the main result of this subsection, which extends various related results in the earlier literature. In the unweighted case with Morrey parameter
\tau=0
, the result is due to Frazier and Jawerth
[
63
, Theorem 2.2]
in the one-parameter case, and due to Georgiadis et al.
[
71
, Theorem 4.2]
in the bi-parameter (
k=2
) case for pure Besov or Triebel–Lizorkin spaces.
In the matrix-weighted one-parameter case, it was proved by Frazier and Roudenko
[
67
, Theorem 1.1]
with
\tau=0
and by Bu et al.
[
12
, Theorem 3.29]
with general Morrey parameter
\tau\in[0,\infty)
.

Theorem 6.16
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
, and
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be admissible for
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
.
Let
\varphi,\psi,\varrho\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
be Littlewood–Paley functions, and
\widetilde{\varphi}(x):=\overline{\varphi(-x)}
for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
Let
\mathbb{V}=\{V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
, where each
V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
takes a constant value
V_{R}\in\mathbb{C}^{m\times m}
on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, and let
\{V_{R}\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
be strongly doubling (Definition
2.23
).
Then the operators

S_{\varphi}:f\mapsto\big\{\langle f,\varphi_{R}\rangle\big\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})},\quad\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\to\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})

and

T_{\psi}:t\mapsto\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}t_{R}\psi_{R},\quad\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})\to\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})^{\varrho}

are bounded.
If
(\varphi,\psi)
is a Littlewood–Paley pair, then

T_{\psi}\circ S_{\varphi}
is the identity on
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})^{\widetilde{\varphi}}
.
In particular, these results hold
if
\mathbb{V}\equiv 1
or
\vec{p}=p\cdot\vec{1}
for some
p\in(0,\infty)
,
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,
and
\mathbb{V}=[V]_{p}
(i.e.,
V_{R}=[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}
).

Proof.

Once the other claims are proved, the last claim is immediate from the strong doubling property of the reducing operators of
\mathscr{A}_{p}
weights (Remark
2.24
).

The boundedness of
S_{\varphi}
: For
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,

t_{R}:=\langle f,\varphi_{R}\rangle=|R|^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle f,\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\cdot-a_{R})\rangle=|R|^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}f(y)\widetilde{\varphi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(a_{R}-y)dy=|R|^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f(a_{R}).

Hence

|R|^{-\frac{1}{2}}|V_{R}t_{R}|=|V_{R}\widetilde{\varphi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f(a_{R})|\leq\sup_{y\in R}|V_{R}\widetilde{\varphi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f(y)|

and thus, simply rewriting the previous line in a different notation,

|V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|\leq\langle V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\widetilde{\varphi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f)\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}.

Then, using definitions, the previous pointwise bound, as well as Proposition
6.9
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}

\displaystyle=\|\{V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}\leq\|\{\langle V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\widetilde{\varphi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f)\rangle_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},\infty}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}

\displaystyle\sim\|\{V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\widetilde{\varphi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f)\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}=\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}.

The boundedness of
T_{\psi}
: We already verified in Lemma
6.14
that
T_{\psi}t
is well defined as a distribution in
\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
. Hence its
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})^{\varrho}
quasi-norm is a well defined number in
[0,\infty]
, which we need to estimate. Now

\displaystyle\varrho_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*T_{\psi}t(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}t_{R}\varrho_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*\psi_{R}(x)=\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|R|^{\frac{1}{2}}t_{R}\varrho_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x-a_{R})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\|\vec{h}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|R|^{\frac{1}{2}}t_{R}\varrho_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}(x-a_{R}),

since the Fourier supports of Littlewood–Paley functions imply that
\varrho_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=0
unless
\|\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
.

For
x\in P\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, we then compute

\displaystyle|V_{P}\varrho_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*T_{\psi}t(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\|\vec{h}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|R|^{\frac{1}{2}}|V_{P}t_{R}\varrho_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}(x-a_{R})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\|\vec{h}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|R|^{\frac{1}{2}}|V_{P}V_{R}^{-1}|\,|V_{R}t_{R}|\,|\varrho_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}(x-a_{R})|.

The last factor is dominated by Lemma
5.9
and
the factor
|V_{P}V_{R}^{-1}|
by the assumption of
strong doubling and Remark
2.25
.
Observing in both cases that there is no essential difference between
2^{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}
and
2^{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle|V_{P}\varrho_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*T_{\psi}t(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\|\vec{h}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|R|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega(2^{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(a_{P}-a_{R}))|V_{R}t_{R}|\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}(x-a_{R})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\|\vec{h}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|R|^{\frac{1}{2}}|V_{R}t_{R}|\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}^{\prime}(a_{P}-a_{R}),

where
\phi^{\prime}
is another function like
\phi
, perhaps with a slower decay due to the absorption of the moderately growing factor
\Omega
, but since we can choose the decay of
\phi
as fast as we like, the same is true for
\phi^{\prime}
. We also noted that
\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}
is essentially constant on
P\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
to replace
x\in P
by
a_{P}
.

Noting that
|R|=2^{-(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h})\cdot\vec{n}}
is the inverse of the scaling factor in front of
\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}^{\prime}
, we can further write

\displaystyle|V_{P}\varrho_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*T_{\psi}t(x)|

\displaystyle=\sum_{\|\vec{h}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|R|^{-\frac{1}{2}}|V_{R}t_{R}|\phi^{\prime}(2^{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}(a_{P}-a_{R}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{\|\vec{h}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big|(V_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}})(a_{R})\Big|\phi^{\prime}(2^{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}(a_{P}-a_{R})).

For
r<\min\{1,p,q_{i}:i\in[k]\}
we obtain, denoting by
\phi^{\prime\prime}:=(\phi^{\prime})^{r}
yet another function like
\phi
and
\phi^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle|V_{P}\varrho_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*T_{\psi}t(x)|^{r}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\|\vec{h}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\big|(V_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}})(a_{R})\big|^{r}\phi^{\prime\prime}(2^{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}(a_{P}-a_{R}))

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\|\vec{h}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|R|\big|(V_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}})(a_{R})\big|^{r}\phi^{\prime\prime}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}(a_{P}-a_{R})

\displaystyle\sim\sum_{\|\vec{h}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}(\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}^{\prime\prime}*|V_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}|^{r})(x),

where in the last step we used the facts that
V_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}
is constant on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
and that
\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}^{\prime\prime}
is essentially constant at the same length scales.

Recalling that
x\in P\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
were arbitrary above, we have shown the pointwise estimate

|V_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(\varrho_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*T_{\psi}t)|^{r}\lesssim\sum_{\|\vec{h}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}^{\prime\prime}*|V_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}|^{r}.

(6.19)

Hence

\displaystyle\|T_{\psi}t\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}^{\varrho}

\displaystyle=\|\{V_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(\varrho_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*T_{\psi}t)\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\|\vec{h}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\big\|\big\{(\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}^{\prime\prime}*|V_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}|^{r})^{1/r}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}\quad\text{by \eqref{Tpsi x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\|\vec{h}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\big\|\big\{V_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{h}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}\quad\text{by Lemma \ref{convo bd A bd}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|\big\{V_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{\dot{\alpha}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}}=\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}\quad\text{by Lemma \ref{shift}.}

The identity of
T_{\psi}\circ S_{\varphi}
: According to Lemma
5.6
, we have

f=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\langle f,\varphi_{R}\rangle\psi_{R}

in
\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, for every
f
in this space. When
f\in\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})^{\tilde{\varphi}}\subset\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, the right-hand side coincides with
T_{\psi}\circ S_{\varphi}(f)\in\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})^{\varrho}\subset\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
. Here we can in particular choose
\varrho=\widetilde{\varphi}
.
This finishes the proof of Theorem
6.16
.
∎

Corollary 6.17
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
, and
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be admissible for
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
.
Let
\mathbb{V}=\{V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
, where each
V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
takes a constant value
V_{R}\in\mathbb{C}^{m\times m}
on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, and let
\{V_{R}\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
be strongly doubling (Definition
2.23
).
If
\varphi
and
\varrho
are two Littlewood–Paley functions,
then the quasi-norms
\|\cdot\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}^{\varphi}

and
\|\cdot\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}^{\varrho}
are equivalent. Thus, the space

\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V}):=\Big\{f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m}):\ \|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}^{\varphi}<\infty\Big\}

is independent of the choice of the Littlewood–Paley function
\varphi
.

Proof.

Given a Littlewood–Paley function
\varphi
, we can find another Littlewood–Paley function
\psi
such that
(\varphi,\psi)
is a Littlewood–Paley pair.
By Theorem
6.16
, for
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, we then have

\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}^{\varrho}=\|T_{\psi}\circ S_{\varphi}f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}^{\varrho}\lesssim\|S_{\varphi}f\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}.

Applying this to the Littlewood–Paley function
\widetilde{\varphi}
in place of
\varphi
and noting that
\widetilde{\widetilde{\varphi}}=\varphi
, we obtain
\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}^{\varrho}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}^{\varphi}
, and by symmetry also the reverse estimate.
∎

While the space
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})
is independent of the choice of the Littlewood–Paley function
\varphi
in its definition, the quasi-norms
\|\ \|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}^{\varphi}
are only equivalent, not equal, for different choices of
\varphi
. Nevertheless, this ambiguity is relatively minor, and we mostly drop the Littlewood–Paley function from the notation of the quasi-norms
\|\ \|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})^{\varphi}}
, writing just
\|\ \|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}
, following the usual practice in the area.

Corollary 6.18
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
, and
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be admissible for
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
.
Let
\mathbb{V}=\{V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
, where each
V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
takes a constant value
V_{R}\in\mathbb{C}^{m\times m}
on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, and let
\{V_{R}\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
be strongly doubling (Definition
2.23
).
If both
\{f_{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
and
f
belong to
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})
and
\lim_{N\to\infty}\|f_{N}-f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}=0
,
then
f_{N}\to f
in
\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
as
N\to\infty
.
In particular, this convergence holds
if
\mathbb{V}\equiv 1
or
\vec{p}=p\cdot\vec{1}
for some
p\in(0,\infty)
,
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,
and
\mathbb{V}=[V]_{p}
(i.e.,
V_{R}=[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}
).

Proof.

Once the first claim is proved, the last claim is immediate from the strong doubling property of the reducing operators of
\mathscr{A}_{p}
weights (Remark
2.24
).

For
f\in\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})\subset\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
\phi\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, we can apply the Calderón reproducing formula (
5.3
) of Lemma
5.3
to see that, for any Littlewood–Paley pair
(\varphi,\psi)
,

\langle f,\phi\rangle=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\langle f,\varphi_{R}\rangle\langle\psi_{R},\phi\rangle=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}t_{R}\langle\psi_{R},\phi\rangle,

where
t_{R}:=\langle f,\varphi_{R}\rangle=(S_{\varphi}f)_{R}
are the
\varphi
-transform coefficients of
f
. A combination of Lemma
6.14
and the
\varphi
-transform characterization Theorem
6.16
shows that

|\langle f,\phi\rangle|\lesssim\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}=\|S_{\varphi}f\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}\sim\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})},

where the implicit positive constants may depend on
\phi
but not on
f
. Applying this to
f_{N}-f
in place of
f
shows that
\|f_{N}-f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})}\to 0
implies
\langle f_{N},\phi\rangle\to\langle f,\phi\rangle
as
N\to\infty
.
As this holds for every
\phi\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, this is the claimed convergence
f_{N}\to f
in
\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
as
N\to\infty
.
∎

For all
x:=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}

and
\vec{\sigma}:=(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{k})\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
, let

|x|^{\vec{\sigma}}:=\prod_{i=1}^{k}|x_{i}|^{\sigma_{i}}.

For every
\vec{\sigma}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
the
multi-parameter lifting operator

\dot{I}_{\vec{\sigma}}

(see, for instance,
[
147
, Section 5.2.3]
)
is defined by setting, for all
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,

\dot{I}_{\vec{\sigma}}(f):=\Big(|\cdot|^{-\vec{\sigma}}\widehat{f}\Big)^{\vee},

(6.20)

where the symbol
\vee
denotes the
inverse Fourier transform
,
which is defined by setting, for any
f\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
,

\check{f}(\xi):=(2\pi)^{-n}\widehat{f}(-\xi)
.
Applying a basic calculation, we obtain that
\dot{I}_{\vec{\sigma}}
maps

\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
onto itself.

Corollary 6.19
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
, and
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be admissible for
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
.
Let
\mathbb{V}=\{V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
, where each
V_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
takes a constant value
V_{R}\in\mathbb{C}^{m\times m}
on each
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, and let
\{V_{R}\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
be strongly doubling (Definition
2.23
).
Then, for every
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
, the lifting operator
\dot{I}_{\sigma}
maps
\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},\tau}(\mathbb{V})
isomorphically onto
\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s}+\vec{\sigma},\tau}(\mathbb{V})
.
Moreover, for every
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
,

\|\dot{I}_{\sigma}f\|_{\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s}+\vec{\sigma},\tau}(\mathbb{V})}\sim\|f\|_{\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},\tau}(\mathbb{V})},

where the positive equivalence constants are independent of
f
.
In particular, these results hold
if
\mathbb{V}\equiv 1
or
\vec{p}=p\cdot\vec{1}
for some
p\in(0,\infty)
,
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,
and
\mathbb{V}=[V]_{p}
(i.e.,
V_{R}=[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}
).

Proof.

Let
\varphi
be a Littlewood–Paley function on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
.
Observe that, by the definition of
\dot{I}_{\sigma}

and properties of the inverse Fourier transform, we have,
for every
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
and
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
,

\displaystyle\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*\Big(\dot{I}_{\sigma}f\Big)=\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*\Big(|\cdot|^{-\vec{\sigma}}\widehat{f}\Big)^{\vee}=\Big(\widehat{\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|\cdot|^{-\vec{\sigma}}\widehat{f}\Big)^{\vee}.

(6.21)

Let
\psi:=(|\cdot|^{-\vec{\sigma}}\widehat{\varphi})^{\vee}
.
Since
\varphi
is a Littlewood–Paley function on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
,
it follows that
\psi
is also a Littlewood–Paley function on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
.
Moreover, note that

\displaystyle\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f

\displaystyle=\Big(\widehat{\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\widehat{f}\Big)^{\vee}=\Big[2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}}\widehat{\psi}\Big(2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\cdot\Big)\widehat{f}\Big]^{\vee}

\displaystyle=2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{\sigma}}\Big[2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}}|\cdot|^{-\vec{\sigma}}\widehat{\varphi}\Big(2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\cdot\Big)\widehat{f}\Big]^{\vee}=2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{\sigma}}\Big(|\cdot|^{-\vec{\sigma}}\widehat{\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\widehat{f}\Big)^{\vee}.

From this and (
6.21
), we infer that
\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*(\dot{I}_{\vec{\sigma}}f)=2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{\sigma}}\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f
and hence

\|\dot{I}_{\sigma}f\|^{\varphi}_{\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s}+\vec{\sigma},\tau}(\mathbb{V})}=\|f\|^{\psi}_{\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},\tau}(\mathbb{V})}.

Noting that the assumptions of Corollary
6.19
include those of Corollary
6.17
,
we can apply the said corollary to conclude that both quasi-norms above are
independent of the particular Littlewood–Paley functions
\varphi,\psi
.
This finishes the proof of Corollary
6.19
.
∎

Due to Theorem
6.4
,
all the results in this subsection
remain valid with
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})
and
\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})
replaced by
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
and
\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
.
We list the results that will be used later, but omit the proofs.

Corollary 6.20
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
, and
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be admissible for
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
.
Let
\varphi,\psi,\varrho\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
be Littlewood–Paley functions, and
\widetilde{\varphi}(x):=\overline{\varphi(-x)}
for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
If
V\equiv 1
or if
\vec{p}=p\cdot\vec{1}
,
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,
and
(\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi)
satisfy (
6.4
),
then the operators

S_{\varphi}:\ \dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\to\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)\quad\text{and}\quad T_{\psi}:\ \dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)\to\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)^{\varrho}

are bounded.
If
(\varphi,\psi)
is a Littlewood–Paley pair, then

T_{\psi}\circ S_{\varphi}
is the identity on
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)^{\widetilde{\varphi}}
.

Corollary 6.21
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
, and
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be admissible for
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
.
If
V\equiv 1
or if
\vec{p}=p\cdot\vec{1}
,
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,
and
(\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi)
satisfy (
6.4
),
then, for every
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\dot{I}_{\sigma}
maps
\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},\tau}(V)
isomorphically onto
\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s}+\vec{\sigma},\tau}(V)
.
Moreover, for every
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
,

\|\dot{I}_{\sigma}f\|_{\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s}+\vec{\sigma},\tau}(V)}\sim\|f\|_{\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},\tau}(V)},

where the positive equivalence constants are independent of
f
.

7.
Almost diagonal operators

Given an infinite matrix
B=\{b_{PR}\}_{P,R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
with
b_{PR}\in\mathbb{F}
and an infinite matrix
t=\{t_{R}\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
with
t_{R}\in\mathbb{F}^{m}
, both indexed by the dyadic rectangles
\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, we say that
Bt
is well defined, if

(Bt)_{P}:=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}b_{PR}t_{R}

is absolutely convergent for every
P\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, and in this case we define the new infinite vector
Bt=\{(Bt)_{P}\}_{P\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
by the formula just given. A basic question is to provide sufficient conditions on
B
that guarantee its bounded action on all
t
in a given sequence space.
Starting with
[
63
]
, this question has been extensively studied in the one-parameter Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin sequence spaces,
and useful conditions for the boundedness are known in terms of
almost diagonal
estimates of
B
.
The aim of this section is to extend this theory to the matrix-weighted multi-parameter Besov–Triebel–Lizorkin-type sequence spaces
\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})
. Thanks to the
\varphi
-transform characterisation (Theorem
6.16
), this will also provide a tool for analysing various operators on the corresponding function or distribution spaces
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})
, and we will make extensive use of this in the subsequent sections.

Following
[
13
, Definition 3.1]
(which, in turn, is based on several earlier works, notably
[
63
, p. 53]
), we define the special matrices

b^{DEF}_{i;PR}:=\bigg[1+\frac{|c_{P}-c_{R}|}{\ell(P)\vee\ell(R)}\bigg]^{-D}\begin{cases}\displaystyle\Big[\frac{\ell(P)}{\ell(R)}\Big]^{E},&\text{if }\ell(P)\leq\ell(R),\\
\displaystyle\Big[\frac{\ell(R)}{\ell(P)}\Big]^{F},&\text{if }\ell(R)<\ell(P),\end{cases}

(7.1)

with parameters
D,E,F\in\mathbb{R}
and indexed by dyadic cubes
P,R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{i}})
. On the product space
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
, we then set

b^{\vec{D}\vec{E}\vec{F}}_{\vec{n};PR}:=\prod_{i=1}^{k}b^{D_{i},E_{i},F_{i}}_{i;P_{i},R_{i}},

with vector parameters
\vec{D}=(D_{i})_{i=1}^{k},\vec{E}=(E_{i})_{i=1}^{k},\vec{F}=(F_{i})_{i=1}^{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
and indexed by dyadic rectangles
P,R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, where e.g.
P=P_{1}\times\cdots\times P_{k}
with
P_{i}\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{i}})
for each
i\in[k]
.

A matrix
B=(b_{PR})_{P,R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
is said to be
(\vec{D},\vec{E},\vec{F})
-almost diagonal, if

|b_{PR}|\lesssim b^{\vec{D}\vec{E}\vec{F}}_{\vec{n};PR},

with the implicit positive constant independent of
P
and
R
.
Starting with
[
63
]
, the one-parameter version of this notion
has been studied in several articles. The rather natural extension to several parameters just given seems to have been first introduced in
[
71
, Definition 5.1]
in the case of
k=2
and a specific choice of
(\vec{D},\vec{E},\vec{F})
. Indeed, they proved in
[
71
, Theorem 5.2]
that if
B
is
(\vec{D},\vec{E},\vec{F})
-almost diagonal with

\vec{D}>\frac{\vec{n}}{r},\quad\vec{E}>\vec{s}+\frac{\vec{n}}{2},\quad\vec{F}>\frac{\vec{n}}{r}-\vec{s}-\frac{\vec{n}}{2},

(7.2)

then
B
is bounded on
\dot{a}^{\vec{s}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,
where
r=\min(1,p)
in the Besov case,
and
r=\min(1,p,q)
in the Triebel–Lizorkin case.

We will now discuss the extension of this result to the matrix-weighted multi-parameter spaces.
The remainder of this section is organized as follows.
In Subsection
7.1
, we present the first approach
to establishing the boundedness of almost diagonal operators on
\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{V})
through a reduction to the unweighted case.
After analysing the advantages and the disadvantages of this approach, we introduce the second approach to this boundedness:
in Subsection
7.2
for the case
\tau=0
,
and in Subsection
7.3
for the case
\tau\in(0,\infty)
.
Finally, in Subsection
7.4
,
we prove the sharpness of the boundedness result obtained via the second approach.

7.1.
The first approach

Using the quasi-norm equivalence of Corollary
6.5
, followed by the identification of
[V]_{p}=\{[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
with the diagonal matrix
\{[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}\delta_{P,R}\}_{P,R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
as explained in Definition
6.3
, we obtain

\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}\sim\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}([V]_{p})}=\|[V]_{p}t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}.

Hence, a prospective estimate

\|Bt\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}\lesssim\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}

is equivalent to the unweighted bound

\|\widetilde{B}t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\lesssim\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}

for the new matrix

\widetilde{B}:=[V]_{p}B[V]_{p}^{-1}=\Big\{[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(P)}b_{P,R}[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}^{-1}\Big\}_{P,R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}.

(7.3)

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
\vec{D},\vec{E},\vec{F}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
.
If
B
is
(\vec{D},\vec{E},\vec{F})
-almost diagonal and
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
has
\mathscr{A}_{p}
-dimensions
(\vec{d},\vec{e},\vec{\Delta})
, then
\widetilde{B}
defined in (
7.3
) is

(\vec{D}-\vec{\Delta},\vec{E}-\frac{\vec{d}}{p},\vec{F}-\frac{\vec{e}}{p^{\prime}})
-almost diagonal.
In particular,
\widetilde{B}
is
(\vec{D}-\frac{\vec{n}}{\min(1,p)},\vec{E}-\frac{\vec{n}}{p},\vec{F}-\frac{\vec{n}}{p^{\prime}})
-almost diagonal for every
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.

Proof.

This is immediate from Definition
2.19
of
\mathscr{A}_{p}
-dimensions,
the related estimate for the term
|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(P)}[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}^{-1}|

provided by Lemma
2.20
, and the general upper
bound on
\mathscr{A}_{p}
-dimensions from Proposition
2.16
. Note in particular that

\vec{\Delta}=\frac{\vec{d}}{p}+\frac{\vec{e}}{p^{\prime}}<\vec{n}\Big(\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}\Big)=\left.\begin{cases}\vec{n},&\text{if}\quad p\in(1,\infty)\\
{\displaystyle\frac{\vec{n}}{p}},&\text{if}\quad p\in(0,1]\end{cases}\right\}=\frac{\vec{n}}{\min(1,p)}.

This finishes the proof of Lemma
7.1
.
∎

Corollary 7.2
.

Let
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,
\vec{p}=p\cdot\vec{1}
with
p\in(0,\infty)
, and
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
.
Suppose that, for some
(\vec{D},\vec{E},\vec{F})\in\mathbb{R}^{3k}
, every
(\vec{D},\vec{E},\vec{F})
-almost diagonal matrix defines a bounded operator on the unweighted
\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
. Then

(i)

every
(\vec{D}+\frac{\vec{n}}{\min(1,p)},\vec{E}+\frac{\vec{n}}{p},\vec{F}+\frac{\vec{n}}{p^{\prime}})
-almost diagonal matrix acts boundedly on
\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}([V]_{p})
for all
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
;

(ii)

every
(\vec{D}+\vec{\Delta},\vec{E}+\frac{\vec{d}}{p},\vec{F}+\frac{\vec{e}}{p^{\prime}})
-almost diagonal matrix acts boundedly on
\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}([V]_{p})
for all
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
with
\mathscr{A}_{p}
-dimensions
(\vec{d},\vec{e},\vec{\Delta})
.

Proof.

This is immediate from Lemma
7.1
and the preceding discussion.
∎

The idea behind Corollary
7.2
, of reducing the weighted boundedness of a matrix
B
to the unweighted boundedness of the conjugated matrix (
7.3
) and deriving conditions for the latter from an estimate in the spirit of Lemma
2.20
, is from
[
67
, Theorem 2.6]
. Several
advantages
of this method are the following:

(i)

The proof of the matrix-weighted estimates is very simple, provided that corresponding unweighted results are already available as input.

(ii)

Information about unweighted estimates can be used as a black box, without having to revisit the details of their proof. In particular, the sufficient condition (
7.2
) of
[
71
, Theorem 5.2]
for
\dot{a}^{\vec{s}}_{p,q}
-boundedness immediately yields conditions for
\dot{a}^{\vec{s}}_{p,q}(V)
-boundedness via Corollary
7.2
. Moreover, any future results in the unweighted case may be equally easily used to obtain new corollaries.

(iii)

The method applies to Besov–Triebel–Lizorkin-type spaces
\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
of quite general form, including mixed versions of not necessarily pure Besov or pure Triebel–Lizorkin-type.

At the same time, this approach also has some
disadvantages
:

(i)

It requires unweighted results as input. In contrast to the one-parameter case, where such information is readily available in the literature (see
[
63
, Theorem 3.3]
for the classical spaces with
\tau=0
,
[
159
, Theorem 4.1]
for
\tau<\frac{1}{p}+\varepsilon
, and
[
160
, Theorem 1]
for a reduction of the remaining cases to those covered in
[
63
]
), the available results about almost diagonal operators in the multi-parameter setting seem to be much fewer at the time of writing. (The works
[
27
,
51
,
116
,
156
]
, while proving the boundedness of various classical operators on multi-parameter Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces, take a somewhat different approach that does not address almost diagonal operators on the related sequence spaces.)

(ii)

While qualitatively covering a wide range of spaces
\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
, the quantitative assumptions [i.e., the conditions on the almost diagonal parameters
(\vec{D},\vec{E},\vec{F})
] imposed by this method are stronger than necessary when specialised to the arguably most important case of pure Besov or pure Triebel–Lizorkin spaces. This suboptimality of the simple approach was already observed in the one-parameter case in
[
13
]
, where a direct analysis of almost diagonal operators in the matrix-weighted spaces gave a result,
[
13
, Theorem 13.1]
, that not only improved on the one-parameter analogue of Corollary
7.2
,
[
13
, Theorem 4.7]
, but also gave the best possible result in several ranges of the parameters, as explained in
[
13
, Remark 13.2]
.

To address these shortcomings of the simple method, we next turn to discuss a multi-parameter extension of the direct approach of
[
13
]
.

7.2.
The second approach

We will now develop another approach to the boundedness of almost diagonal operators on
\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
following
[
13
]
, which gives the best available (and in several cases the best possible) results in the one-parameter case. We will now concentrate on the following
main cases
:

Definition 7.3
.

Let
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
,
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be admissible, and
V
be a matrix weight on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
. We say that
(\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi,V)
belongs to
one of the main cases
if
either

(i)

V\equiv 1
and the other parameters are arbitrary (unweighted case), or

(ii)

\vec{p}=p\cdot\vec{1}
, the weight is
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, and
(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}=\ell^{\vec{q}}L^{p}
(Besov case), or

(iii)

\vec{p}=p\cdot\vec{1}
, the weight is
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, and
(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}=L^{p}\ell^{\vec{q}}
(Triebel–Lizorkin case).

The motivation for cases (
ii
) and (
iii
) should be clear, as these correspond to the typical cases of interest in the theory of function spaces. The motivation for also addressing case (
i
) is related to our
first approach in Subsection
7.1
: as soon as we have estimates for almost diagonal operators on the unweighted
\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, these immediately imply estimates on the matrix weighted
\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
, and not only those of pure Besov or pure Triebel–Lizorkin-type. On the other hand, as noted in Subsection
7.1
, the existing results even this unweighted case are not so many, and thus it is useful to provide new input that can be fed into the general machine of Corollary
7.2
.

Remark 7.4
.

The case of general
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
may be reduced to
\vec{s}=\vec{0}
by an argument essentially similar to the reduction of the weighted case to the unweighted one in the first approach. Indeed,

\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}=\|\mathfrak{I}^{\vec{s}}t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{0},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)},\qquad\mathfrak{I}^{\vec{s}}:=\Bigg\{\delta_{P,R}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\ell(P_{i})^{-s_{i}}\Bigg\}_{P,R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})},

which means that

\|Bt\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}\lesssim\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}\Longleftrightarrow\|B_{\vec{s}}t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{0},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}\lesssim\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{0},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)},\quad B_{\vec{s}}:=\mathfrak{I}^{\vec{s}}B\mathfrak{I}^{-\vec{s}},

where

\displaystyle B_{\vec{s}}\text{ is }(\vec{D},\vec{E},\vec{F})\text{-almost diagonal }

\displaystyle\quad\Longleftrightarrow B\text{ is }(\vec{D},\vec{E}+\vec{s},\vec{F}-\vec{s})\text{-almost diagonal}.

The following lemma is essentially a combination of algebraic manipulations and elementary inequalities; it is stated for general sequences of matrix-valued multiplying sequences to emphasise the fact that no special assumptions on the matrix weights, like
\mathscr{A}_{p}
or similar, are used at this point.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
,
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
, and
B
be
(\vec{D},\vec{E},\vec{F})
-almost diagonal for some
\vec{D},\vec{E},\vec{F}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
.
Let
a\in(0,1]
, let
H_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{F}^{m\times m}
be measurable and almost everywhere invertible, and let
0<u\leq\min\{1,p_{i},q_{i}:i\in[k]\}
.
If
t=\{t_{R}\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
is such that the right-hand side of (
7.4
) below is finite, then

Bt
is well defined and

\begin{split}\|\{H_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(Bt)_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}^{u}\lesssim&\sum_{\vec{h}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\sum_{\vec{l}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\Bigg[2^{-(\vec{E}-\frac{\vec{n}}{2})\cdot\vec{h}_{-}}2^{-(\vec{F}+\frac{\vec{n}}{2}-\frac{\vec{n}}{a})\cdot\vec{h}_{+}}2^{-(\vec{D}-\frac{\vec{n}}{a})\cdot\vec{l}}\phantom{\fint}\Big.\\
&\times\Big.\Big\|\Big\{\Big[\fint_{C(\cdot,2^{\vec{l}+\vec{h}_{+}-\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}})}|H_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{h}}(\cdot)t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(y)|^{a}dy\Big]^{\frac{1}{a}}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}\Bigg]^{u},\end{split}

(7.4)

where the implicit positive constant is independent of
t
,

C(x,2^{\vec{l}+\vec{h}_{+}-\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}):=\prod_{\nu=1}^{k}B_{\nu}(x_{\nu},2^{l_{\nu}+(h_{\nu})_{+}-i_{\nu}}),\quad x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}),

and each
B_{\nu}
is a ball in the respective
\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}
.

Proof.

Case
k=1
is
[
13
, Lemma 5.4 and Remark 5.5]
. The general case follows by routine modifications of the same argument.
∎

Remark 7.6
.

As in
[
13
, Lemma 5.4]
, we have formulated Lemma
7.5
for general functions
H_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
, although we will actually only apply it with
H_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\equiv V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
. Another natural choice that suggests itself would be to take
H_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}
, leading to quantities related to the quasi-norm
\|\ \|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}([V]_{p})}
.

However, one should observe the mismatch of the indices
\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{h}
and
\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}
on the right-hand side of (
7.4
). This is of no concern when all
H_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{h}}\equiv V
are equal anyway. On the other hand,
with
H_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}=[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}},p}
, any attempts of estimating the right-hand side of (
7.4
) by
\|[V]_{p}t\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}
would easily ask for an application of Lemma
2.20
which, in turn, would introduce dependence on the
\mathscr{A}_{p}
-dimensions
(\vec{d},\vec{e},\vec{\Delta})
. Working with
H_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\equiv V
instead, we are led to applying the Carleson estimates of Corollary
4.39
and Proposition
4.40
instead of Lemma
2.20
, and this has the advantage of avoiding dependence on the
\mathscr{A}_{p}
-dimensions.

Taking
H_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\equiv V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, we will then estimate the quasi-norms on the right-hand side of (
7.4
). Let us simply write
\vec{l}
in place of
\vec{l}+\vec{h}_{+}
; in any case, this is an arbitrary vector in
\mathbb{N}^{k}
. In our treatment of the one-parameter case in
[
13
]
, we gave a separate argument in the Besov and in the Triebel–Lizorkin case, the former one being somewhat easier. Here, we treat all cases at once, and leave it to the interested reader to work out the simplifications that one can make if one is only interested in the Besov case.

Lemma 7.7
.

Let
\vec{p}=p\cdot\vec{1}
with
p\in(0,\infty)
, let
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
, and let
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.
Let
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be an admissible permutation of
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
(Definition
4.6
) and
a\in(0,r(\pi,\vec{p},\vec{q}))
(see (
4.7
)).
Suppose, moreover, that
(\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi,V)
belongs to one of the main cases (Definition
7.3
).
Then all
t\in(\mathbb{C}^{m})^{\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
and
\vec{l}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
satisfy

\bigg\|\bigg\{\bigg[\fint_{C(\cdot,2^{\vec{l}-\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}})}|V(\cdot)t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(y)|^{a}\,dy\bigg]^{\frac{1}{a}}\bigg\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\bigg\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}\lesssim\|Vt\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}},

where the implicit positive constant is independent of
t
and
\vec{l}
.

Remark 7.8
.

Note that
r(\pi,\vec{p},\vec{q})=p
in the Besov case, and
r(\pi,\vec{p},\vec{q})=\min\{p,q_{\nu}:\nu\in[k]\}
in the Triebel–Lizorkin case. Moreover, the condition that
\pi
be admissible is automatic in the Besov case, while in the Triebel–Lizorkin case it requires that
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty)^{k-h}\times\{\infty\}^{h}
for some
h\in\{0,1,\ldots,k\}
, i.e., all infinite components of
\vec{q}
, if any, are at the end of the sequence.

Proof of Lemma
7.7
.

We may clearly assume that
\|Vt\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}<\infty
, for otherwise there is nothing to prove.

Let us first take care of the unweighted case
V\equiv 1
. Then we can simply dominate the averages over the rectangles
C(\cdot,2^{\vec{l}-\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}})
by the strong maximal operator
\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}
and apply Theorem
4.7
to deduce that

\displaystyle\bigg\|\Big\{\Big[\fint_{C(\cdot,2^{\vec{l}-\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}})}|t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(y)|^{a}dy\Big]^{\frac{1}{a}}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\bigg\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\{[\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}(|t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}|^{a})]^{\frac{1}{a}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}=\|\{\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}(|t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}|^{a})\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{\frac{\vec{p}}{a}}\ell^{\frac{\vec{q}}{a}})_{\pi}}^{\frac{1}{a}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\{|t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}|^{a}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{\frac{\vec{p}}{a}}\ell^{\frac{\vec{q}}{a}})_{\pi}}^{\frac{1}{a}}=\|\{t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}},

which completes the short argument in the unweighted case.

We then turn to the more difficult matrix-weighted cases, where we assume that
(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}
takes one of the forms
L^{p}\ell^{\vec{q}}
or
\ell^{\vec{q}}L^{p}
.

As in the proof of
[
13
, Lemma 8.5]
, for each
\nu\in[k]
, the “one-third trick”
[
84
, Lemma 3.2.26]
allows one to pick
3^{n_{\nu}}
shifted systems of dyadic cubes
\mathscr{D}^{\alpha_{\nu}}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}})
,
\alpha_{\nu}\in\{0,\frac{1}{3},\frac{2}{3}\}^{n_{\nu}}
, so that each ball
B_{\nu}(x_{\nu},2^{l_{\nu}-i_{\nu}})
is contained in some shifted dyadic cube
S_{\nu}\in\mathscr{D}^{\alpha_{\nu}}_{i_{\nu}-l_{\nu}-2}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}})
; thus,
C(x,2^{\vec{l}-\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}})
is contained in

S:=\prod_{\nu=1}^{k}S_{\nu}\in\prod_{\nu=1}^{k}\mathscr{D}^{\alpha_{\nu}}_{i_{\nu}-l_{\nu}-2}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}})=:\mathscr{D}^{\vec{\alpha}}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{l}-2\cdot\vec{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}),\quad\vec{\alpha}=(\alpha_{\nu})_{\nu=1}^{k}\in\{0,\tfrac{1}{3},\tfrac{2}{3}\}^{\vec{n}},

where each
\mathscr{D}^{\vec{\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}):=\bigcup_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\mathscr{D}^{\alpha_{\nu}}_{i_{\nu}}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}})
is a shifted system of dyadic rectangles on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
. Then

\displaystyle\bigg[\fint_{C(x,2^{\vec{l}+\vec{h}_{+}-\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}})}|V(\cdot)t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(y)|^{a}\,dy\bigg]^{\frac{1}{a}}\lesssim\sum_{\vec{\alpha}\in\{0,\frac{1}{3},\frac{2}{3}\}^{n}}\sum_{S\in\mathscr{D}^{\vec{\alpha}}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{l}-2\cdot\vec{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\mathbf{1}_{S}(\cdot)\bigg[\fint_{S}|V(\cdot)t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(y)|^{a}\,dy\bigg]^{\frac{1}{a}}.

Thanks to the quasi-triangle inequality, it is enough to estimate the quantity on the right for each fixed
\vec{\alpha}
, and we will just write
\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
instead of
\mathscr{D}^{\vec{\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.

The next issue is the quantity
V(x)t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(y)
, where
V
and
t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}
are evaluated at different points, in contrast to how they should appear in
\|Vt\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}
. To fix this, we will make use of the decomposition

V(x)t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(y)=\Big(V(x)[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(S)}^{-1}\Big)\Big([V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(S)}V(y)^{-1}\Big)\Big[V(y)t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(y)\Big].

(7.5)

Extracting the first factor and applying Corollary
4.39
, we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg\|\Big\{\sum_{S\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{l}-2\cdot\vec{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\mathbf{1}_{S}\Big(\fint_{S}|V(\cdot)t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(y)|^{a}dy\Big)^{\frac{1}{a}}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\bigg\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\bigg\|\Big\{\sum_{S\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{l}-2\cdot\vec{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\mathbf{1}_{S}|V(\cdot)[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(S)}^{-1}|\Big(\fint_{S}|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(S)}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(y)|^{a}dy\Big)^{\frac{1}{a}}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\bigg\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\bigg\|\Big\{\sum_{S\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{l}-2\cdot\vec{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\mathbf{1}_{S}\Big(\fint_{S}|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(S)}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(y)|^{a}dy\Big)^{\frac{1}{a}}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\bigg\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}\quad\text{by Corollary \ref{Carl Ap}}

\displaystyle\quad=\bigg\|\Big\{\sum_{S\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{l}-2\cdot\vec{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\mathbf{1}_{S}\fint_{S}|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(S)}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(y)|^{a}dy\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\bigg\|_{(L^{\frac{\vec{p}}{a}}\ell^{\frac{\vec{q}}{a}})_{\pi}}^{\frac{1}{a}}.

To remove the second factor in (
7.5
), we dualise with a suitable
\|g\|_{(L^{(\frac{\vec{p}}{a})^{\prime}}\ell^{(\frac{\vec{q}}{a})^{\prime}})_{\pi}}=1
:

\displaystyle\bigg\|\Big\{\sum_{S\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{l}-2\cdot\vec{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\mathbf{1}_{S}\fint_{S}|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(S)}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(y)|^{a}\,dy\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\bigg\|_{(L^{\frac{\vec{p}}{a}}\ell^{\frac{\vec{q}}{a}})_{\pi}}

\displaystyle\quad\sim\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(x)\bigg(\sum_{S\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{l}-2\cdot\vec{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\mathbf{1}_{S}(x)\fint_{S}|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(S)}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(y)|^{a}\,dy\bigg)dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\bigg(\sum_{S\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{l}-2\cdot\vec{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(S)}V^{-1}(y)|^{a}\fint_{S}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(x)dx\,\mathbf{1}_{S}(y)\bigg)|V(y)t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(y)|^{a}\,dy

\displaystyle\quad\leq\bigg\|\Big\{\sum_{S\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{l}-2\cdot\vec{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(S)}V^{-1}(\cdot)|^{a}\fint_{S}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(x)dx\,\mathbf{1}_{S}(\cdot)\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\bigg\|_{(L^{(\frac{\vec{p}}{a})^{\prime}}\ell^{(\frac{\vec{q}}{a})^{\prime}})_{\pi}}\|\{|V(\cdot)t_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(\cdot)|^{a}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{\frac{\vec{p}}{a}}\ell^{\frac{\vec{q}}{a}})_{\pi}}

\displaystyle\quad=\bigg\|\Big\{\sum_{S\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{l}-2\cdot\vec{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(S)}V^{-1}(\cdot)|\Big(\fint_{S}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(x)dx\Big)^{\frac{1}{a}}\mathbf{1}_{S}(\cdot)\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\bigg\|_{(L^{a(\frac{\vec{p}}{a})^{\prime}}\ell^{a(\frac{\vec{q}}{a})^{\prime}})_{\pi}}^{a}\|Vt\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}^{a}.

We now want to apply the “dual” Carleson embedding inequality, Proposition
4.40
(
ii
), with multipliers
|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(S)}V^{-1}(\cdot)|
and exponents
r:=a(\frac{p}{a})^{\prime}
as well as
a(\frac{\vec{q}}{a})^{\prime}
in place of
\vec{q}
.

Let us check that the assumptions of Proposition
4.40
(
ii
) are satisfied. We have
p\in(0,\infty)
and
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
as in Proposition
4.40
. Moreover, since
a<\min(1,p)
, it follows that
\frac{p}{a}>\max(1,p)
, thus
(\frac{p}{a})^{\prime}<p^{\prime}
, and then
r=a(\frac{p}{a})^{\prime}<p^{\prime}
as well.
This confirms condition (
4.49
) of Proposition
4.40
(
ii
).
Thus, Proposition
4.40
(
ii
) applies to give the bound

\displaystyle\bigg\|\bigg\{\sum_{S\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{l}-2\cdot\vec{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(S)}V^{-1}(\cdot)|\bigg[\fint_{S}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(x)dx\bigg]^{\frac{1}{a}}\mathbf{1}_{S}(\cdot)\bigg\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\bigg\|_{(L^{a(\frac{\vec{p}}{a})^{\prime}}\ell^{a(\frac{\vec{q}}{a})^{\prime}})_{\pi}}^{a}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\bigg\|\bigg\{\sum_{S\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{l}-2\cdot\vec{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\bigg[\fint_{S}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(x)dx\bigg]^{\frac{1}{a}}\mathbf{1}_{S}\bigg\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\bigg\|_{(L^{a(\frac{\vec{p}}{a})^{\prime}}\ell^{a(\frac{\vec{q}}{a})^{\prime}})_{\pi}}^{a}

\displaystyle\quad=\bigg\|\bigg\{\sum_{S\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{l}-2\cdot\vec{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\fint_{S}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(x)dx\,\mathbf{1}_{S}\bigg\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\bigg\|_{(L^{(\frac{\vec{p}}{a})^{\prime}}\ell^{(\frac{\vec{q}}{a})^{\prime}})_{\pi}}.

We dualise this again with a suitable
\|f\|_{(L^{\frac{\vec{p}}{a}}\ell^{\frac{\vec{q}}{a}})_{\pi}}=1
. Thus,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\bigg\{\sum_{S\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{l}-2\cdot\vec{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\fint_{S}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(x)dx\,\mathbf{1}_{S}\bigg\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\bigg\|_{(L^{(\frac{\vec{p}}{a})^{\prime}}\ell^{(\frac{\vec{q}}{a})^{\prime}})_{\pi}}

\displaystyle\quad\sim\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(y)\bigg[\sum_{S\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{l}-2\cdot\vec{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\fint_{S}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(x)dx\,\mathbf{1}_{S}(y)\bigg]dy

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\bigg[\sum_{S\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{l}-2\cdot\vec{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\fint_{S}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(y)dy\,\mathbf{1}_{S}(x)\bigg]g_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(x)dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq\bigg\|\bigg\{\sum_{S\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}-\vec{l}-2\cdot\vec{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\fint_{S}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(y)dy\,\mathbf{1}_{S}\bigg\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\bigg\|_{(L^{\frac{\vec{p}}{a}}\ell^{\frac{\vec{q}}{a}})_{\pi}}\|g\|_{(L^{(\frac{\vec{p}}{a})^{\prime}}\ell^{(\frac{\vec{q}}{a})^{\prime}})_{\pi}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\|\{\mathcal{M}_{\vec{n}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{\frac{\vec{p}}{a}}\ell^{\frac{\vec{q}}{a}})_{\pi}}\times 1\lesssim\|\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{(L^{\frac{\vec{p}}{a}}\ell^{\frac{\vec{q}}{a}})_{\pi}}=1,

using the vector-valued maximal inequality, which is valid under the assumption that
\pi
is an admissible permutation. (Note that this last step was essentially the same as the full argument in the unweighted case; in the matrix-weighted setting, it took some additional effort to get to this point!)
This finishes the proof of Lemma
7.7
.
∎

Remark 7.9
.

The main working engine of the proof of Lemma
7.7
in the matrix weighted cases consisted of the Carleson measure estimates of Corollary
4.39
and Proposition
4.40
. Since the assumptions of these results allow an additional case besides the pure Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces, one may ask why a version of this third case is not included in Lemma
7.7
. The reason is the following: While the third case of both Corollary
4.39
and Proposition
4.40
consists of the same condition
\vec{q}\leq\vec{p}=p\cdot\vec{1}
, in the proof of Lemma
7.7
, we apply these two results in two different spaces, essentially dual to each other. More precisely, we apply Corollary
4.39
with the original exponents
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
and Proposition
4.40
with exponents
(a(\frac{\vec{p}}{a})^{\prime},a(\frac{\vec{q}}{a})^{\prime})
.
Note that
a(\frac{\vec{q}}{a})^{\prime}\leq a(\frac{\vec{p}}{a})^{\prime}
is equivalent to
\vec{p}\leq\vec{q}
, so if we have both
\vec{q}\leq\vec{p}
and
a(\frac{\vec{q}}{a})^{\prime}\leq a(\frac{\vec{p}}{a})^{\prime}
, then indeed
\vec{q}=\vec{p}=p\cdot\vec{1}
. Then
(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}=L^{p}\ell^{p}=\ell^{p}L^{p}
by Fubini’s theorem, and we would be in the scope of these pure cases anyway. Thus, there is nothing new that this third case of Corollary
4.39
