for details in the one-parameter case that can be easily adapted); we omit the details.

Proposition 8.11
.

Let
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
,
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,

\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be admissible for
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
,
and
V
be a matrix weight such that
(\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi,V)
belongs
to one of the main cases (Definition
7.3
).
Then
\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})\subset\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
.
Moreover, there exist
\vec{M}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}

and a positive constant
C
such that,
for every
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
,

\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}\leq C\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\gamma\in\N^{\vec n}}{|\gamma|_{\rm{vec}}\leq\vec M}}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}|(\partial^{\gamma}f)(x)|\prod_{\nu=1}^{k}(1+|x_{\nu}|)^{n+M_{\nu}+|\gamma_{\nu}|}.

8.2.
Pseudo-differential operators

In this section, we follow the approach of
Grafakos and Torres used in
[
77
]
and
establish the boundedness of multi-parameter
pseudo-differential operators on
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
.

Now, we recall the concepts of the class
\dot{S}_{1,1}^{\vec{u}}

and its related pseudo-differential operators
(see, for instance,
[
77
, pp. 261–263]
).

Definition 8.12
.

Let
\vec{u}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
.
The
class
\dot{S}_{1,1}^{\vec{u}}
is defined to be the set of all functions

a\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\times(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}))

such that, for every
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{\vec{n}}
,

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}},\,\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\prod_{\nu=1}^{k}|\xi_{\nu}|^{-u_{\nu}-|\alpha_{\nu}|+|\beta_{\nu}|}\Big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}a(x,\xi)\Big|<\infty.

Definition 8.13
.

Let
\vec{u}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
and
a\in\dot{S}_{1,1}^{\vec{u}}
.
Define the
multi-parameter pseudo-differential operator

a(x,D)
with symbol
a
by setting,
for every
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
,

a(x,D)(f):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}e^{ix\cdot\xi}a(x,\xi)\widehat{f}(\xi)\,d\xi.

To present the main theorem in this section, we need the following technical lemmas.
Here, and thereafter, let
\mathcal{R}_{l,N}
be an appropriate seminorm in
\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.

Lemma 8.14
.

Let
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.
Then, for every
\vec{M}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
and every
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{\vec{n}}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\Big(\prod_{\nu=1}^{k}|\xi_{\nu}|^{M_{\nu}}\Big)\Big|\partial^{\gamma}\widehat{f}(\xi)\Big|\lesssim\mathcal{R}_{l,N}(f),

with
l,N\in\mathbb{N}
large enough.

Proof.

Let
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{\vec{n}}
.
From (
5.2
)
and the fact that the Fourier transform is an isomorphism on
\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,
it follows that, for every
M\in\mathbb{N}
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
,

\displaystyle\Big|\partial^{\gamma}\widehat{f}(\xi)\Big|\lesssim|\xi_{1}|^{M}\mathcal{R}_{l,N}(f),

where
\delta\in(0,1)
and
l,N\in\mathbb{N}
are large enough.
Since each dimension plays an equal role, we have,
for every
\nu\in[k]
and every
M\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}|\xi_{\nu}|^{-M}\Big|\partial^{\gamma}\widehat{f}(\xi)\Big|\lesssim\mathcal{R}_{l,N}(f),

(8.28)

where
l,N\in\mathbb{N}
are large enough.
In addition, by the fact that the Fourier transform is
an isomorphism on
\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
again,
we conclude that, for every
\nu\in[k]
and every
M\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}|\xi_{\nu}|^{M}\Big|\partial^{\gamma}\widehat{f}(\xi)\Big|\leq\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}|\xi|^{M}\Big|\partial^{\gamma}\widehat{f}(\xi)\Big|\lesssim\mathcal{R}_{l,N}(f),

(8.29)

where
l,N\in\mathbb{N}
are large enough.
Combining (
8.28
), (
8.29
), and the arbitrariness of
M
,
we find that,
for every
\vec{M}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\Big(\prod_{\nu=1}^{k}|\xi_{\nu}|^{M_{\nu}}\Big)\Big|\partial^{\gamma}\widehat{f}(\xi)\Big|\leq\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\sup_{\nu\in[k]}|\xi_{\nu}|^{kM_{\nu}}\Big|\partial^{\gamma}\widehat{f}(\xi)\Big|\lesssim\mathcal{R}_{l,N}(f),

where
l,N\in\mathbb{N}
are large enough.
This finishes the proof of Lemma
8.14
.
∎

Lemma 8.15
.

Let
\vec{u}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
and
a\in\dot{S}_{1,1}^{\vec{u}}
,
and let
a(x,D)
be a multi-parameter pseudo-differential operator as defined in
Definition
8.13
. Then
a(x,D)
is continuous from

\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
to
\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.
Consequently, its formal adjoint
a(x,D)^{\#}
, defined by setting,
for every
f\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
and
\phi\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,

\Big\langle a(x,D)^{\#}f,\phi\Big\rangle:=\langle f,a(x,D)\phi\rangle,

is a continuous linear operator from
\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
to
\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

Proof.

Let
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
and
\Delta_{\xi}
be the Laplace operator in the
variable
\xi
. Using the fact that

(I-\Delta_{\xi})^{N}e^{ix\cdot\xi}=(1+|x|^{2})^{N}e^{ix\cdot\xi}

and integration by parts, we conclude that, for
every
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{\vec{n}}
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
, and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
,

\displaystyle\partial^{\gamma}a(x,D)f=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}e^{ix\cdot\xi}\frac{(I-\Delta_{\xi})^{N}}{(1+|x|^{2})^{N}}\Big[\partial^{\gamma}_{x}a(x,\xi)\widehat{f}(\xi)\Big]\,d\xi.

Applying Leibniz’s rule, Definition
8.12
, and Lemma
8.14
,
we find that, for every
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{\vec{n}}
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
, and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
,

\displaystyle\Big|(1+|x|^{2})^{N}\partial^{\gamma}a(x,D)f\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\sum_{|\beta|\leq 2N}\Big|\partial^{\gamma}_{x}\partial^{\beta}_{\xi}a(x,\xi)\Big|\sum_{|\beta|\leq 2N}\Big|\partial^{\beta}_{\xi}\widehat{f}(\xi)\Big|\,d\xi

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\sum_{|\beta|\leq 2N}\prod_{\nu=1}^{k}|\xi_{\nu}|^{u_{\nu}+|\gamma_{\nu}|-|\beta_{\nu}|}\sum_{|\beta|\leq 2N}\Big|\partial^{\beta}_{\xi}\widehat{f}(\xi)\Big|\,d\xi\lesssim\mathcal{R}_{l,N}(f),

where
l,N\in\mathbb{N}
are large enough.
This shows
a(x,D)f\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
and

a(x,D)
is continuous. This finishes the proof of Lemma
8.15
.
∎

Applying an argument similar to that used in the proof of

[
136
, Theorem 1.5]
(see also the proofs of
[
77
, Theorems 1.1 and 1.2]
),
we obtain the following theorem.
For the convenience of the reader, we give the details of its proof here.

Theorem 8.16
.

Let
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
,
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,

r:=r(\pi,\vec{p},\vec{q})\wedge 1
,

\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be admissible for
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
,
and
V
be a matrix weight such that
(\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi,V)
belongs
to one of the main cases (Definition
7.3
).
Assume that
\vec{L}:=\lfloor\frac{\vec{n}}{r}-\vec{n}-\vec{s}\rfloor
.
Let
\vec{u}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
,
a\in\dot{S}_{1,1}^{\vec{u}}
,
and
a(x,D)
be a multi-parameter pseudo-differential operator with symbol
a
.
Assume that its formal adjoint
a(x,D)^{\#}
has the property,
for every
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{\vec{n}}
satisfying
|\gamma|_{\rm{vec}}\leq\vec{L}
, that

a(x,D)^{\#}(x^{\gamma})=0\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}).

(8.30)

Then
a(x,D)
can be extended to a continuous linear mapping
from
\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s}+\vec{u},\tau}(V)
to
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
.

Proof.

To simplify the presentation,
in this proof, we denote
a(x,D)
simply by
T
.
Let
f\in\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s}+\vec{u},\tau}(V)

and
(\varphi,\varphi)
be a Littlewood–Paley pair on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
.
By this and Lemma
5.3
, we find that

f=\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\langle f,\varphi_{Q}\rangle\varphi_{Q}

in
\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, and hence it is natural to define

\widetilde{T}(f):=\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\langle f,\varphi_{Q}\rangle T(\varphi_{Q})=\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big[2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}_{Q}\cdot\vec{u}}\Big(S_{\varphi}f\Big)_{Q}\Big]\Big[2^{-(\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}_{Q}\cdot\vec{u})}T(\varphi_{Q})\Big]

(8.31)

in
\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
.
If
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
,
then Lemmas
5.3
and
8.15
imply that
the series in (
8.31
) converges in
\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
to
T(f)
.
Therefore,
\widetilde{T}
and
T
coincide on
\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
.
Now, we show that
\widetilde{T}(f)
is well defined when
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
.
From Corollary
6.20
, we deduce that

\Big\|\Big\{2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}_{Q}\cdot\vec{u}}\Big(S_{\varphi}f\Big)_{Q}\Big\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|_{\dot{a}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},\tau}(V)}=\Big\|S_{\varphi}f\Big\|_{\dot{a}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s}+\vec{u},\tau}(V)}\sim\|f\|_{\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s}+\vec{u},\tau}(V)}.

(8.32)

Using a straightforward multi-parameter extension of
[
77
, pp. 265–266]
,
we conclude that,
for every
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{\vec{n}}
,
\vec{N}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
, and
Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,

\displaystyle|\partial^{\gamma}(T\varphi_{Q})(x)|\lesssim\prod_{\nu=1}^{k}|Q_{\nu}|^{-\frac{u_{\nu}}{n_{\nu}}-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{|\gamma_{\nu}|}{n_{\nu}}}\Big[1+\ell(Q_{\nu})^{-1}|x_{\nu}-c_{Q_{\nu}}|\Big]^{-N_{\nu}}.

(8.33)

Now, we verify the vanishing moment of
T(\varphi_{Q})
.
From (
8.30
), we infer that, for every
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{\vec{n}}

satisfying
|\gamma|_{\rm{vec}}\leq\vec{L}
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}x^{\gamma}T(\varphi_{Q})(x)\,dx=\Big\langle T^{\#}(x^{\gamma}),\varphi_{Q}\Big\rangle=0.

This, together with (
8.33
), further implies that

2^{-(\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}_{Q}\cdot\vec{u})}T(\varphi_{Q})
is a constant multiple of
a
(\vec{K},\vec{L},\vec{M},\vec{N})
-molecule on
Q
for all
\vec{K},\vec{M},\vec{N}

and hence a constant multiple of an
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}
-synthesis molecule (Definition
8.6
).
From this and Theorem
8.9
(
ii
), we deduce that
the series in (
8.31
) is convergent in
\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
.
Using Theorem
8.9
(
ii
) again, we conclude that

\begin{split}\|T(f)\|_{\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},\tau}(V)}&\lesssim\Big\|\Big\{2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}_{Q}\cdot\vec{u}}\Big(S_{\varphi}f\Big)_{Q}\Big\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|_{\dot{a}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},\tau}(V)}\\
&\sim\|f\|_{\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s}+\vec{u},\tau}(V)}\quad\text{by \eqref{102}}.\end{split}

This finishes the proof of Theorem
8.16
.
∎

Remark 8.17
.

(i)

Theorem
8.16
is new in the multi-parameter case. In the one-parameter case, it essentially agrees with
[
14
, Theorem 3.23]
with a slightly more restrictive range of indices for the usual Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces with
\tau=0
and more generally whenever the parameters are such that
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}
spaces defined by open sets agree with the
\dot{A}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}
spaces defined by cubes (See Theorem
9.12
); otherwise, it deals with a different scale of spaces.

(ii)

In
[
156
]
, Xu established the boundedness of
pseudo-differential operators on inhomogeneous bi-parameter
Besov–Triebel–Lizorkin spaces for a different symbol class.

8.3.
Wavelet characterisations

In this section, we establish wavelet characterisation of

\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
using either the Meyer wavelet or the Daubechies wavelet.
Let us first recall the wavelet constructed by Lemarié and Meyer
(see, for instance,
[
105
, Theorem 2]
).

Lemma 8.18
.

There exists a sequence
\{\theta^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{2^{n}-1}\subset\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

of real-valued basic wavelets such that

\Big\{\theta^{(i)}_{Q}:\ i\in[2^{n}-1],\ Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\Big\}

forms an orthonormal basis of
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

Here and below, for any
\vec{a}:=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{k})\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}_{+}
,
let
[\vec{a}]:=[a_{1}]\times\cdots\times[a_{k}]
.
For every
\nu\in[k]
,
there exist Meyer wavelets

\{\theta^{(i_{\nu})}\}_{i_{\nu}=1}^{2^{n_{\nu}}-1}
on
\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}
.
For every
\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]

and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
, let

\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}(x):=\prod_{\nu=1}^{k}\theta^{(i_{\nu})}(x_{\nu})

be the
Meyer wavelet on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
.

Remark 8.19
.

In Lemma
8.18
, for every
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,

f=\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\langle f,\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\Big\rangle\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}

in
\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})

(see the proof of
[
64
, Theorem 7.20]
for
k=1
and

[
71
, Proposition 6.2]
for
k=2
;
the same argument as that used in
[
71
, Proposition 6.2]
can extend to all
k\geq 3
with slight modifications).

Now, we establish the wavelet characterization of

\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)

via Meyer wavelets.

Theorem 8.20
.

Let
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
,
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,

\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be admissible for
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
,
and
V
be a matrix weight such that
(\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi,V)
belongs
to one of the main cases (Definition
7.3
).
Let
\{\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}
be Meyer wavelets on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
.
Then
f\in\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
if and only if
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
and

\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)_{\mathrm{w}}}:=\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\Big\|\Big\{\Big\langle f,\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\Big\rangle\Big\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}<\infty.

Moreover, for every
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
,

\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}\sim\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)_{\mathrm{w}}},

where the positive equivalence constants are independent of
f
.

Proof.

For any
\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]
, the estimate

\|\{\langle f,\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\rangle\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}

is immediate from Theorem
8.9
(
i
) and the fact that

\{\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
is a
constant multiple of a family of

\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}
-analysis molecules.
Thus,
\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)_{\mathrm{w}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}

follows from summing over all
\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]
.

Next, we show that, for every
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})

with
\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)_{\mathrm{w}}}<\infty
,

\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)_{\mathrm{w}}}.

(8.34)

For such
f
and every
\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]
,
from the definition of
\|\cdot\|_{\dot{A}_{p,q}^{s,\tau}(W)_{\mathrm{w}}}
,
it follows that

t^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}:=\Big\{t^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\Big\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}:=\Big\{\Big\langle f,\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\Big\rangle\Big\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\in\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V).

By Remark
8.19
, we obtain

\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}=\Big\|\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}t^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\Big\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}\lesssim\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\Big\|\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}t^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\Big\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}.

(8.35)

Since
\theta^{(i_{\nu})}\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}})
for all
\nu\in[k]
,
it follows that
\{\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
is a constant multiple of
a family of
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}
-synthesis molecules,
each on the cube indicated by its subscript.
From this, the fact that
t^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}\in\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
, Theorem
8.9
(ii),
and (
8.35
), we deduce that

\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}\lesssim\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\big\|t^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}\big\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}=\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)_{\mathrm{w}}},

which completes the proof of (
8.34
)
and hence Theorem
8.20
.
∎

Remark 8.21
.

Let
k=2
,
\vec{p}:=p\cdot\vec{1}
for some
p\in(0,\infty)
,

\vec{q}:=q\cdot\vec{1}
for some
q\in(0,\infty]
,

\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
\tau=0
,
m=1
, and
V\equiv 1
.
Further assume that
(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}=\ell^{q}L^{p}
(Besov case) or

(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}=L^{p}\ell^{q}
(Triebel–Lizorkin case).
Then Theorem
8.20
in this case coincides with
[
71
, Theorem 6.4]
.

In what follows, for any
\mathscr{N}\in\mathbb{N}
,
we use
C^{\mathscr{N}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
to denote the set of all

\mathscr{N}
times continuously differentiable functions on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Definition 8.22
.

Let
\mathscr{N}\in\mathbb{N}
. We say that
\{\theta^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{2^{n}-1}

are
Daubechies wavelets
of class
C^{\mathscr{N}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
if each

\theta^{(i)}\in C^{\mathscr{N}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is real-valued with compact support and if

\Big\{\theta^{(i)}_{Q}:\ i\in[2^{n}-1],\ Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\Big\}

is an orthonormal basis of
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

The following wavelet basis was constructed by Daubechies
(see, for instance,
[
41
]
).

Lemma 8.23
.

For any
\mathscr{N}\in\mathbb{N}
,
there exist Daubechies wavelets of class
C^{\mathscr{N}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

Remark 8.24
.

Let
\{\theta^{(i)}\}_{i\in[2^{n}-1]}
be Daubechies wavelets of class
C^{\mathscr{N}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.
By
[
42
, Corollary 5.5.2]
, we find that,
for any
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
with
|\alpha|\leq\mathscr{N}
,
the following cancellation conditions are satisfied:

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}x^{\alpha}\theta^{(i)}(x)\,dx=0.

For every
\vec{\mathscr{N}}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
and
\nu\in[k]
,
there exist Daubechies wavelets

\{\theta^{(i_{\nu})}\}_{i_{\nu}=1}^{2^{n_{\nu}}-1}
of class
C^{\mathscr{N}_{\nu}}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}})
.
For every
\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]

and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
, let

\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}(x):=\prod_{\nu=1}^{k}\theta^{(i_{\nu})}(x_{\nu})

be the Daubechies wavelet of class
C^{\vec{\mathscr{N}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.
Then, for every
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{\vec{n}}
with
|\alpha|_{\rm{vec}}\leq\vec{\mathscr{N}}
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}}x_{\nu}^{\alpha_{\nu}}\theta^{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(x_{\nu},x^{\prime})\,dx_{\nu}=0\quad\text{for a.e.}\quad x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\ominus\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}},

(8.36)

as required for the molecular cancellation condition (
8.3
).

Corollary 8.25
.

Let
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
,
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,

r:=r(\pi,\vec{p},\vec{q})\wedge 1
,

\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be admissible for
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
,
and
V
be a matrix weight such that
(\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi,V)
belongs
to one of the main cases (Definition
7.3
).
Let
\vec{\mathscr{N}}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
satisfy

\vec{\mathscr{N}}>(\vec{n}\tau+\vec{s}-\vec{1})\vee\Big(\frac{\vec{n}}{r}-\vec{n}-\vec{s}\Big)

(8.37)

and

\{\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}

be Daubechies wavelets of class

C^{\vec{\mathscr{N}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
. Then

(i)

For each
\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]
and
Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,
the wavelet
\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}
is a constant multiple of an

\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}
-analysis molecule on
Q
,
where the constant is independent of
Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.

(ii)

If
(\varphi,\psi)
is a Littlewood–Paley pair on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
,
then, for each
\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]
and
Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,

\Big\langle f,\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\Big\rangle:=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\langle f,\varphi_{R}\rangle\Big\langle\psi_{R},\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\Big\rangle

is well defined: the series converges absolutely
and its value is independent of the choice of
\varphi
and
\psi
.

Proof.

From the fact that
\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}
has compact support
and (
8.36
),
it follows that the wavelet
\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}
is a constant multiple of a
(\vec{K},\vec{\mathscr{N}},\vec{M},\vec{\mathscr{N}})
-molecule on
Q
(see Definition
8.1
)
for all
\vec{K},\vec{M}\in[0,\infty)^{k}
.
This, together with (
8.37
), further implies that

\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}
is a constant multiple of an
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}
-analysis molecule on
Q
(see Definition
8.6
).
This finishes the proof of (
i
).

By just proven (
i
) and Lemma
8.8
, we obtain (
ii
),
which completes the proof of Corollary
8.25
.
∎

Next, we can establish the wavelet decomposition of
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)

via Daubechies wavelets.

Theorem 8.26
.

Let
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
,
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,

\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be admissible for
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
,
and
V
be a matrix weight such that
(\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi,V)
belongs
to one of the main cases (Definition
7.3
).
Let
\vec{\mathscr{N}}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
satisfy (
8.37
) and

\{\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}

be Daubechies wavelets of class

C^{\vec{\mathscr{N}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.
Then, for any
f\in\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
,

f=\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\langle f,\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\Big\rangle\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}

(8.38)

in
\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
and

\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}\sim\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)_{\mathrm{w}}},

(8.39)

where the positive equivalence constants are independent of
f
.

Proof.

Let
(\varphi,\psi)
be a Littlewood–Paley pair on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
.
For any
\phi\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, we consider the series

\displaystyle S:=\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\sum_{Q,R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\langle f,\varphi_{R}\rangle\Big\langle\psi_{R},\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\Big\rangle\Big\langle\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q},\phi\Big\rangle.

(8.40)

Let
t:=\{t_{R}\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}:=\{\langle f,\varphi_{R}\rangle\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
.
By Corollary
6.20
, we find that
t\in\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
.
Moreover, both
\psi_{R}
and
\theta_{Q}^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}
are constant multiples of

\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}
-analysis
and
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}
-synthesis molecules
associated with the cubes specified by their subscripts.
In addition,
\phi\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
is
a constant multiple of an
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}
-synthesis
molecule. Consequently, the absolute convergence of (
8.40
)
follows directly from Corollary
8.5
(
ii
).

If we first sum over
Q
and
\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}
and use the fact that

\Big\{\theta_{Q}^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}:\ Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}),\ \vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]\Big\}

is an orthonormal basis of
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, we obtain

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\langle f,\varphi_{R}\rangle\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\langle\psi_{R},\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\Big\rangle\Big\langle\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q},\phi\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\langle f,\varphi_{R}\rangle\langle\psi_{R},\phi\rangle=\langle f,\phi\rangle,

where the last equality used the Calderón reproducing formula
Lemma
5.3
.
On the other hand, if we first sum over
R
,
then we recognise the definition of the pairing of
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)

with an
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}
-analysis molecule
\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}
to the result that

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle=\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big(\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\langle f,\varphi_{R}\rangle\Big\langle\psi_{R},\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\Big\rangle\Big)\Big\langle\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q},\phi\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\langle f,\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\Big\rangle\Big\langle\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q},\phi\Big\rangle.

A combination of the last two displays proves (
8.38
) in the sense of
convergence in
\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{C}^{m})
.

The estimate

\|\{\langle f,\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\rangle\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}

follows directly from Theorem
8.9
(
i
), together with the observation that

\{\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
are
constant multiples of

\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}
-analysis molecules.
Consequently, by summing over
\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]
, we obtain

\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)_{\mathrm{w}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}
.

To establish the reverse inequality, we note that
\{\langle f,\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\rangle\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\in\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
. Since

\{\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}

are constant multiples of
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}
-synthesis molecules,
Theorem
8.9
(
ii
) guarantees that the series

f^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}:=\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\langle f,\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\rangle\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}

converges in
\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
and satisfies

\big\|f^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}\big\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}\lesssim\Big\|\Big\{\Big\langle f,\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\Big\rangle\Big\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}.

Using (
8.38
), we conclude that
f=\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}f^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}
, which further implies that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\big\|f^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}\big\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\Big\|\Big\{\Big\langle f,\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{Q}\Big\rangle\Big\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}=\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)_{\mathrm{w}}}

by the quasi-triangle inequality in
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
.
This finishes the proof of (
8.39
) and hence Theorem
8.26
.
∎

Remark 8.27
.

In the multi-parameter context, Theorems
8.20
and
8.26
are completely new.
In the one-parameter case, for the usual Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin space with
\tau=0
, and more generally whenever the parameters are such that the definitions of the relevant space with open sets and dyadic cubes coincide (see Theorem
9.12
), Theorems
8.20
and Theorem
8.26
coincide with
[
14
, Theorems 4.3 and 4.10]
with a slightly more restrictive range of
\vec{\mathscr{N}}
. In the remaining one-parameter cases, Theorems
8.20
and
8.26
deal with a slightly different scale of spaces from those in
[
14
]
, as detailed in Theorem
9.12
.

9.
Relationship with other matrix-weighted function spaces

In this section, we show that the spaces
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
contain
the matrix-weighted versions of various classical function spaces, including Lebesgue spaces (Subsection
9.1
),
Sobolev spaces (Subsection
9.2
),
and BMO spaces (Subsection
9.3
).
We also study the relationship between

\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
and a variant obtained by replacing the supremum over open sets in the definition by one over dyadic rectangles only (Subsections
9.4
and
9.5
for
k=1

and
k\geq 2
, respectively).

9.1.
Matrix-weighted
L^{p}
spaces

They are certainly the most important examples of matrix-weighted function spaces, going back to works of Nazarov, Treil, and Volberg
[
122
,
145
,
150
]
or even Wiener and Masani
[
155
]
for
p=2
. Hence, it is relevant to know that these spaces are also covered as a special case of our theory. In the one-parameter case, the following result is due to
[
67
, Theorem 4.1]
. (Variant with a Triebel–Lizorkin-type norm, but involving Haar functions, already appear in
[
122
,
150
]
and more recently in
[
90
]
.) We provide the multi-parameter extension.

Theorem 9.1
.

For
p\in(1,\infty)
and
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, we have

\dot{F}^{0,0}_{p,2}(V)=L^{p}(V)

in the following precise sense:

(i)

Every
f\in L^{p}(V)
defines in the usual way a distribution
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, and this satisfies

\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{0,0}_{p,2}(V)}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{p}(V)}.

(ii)

For every
f\in\dot{F}^{0,0}_{p,2}(V)\subset\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, there is a unique function
F\in L^{p}(V)
that agrees with
f
as an element of
\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, and

\|F\|_{L^{p}(V)}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{0,0}_{p,2}(V)}.

Here, all the implicit positive constants are independent of
f
.

Proof of Theorem
9.1
, part (
i
).

Let us first consider
k=1
. In this case, the result is due to
[
67
, Theorem 4.1]
, but we indicate a slight variant of the argument that readily extends to several parameters. Fix a Littlewood–Paley function
\varphi
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For finite subsets
\mathscr{F}\subset\mathbb{Z}
and signs
\varepsilon_{i}=\pm 1
, consider the operator

T:f\mapsto\sum_{i\in\mathscr{F}}\varepsilon_{i}\varphi_{i}*f.

(9.1)

It is well known that
T
is a convolution-type Calderón–Zygmund operator, whose kernel
K
satisfies the standard estimates

|K(x)|\lesssim\frac{1}{|x|^{n}},\qquad|\nabla K(x)|\lesssim\frac{1}{|x|^{n+1}}

uniform with respect to the choice of
\mathscr{F}
and
\varepsilon_{i}
. Thus, from the well-known boundedness of such operators on
L^{p}(V)
(see
[
73
, Theorem 5.1]
), it follows that

\Big\|\sum_{i\in\mathscr{F}}\varepsilon_{i}\varphi_{i}*f\Big\|_{L^{p}(V)}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{p}(V)}

with the said uniformity. Thus, taking the expectation
\mathbb{E}
over a random choice of
\varepsilon_{i}=\pm 1

(see, for instance,
[
84
, p. 73]
for its definition), it follows that

\|f\|_{L^{p}(V)}^{p}\gtrsim\mathbb{E}\Big\|\sum_{i\in\mathscr{F}}\varepsilon_{i}\varphi_{i}*f\Big\|_{L^{p}(V)}^{p}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathbb{E}\Big|\sum_{i\in\mathscr{F}}\varepsilon_{i}V(x)\varphi_{i}*f(x)\Big|^{p}dx,

and by Khintchine’s inequality
[
75
, Sections C.2–C.3]
,

\mathbb{E}\Big|\sum_{i\in\mathscr{F}}\varepsilon_{i}V(x)\varphi_{i}*f(x)\Big|^{p}\sim\Big[\sum_{i\in\mathscr{F}}|V(x)\varphi_{i}*f(x)|^{2}\Big]^{\frac{p}{2}},

where the positive equivalence constants depend only on
p
.
Thus,

\Big\|\Big[\sum_{i\in\mathscr{F}}|V(\varphi_{i}*f)|^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{p}(V)}

for any finite
\mathscr{F}\subset\mathbb{Z}
, and then by monotone convergence also for
\mathscr{F}=\mathbb{Z}
. The left-hand side is recognised as
\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{0,0}_{p,2}(V)}
, and this completes the proof of (
i
) for
k=1
.

In the case of arbitrarily many parameters, we consider the versions of the Littlewood–Paley operators (
9.1
) over each subspace
\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}
. Let us denote the corresponding objects with superscript
(\nu)
for each
\nu\in[k]
.

Recall from Lemma
2.11
that
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
implies that
x_{\nu}\mapsto V(x^{\prime}\oplus x_{\nu})\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}})
for each
\nu\in[k]
, uniformly in
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\ominus\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}
. Thus, by the one-parameter case,

\displaystyle\|T^{(\nu)}f\|_{L^{p}(V)}^{p}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\ominus\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}}\|T^{(\nu)}f(x^{\prime}\oplus\cdot)\|_{L^{p}(V(x^{\prime}\oplus\ \cdot))}^{p}\,dx^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\ominus\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}}\|f(x^{\prime}\oplus\cdot)\|_{L^{p}(V(x^{\prime}\oplus\ \cdot))}^{p}\,dx^{\prime}=\|f\|_{L^{p}(V)}^{p}.

Using this repeatedly for every
\nu\in[k]
, we obtain

\Bigg\|\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathscr{F}}\varepsilon^{\otimes}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*f\Bigg\|_{L^{p}(V)}=\Bigg\|\Bigg(\prod_{\nu=1}^{k}T^{(\nu)}\Bigg)f\Bigg\|_{L^{p}(V)}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{p}(V)},

where
\mathscr{F}=\mathscr{F}^{(1)}\times\cdots\times\mathscr{F}^{(k)}
and
\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(x):=\prod_{\nu=1}^{k}\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}_{\nu}}^{(\nu)}(x_{\nu})
is the usual product Littlewood–Paley function, and
\varepsilon^{\otimes}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}:=\prod_{\nu=1}^{k}\varepsilon^{(\nu)}_{i_{\nu}}
. Averaging over independent
\varepsilon^{(\nu)}_{i_{\nu}}=\pm 1
as for
k=1
, we arrive at

\begin{split}\|f\|_{L^{p}(V)}&\gtrsim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg|\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathscr{F}}\varepsilon^{\otimes}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}V(x)\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*f(x)\Bigg|^{p}dx\\
&\sim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}\Bigg[\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathscr{F}}|V(x)\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*f(x)|^{2}\Bigg]^{\frac{p}{2}}dx,\end{split}

where the last step used Khintchine’s inequality for multiple random sums
[
75
, Section C.5]
. Applying monotone convergence to
\mathscr{F}\uparrow\mathbb{Z}^{k}
, we complete the proof of (
i
) in the general case.
∎

As a preparation for the proof of part (
ii
) of Theorem
9.1
, we provide the following density result. As it might have other applications, we state it in a form that is more general than our immediate needs.

Proposition 9.2
.

Let
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
p\in(0,\infty)
,
\vec{p}=p\cdot\vec{1}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be an admissible permutation of
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
, and
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.
Let
(\varphi,\psi)
be a Littlewood–Paley pair on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
.
For each finite subset
\mathscr{F}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{k}
and
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, let

P_{\mathscr{F}}f:=\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathscr{F}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f.

If
\mathscr{F}_{N}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{k}
is an increasing sequence of finite subsets with
\bigcup_{N=1}^{\infty}\mathscr{F}_{N}=\mathbb{Z}^{k}

and if
f\in\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},0}(V)
, then
\|f-P_{\mathscr{F}_{N}}f\|_{\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},0}(V)}\to 0
as
N\to\infty
.

This generalises
[
67
, Lemma 4.5]
, even in the one-parameter case, where their result is formulated for
p\in(1,\infty)
and
\mathscr{F}_{N}=[-N,N]\cap\mathbb{Z}
. For such
\mathscr{F}_{N}
, there are just two boundary terms, and the
\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},0}(V)
quasi-norms of single-term sequences reduce to
L^{p}(V)
norms, for which
p\in(1,\infty)
gives access to a version of Young’s convolution inequality
[
67
, Lemma 4.4]
.
In our setting, we need to replace these points by other tools, although other aspects of our proof are similar to those of
[
67
, Lemma 4.5]
.

Proof of Proposition
9.2
.

The sum defining
P_{\mathscr{F}}f
is finite and hence clearly well defined. In place of
\|\ \|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}
, we will consider the equivalent (by Corollary
6.5
) quasi-norm
\|\ \|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}([V]_{p})}
defined with the reducing operators.

To estimate
\|f-P_{\mathscr{F}}f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}([V]_{p})}
we need to compute
\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*(f-P_{\mathscr{F}}f)
. Let

\mathscr{F}^{-}:=\{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}:\ \vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}+\vec{u}\in\mathscr{F}\text{ for all }\vec{u}\in\{-1,0,1\}^{k}\}.

Then
\mathscr{F}^{-}\subset\mathscr{F}
for each
\mathscr{F}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{k}
, and the new sequence
\mathscr{F}_{N}^{-}
is also increasing with
\bigcup_{N=1}^{\infty}\mathscr{F}_{N}^{-}=\mathbb{Z}^{k}
.

On the Fourier transform side, we see that

[\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*(f-P_{\mathscr{F}}f)]^{\wedge}=\widehat{\varphi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}\Big(1-\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathscr{F}}\overline{\widehat{\varphi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\widehat{\psi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Big)\widehat{f},

where the supports of
\widehat{\varphi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}
and
\overline{\widehat{\varphi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\widehat{\psi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
meet only if
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}-\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\{-1,0,1\}^{k}
.

If
\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathscr{F}^{-}
, then the sum above is identically
1
on the support of
\widehat{\varphi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}
. It follows that

\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*(f-P_{\mathscr{F}}f)=\begin{cases}0,&\text{if }\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathscr{F}^{-},\\
\displaystyle\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*f-\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\vu\in\{-1,0,1\}^k}{\vi+\vu\in\Fs}}\chi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{u}}*\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*f,&\text{else},\end{cases}

where
\chi:=\widetilde{\varphi}*\psi
.
Hence

\begin{split}\|f-P_{\mathscr{F}}f\|_{\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},0}([V]_{p})}&=\|\{[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}},p}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*(f-P_{\mathscr{F}}f))\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},0}}\\
&\lesssim\|\{[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}},p}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*f)\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}\setminus\mathscr{F}^{-}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},0}}\\
&\quad+\sum_{\vec{u}\in\{-1,0,1\}^{k}}\Big\|\Big\{[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}},p}(\chi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{u}}*\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*f)\Big\}_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\vi\in\Z^k\setminus\Fs^-}{\vi+\vu\in\Fs}}\Big\|_{\dot{\alpha}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},0}}.\end{split}

(9.2)

Let us consider the first term on the right-hand side of (
9.2
),

\|\{[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}},p}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*f)\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}\setminus\mathscr{F}^{-}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},0}}=\|\{2^{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}},p}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*f)\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}\setminus\mathscr{F}^{-}}\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}.

We write the iterated
(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}
as

(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}=(L^{\vec{p}_{\operatorname{out}}}\ell^{\vec{q}_{\operatorname{out}}})_{\pi_{\operatorname{out}}}\ell^{q_{\nu}}(L^{\vec{p}_{\operatorname{in}}}),

identifying the innermost instance of an
\ell^{q_{\nu}}
space in this iteration. Note that either
\vec{p}_{\operatorname{out}}
or
\vec{p}_{\operatorname{in}}
may be just an empty string, in which case it is simply omitted above.
Let us write
\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}=\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}_{\operatorname{out}}\oplus i_{\nu}
with

\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}_{\operatorname{out}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k-1}

and
x=x_{\operatorname{out}}\oplus x_{\operatorname{in}}
, where
x_{\operatorname{in}}
is the variable (if any) of the
L^{\vec{p}_{\operatorname{in}}}

space inside the last
\ell^{q_{\nu}}
, and
x_{\operatorname{out}}
consist of the remaining variables. Then the
(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}
quasi-norm above is the

(L^{\vec{p}_{\operatorname{out}}}\ell^{\vec{q}_{\operatorname{out}}})_{\pi_{\operatorname{out}}}
quasi-norm of

\Bigg(\sum_{i_{\nu}\in\mathbb{Z}:i_{\nu}\oplus\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}_{\operatorname{out}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}\setminus\mathscr{F}^{-}}\|2^{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}},p}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*f)(x_{\operatorname{out}},\cdot)\|_{L^{\vec{p}_{\operatorname{in}}}}^{q_{\nu}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{q_{\nu}}}.

(9.3)

This is the tail of the infinite convergent series (at least for almost every
x_{\operatorname{out}}
) with summation simply over all
i_{\nu}\in\mathbb{Z}
. Such a tail converges to
0

as
\mathscr{F}^{-}=\mathscr{F}_{N}^{-}\uparrow\mathbb{Z}^{k}
. By the finiteness of the quasi-norm of the full series, and the assumption that all exponents are finite, it then follows from dominated convergence that also the
(L^{\vec{p}_{\operatorname{out}}}\ell^{\vec{q}_{\operatorname{out}}})_{\pi_{\operatorname{out}}}
quasi-norm of (
9.3
) converges to zero as
N\to\infty
, i.e.,

\|\{[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}},p}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*f)\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}\setminus\mathscr{F}_{N}^{-}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},0}}\to 0\quad\text{as}\quad N\to\infty.

This handles the first term on the right-hand side of (
9.2
), and it remains to control the second one. (A careful reader will have observed that the proof so far made no use of the particular properties of the weight
V
, and we could have equally well used just
V
in place of
[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}},p}
; the reducing operators will play a role in the remainder of the proof.)

First we observe that each
\ell^{q_{\nu}}
quasi-norm, and hence also the
\dot{\alpha}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},0}
quasi-norm, is monotone with respect to truncations of the sequence inside the quasi-norm, and hence we may simply estimate up by dropping the condition
\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{u}\in\mathscr{F}
. Hence, for each of the finitely many
\vec{u}\in\{-1,0,1\}^{k}
, we need to estimate

\|\{[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}},p}(\chi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{u}}*\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*f)\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}\setminus\mathscr{F}^{-}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},0}}.

Note further that
\chi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{u}}=(\chi_{\vec{u}})_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}
are usual dilations of the finitely many test functions
\chi_{\vec{u}}\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
with
\vec{u}\in\{-1,0,1\}^{k}
. By Lemma
5.8
, applied to
V_{R}f:=[V]_{\textit{\L}^{p}(R)}f
in place of
f
, and
\chi_{\vec{u}}
in place of
\chi
, we obtain for
x\in R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})

\begin{split}|V_{R}(\chi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{u}}*\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*f)(x)|^{r}&\lesssim(\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*|V_{R}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*f)|^{r})(x)\\
&\leq\sum_{P\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\int_{P}\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(x-y)|V_{R}V_{P}^{-1}|^{r}|V_{P}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*f)(y)|^{r}dy\\
&\lesssim\sum_{P\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\int_{P}\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}^{\prime}(x-y)|V_{P}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*f)(y)|^{r}dy\\
&=(\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*|[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}},p}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*f)|^{r})(x),\end{split}

where the last estimate was an application of Lemma
2.22
, resulting in a new function
\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}^{\prime}
in place of
\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}
, still with as good decay as we want.

Taking quasi-norms of both sides it follows that

\begin{split}\|\{[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}},p}(\chi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}+\vec{u}}*\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*f)\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}\setminus\mathscr{F}^{-}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},0}}&\lesssim\|\{(\phi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*|[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}},p}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*f)|^{r})^{\frac{1}{r}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}\setminus\mathscr{F}^{-}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},0}}\\
&\lesssim\|\{[V]_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}},p}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}}*f)\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}\setminus\mathscr{F}^{-}}\|_{\dot{\alpha}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},0}},\end{split}

where the last estimate was an application of Lemma
6.7
, valid provided that we choose
r\in(0,\min\{\vec{p},\vec{q}\})
, as we can.

Thus, we estimated the second term on the right-hand side of (
9.2
) by the first term, and we already checked that the first term tends to
0
for
\mathscr{F}=\mathscr{F}_{N}
as
N\to\infty
.
This finishes the proof of Proposition
9.2
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
9.1
, part (
ii
).

Let
f\in\dot{F}^{0,0}_{p,2}(V)\subset\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, and let
P_{\mathscr{F}}f
be as in Proposition
9.2
.
By the well-known properties of convolutions of distributions and test functions, each
\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f
is a smooth function of polynomially bounded growth. Hence, for any compactly supported
g\in L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, the following expressions are well defined, and the identity can be justified by Fubini’s theorem:

\langle P_{\mathscr{F}}f,g\rangle=\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathscr{F}}\langle\widetilde{\varphi}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f,g\rangle=\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathscr{F}}\langle\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f,\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*g\rangle.

On the right, each object on both sides of the pairing is a proper function, and the pairing agrees with the usual integral pairing. Hence we may insert the weight
V
into this pairing by

\langle\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f,\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*g\rangle=\langle V(\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f),V^{-1}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*g)\rangle,

and then

|\langle P_{\mathscr{F}}f,g\rangle|\leq\Bigg\|\Bigg[\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathscr{F}}|V(\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f)|^{2}\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg\|_{L^{p}}\Bigg\|\Bigg[\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathscr{F}}|V^{-1}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*g)|^{2}\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}},

where

\Bigg\|\Bigg[\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathscr{F}}|V^{-1}(\varphi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*g)|^{2}\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}}\leq\|g\|_{\dot{F}^{0,0}_{p^{\prime},2}(V^{-1})}\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(V^{-1})}

by part (
i
) of the theorem, which we already proved, with
p^{\prime}
and
V^{-1}\in\mathscr{A}_{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
in place of
p
and
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, and
g\in L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})\subset L^{p^{\prime}}(V^{-1})
in place of
f
.

Since
P_{\mathscr{F}}f
is a proper function, and
L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
is dense in
L^{p^{\prime}}(V^{-1})=(L^{p}(V))^{*}
, the previous estimates imply that

\|P_{\mathscr{F}}f\|_{L^{p}(V)}\lesssim\Bigg\|\Bigg[\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathscr{F}}|V(\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f)|^{2}\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg\|_{L^{p}}.

(9.4)

Let us then consider an increasing sequence of finite
\mathscr{F}_{N}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{k}
with
\bigcup_{N=1}^{\infty}\mathscr{F}_{N}=\mathbb{Z}^{k}
, as in Proposition
9.2
. Applying (
9.4
) to
\mathscr{F}=\mathscr{F}_{N}\setminus\mathscr{F}_{M}
for
M<N
, and noting that
P_{\mathscr{F}}=P_{\mathscr{F}_{N}}-P_{\mathscr{F}_{M}}
in this case, we obtain

\displaystyle\|P_{\mathscr{F}_{N}}f-P_{\mathscr{F}_{M}}f\|_{L^{p}(V)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigg\|\Bigg[\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathscr{F}_{N}\setminus\mathscr{F}_{M}}|V(\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f)|^{2}\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg\|\Bigg[\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}\setminus\mathscr{F}_{M}}|V(\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f)|^{2}\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg\|_{L^{p}}\to 0

as
M\to\infty
by dominated convergence, since

\Bigg\|\Bigg[\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}|V(\psi_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}*f)|^{2}\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg\|_{L^{p}}=\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{0,0}_{p,2}(V)}<\infty.

Hence
P_{\mathscr{F}_{N}}f
is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{p}(V)
, and thus converges to some
F\in L^{p}(V)
. On the other hand, Proposition
9.2
shows that
P_{\mathscr{F}_{N}}f\to f
in
\dot{F}^{0,0}_{p,2}(V)
, and thus in
\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
by Corollary
6.18
. For
h\in L^{p}(V)
and
\phi\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,

|\langle h,\phi\rangle|=|\langle Vh,V^{-1}h\rangle|\leq\|h\|_{L^{p}(V)}\|\phi\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(V^{-1})},

and the finiteness of the second term is easy to check; hence convergence in
L^{p}(V)
also implies convergence in
\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
.
Now
F=f
in the sense of
\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
follows from the uniqueness of the limit in this space.
∎

9.2.
Matrix-weighted Sobolev spaces

Another important class of matrix-weighted extensions of classical function spaces, these have been previously identified with Triebel–Lizorkin spaces in the one-parameter setting by Frazier and Roudenko
[
67
, Proposition 1.4]
. As usual, we provide a multi-parameter extension. While
[
67
, Proposition 1.4]
deals with non-homogeneous Sobolev spaces (involving
L^{p}
norms of both a function and its derivatives), we prove a result for homogeneous Sobolev spaces, which may be a new statement (although an easy variant of
[
67
, Proposition 1.4]
) also in the one-parameter case.

Definition 9.3
.

Let
\vec{l}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
,
p\in(0,\infty)
,
and
V\in L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m\times m})
.
The
homogeneous matrix-weighted Sobolev space

\dot{W}^{\vec{l}}_{p}(V)

consists of all
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
such that
the distributional derivatives
\partial^{\alpha}f

are functions belonging to
L^{p}(V)

for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{\vec{n}}
with
|\alpha|_{\rm{vec}}=\vec{l}
.
We equip this set with the quasi-norm

\|f\|_{\dot{W}^{\vec{l}}_{p}(V)}:=\sum_{|\alpha|_{\rm{vec}}=\vec{l}}\|\partial^{\alpha}f\|_{L^{p}(V)}.

(9.5)

Theorem 9.4
.

For
\vec{l}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
,
p\in(1,\infty)
, and
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,
we have
\dot{F}^{\vec{l},0}_{p,2}(V)=\dot{W}^{\vec{l}}_{p}(V)
.
Moreover, for every
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
,

\|f\|_{\dot{W}^{\vec{l}}_{p}(V)}\sim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{\vec{l},0}_{p,2}(V)},

where the positive equivalence constants are independent of
f
.

To prove Theorem
9.4
, we need the following equivalent characterisation of
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
in terms of derivatives.

Proposition 9.5
.

Let
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
,
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,

\tau\in[0,\infty)
,
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be admissible for
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
, and
V
be a matrix weight such that
(\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi,V)
belongs to one of the main cases (Definition
7.3
).
Assume that
\vec{l}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
and
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
.
Then
f\in\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
if and only if

\partial^{\alpha}f\in\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s}-\vec{l},\tau}(V)

for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{\vec{n}}
with
|\alpha|_{\rm{vec}}=\vec{l}
.
Moreover, for every
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
,

\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}\sim\sum_{|\alpha|_{\rm{vec}}=\vec{l}}\|\partial^{\alpha}f\|_{\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s}-\vec{l},\tau}(V)},

where the positive equivalence constants are independent of
f
.

Proof.

We first prove that, for every
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{\vec{n}}
with
|\alpha|_{\rm{vec}}=\vec{l}
,

\|\partial^{\alpha}f\|_{\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s}-\vec{l},\tau}(V)}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}.

(9.6)

Let
(\varphi,\psi)
be a Littlewood–Paley pair on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
.
By Corollary
6.20
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\|\partial^{\alpha}f\|_{\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s}-\vec{l},\tau}(V)}\lesssim\|S_{\varphi}\partial^{\alpha}f\|_{\dot{a}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s}-\vec{l},\tau}(V)}.

Note that
\langle\partial^{\alpha}f,\varphi_{Q}\rangle=\langle f,(-\partial)^{\alpha}\varphi_{Q}\rangle.

Using Lemma
5.3
and the integration by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial^{\alpha}\varphi_{Q}

\displaystyle=\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\langle\partial^{\alpha}\varphi_{Q},\psi_{R}\rangle\psi_{R}=(-1)^{|\vec{l}|}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\langle\varphi_{Q},\partial^{\alpha}\psi_{R}\rangle\psi_{R}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\ell(Q_{i})^{-|\alpha_{i}|}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}b_{Q,R}\psi_{R}

with convergence in
\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, where

b_{Q,R}:=\prod_{i=1}^{k}\bigg[\frac{\ell(Q_{i})}{\ell(R_{i})}\bigg]^{|\alpha_{i}|}\langle\varphi_{Q},(\partial^{\alpha}\psi)_{R}\rangle.

Therefore,

\|S_{\varphi}\partial^{\alpha}f\|_{\dot{a}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s}-\vec{l},\tau}(V)}\leq\|B(S_{\psi}f)\|_{\dot{a}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},\tau}(V)}.

By the fact that
\varphi
is a Littlewood–Paley function on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}

and Parseval’s identity,
we conclude that
\langle\varphi_{Q},(\partial^{\alpha}\psi)_{R}\rangle\neq 0

if and only if
\frac{\ell(Q_{i})}{\ell(R_{i})}\in\{\frac{1}{2},1,2\}
for all
i\in[k]
.
This, together with the rapid decay of Schwartz pairings (Lemma
5.10
),
further implies that
B:=\{b_{Q,R}\}
is
(\vec{D},\vec{E},\vec{F})
-almost diagonal
for all
\vec{D},\vec{E},\vec{F}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
and hence

\|B(S_{\psi}f)\|_{\dot{a}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},\tau}(V)}\lesssim\|S_{\psi}f\|_{\dot{a}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},\tau}(V)}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s},\tau}(V)}.

This finishes the proof of (
9.6
).

Next, we show that, for every
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{\vec{n}}
with
|\alpha|_{\rm{vec}}=\vec{l}
,

\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}\lesssim\|\partial^{\alpha}f\|_{\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s}-\vec{l},\tau}(V)}.

(9.7)

Let
\Delta^{\vec{l}}:=\prod_{i\in[k]}\Delta_{i}^{l_{i}}
,
where
\Delta_{i}
is the Laplace operator on
\mathbb{R}^{n_{i}}
.
Note that

\Delta_{i}^{l_{i}}=\Bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n_{i}}\partial_{i,j}^{2}\Bigg)^{l_{i}}=\sum_{|\beta_{i}|=l_{i}}\binom{l_{i}}{\beta_{i}}\prod_{j=1}^{n_{i}}\partial_{i,j}^{2\beta_{i,j}}

and hence

\Delta^{\vec{l}}=\sum_{|\beta|_{\rm{vec}}=\vec{l}}\binom{\vec{l}}{\vec{\beta}}\partial^{2\beta}=C(-1)^{|\vec{l}|}\dot{I}_{-2\vec{l}}

in
\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,
where
\binom{\vec{l}}{\vec{\beta}}:=\prod_{i=1}^{k}\binom{l_{i}}{\beta_{i}}
,

C
is a positive constant,
and the lifting operator
\dot{I}_{-2|\alpha|_{\rm{vec}}}
is defined as in (
6.20
). Then

\begin{split}\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}&\sim\|\dot{I}_{-2\vec{l}}f\|_{\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s}-2\vec{l},\tau}(V)}\quad\text{by Corollary \ref{257V}}\\
&\sim\Big\|\Delta^{\vec{l}}f\Big\|_{\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s}-2\vec{l},\tau}(V)}\lesssim\sum_{|\alpha|_{\rm{vec}}=\vec{l}}\|\partial^{\alpha}(\partial^{\alpha}f)\|_{\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{(\vec{s}-\vec{l})-\vec{l},\tau}(V)}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{|\alpha|_{\rm{vec}}=\vec{l}}\|\partial^{\alpha}f\|_{\dot{A}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}^{\vec{s}-\vec{l},\tau}(V)}\quad\text{by \eqref{B<A}},\end{split}

where we used (
9.6
) with
\partial^{\alpha}f
in place of
f
and
\vec{s}-\vec{l}
in place of
\vec{s}
.
This finishes the proof of (
9.7
)
and hence Proposition
9.5
.
∎

Remark 9.6
.

Proposition
9.5
extends some previous results as follows.
For one-parameter Triebel–Lizorkin space, it coincides with

[
67
, Proposition 7.3]
in the matrix-weighted case,
and with
[
64
, (5.6)]
in the unweighted case.

Now, we prove Theorem
9.4
.

Proof of Theorem
9.4
.

Combining two previous results (as quoted below),
we find that, for every
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
,

\begin{split}\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{\vec{l},0}_{p,2}(V)}&\sim\sum_{|\alpha|_{\rm{vec}}=\vec{l}}\|\partial^{\alpha}f\|_{\dot{F}^{0,0}_{p,2}(V)}\quad\text{by Proposition \ref{partial derivative}}\\
&\sim\sum_{|\alpha|_{\rm{vec}}=\vec{l}}\|\partial^{\alpha}f\|_{L^{p}(V)}\quad\text{by Theorem \ref{Lp=F}}\\
&=\|f\|_{\dot{W}^{\vec{l}}_{p}(V)}\quad\text{by definition \eqref{def Sobo}}.\end{split}

This finishes the proof of Theorem
9.4
.
∎

9.3.
Multi-parameter BMO spaces

Introduced by John and Nirenberg
[
96
]
, the space BMO of functions of bounded mean oscillation has a rich history.
For the present, we are mainly concerned about multi-parameter version of this space introduced by Chang and R. Fefferman
[
25
]
. The following is a slight variant of one of the original characterisations of this space, which has been adopted as the definition in some subsequent works (e.g.
[
102
, (3.5)]
; we note that this part is independent of the error discovered in this paper
[
103
]
).

Definition 9.7

(cf.
[
102
]
, (3.5))
.

Let
f\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})

and
\{\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}
be the Meyer wavelets on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
.
Then
f\in\mathrm{BMO}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
if and only if

\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Bigg[\frac{1}{|\Omega|}\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\sum_{R\in\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|(f,\theta_{R}^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})})|^{2}\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}<\infty.

(9.8)

Based on this definition, we introduce the matrix-weighted version as follows:

Definition 9.8
.

Let
V\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m\times m})

and
\{\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}
be the Meyer wavelets on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
.
The
matrix-weighted bounded mean oscillation space
\mathrm{BMO}(V)

consists of all vector-valued functions
b\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
such that

\|b\|_{\mathrm{BMO}(V)}:=\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Bigg[\frac{1}{|\Omega|}\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\sum_{R\in\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\fint_{R}|V(x)(b,\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{R})|^{2}\,dx\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}<\infty.

Remark 9.9
.

From the definition of reducing operators, we immediately deduce the alternative formula

\|b\|_{\mathrm{BMO}(V)}\sim\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Bigg[\frac{1}{|\Omega|}\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\sum_{R\in\Omega_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|[V]_{\textit{\L}^{2}(R)}(b,\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{R})|^{2}\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}},

(9.9)

where the
\textit{\L}^{2}(R)
-reducing operator also has the explicit formula
[V]_{\textit{\L}^{2}(R)}=\langle V^{2}\rangle_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}

A version of the matrix-weighted multi-parameter BMO was also recently studied by Kakaroumpas and Soler i Gibert
[
101
]
. In their notation, the right-hand side of (
9.9
) is the norm of the sequence
\{(b,\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})})\}_{R\in\mathscr{D},\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}
in a space
\operatorname{BMO}_{\operatorname{prod},\mathscr{D}}(U,V,2)
, where
U\equiv I
is the identity-valued constant weight. Indeed,
[
101
]
define their multi-parameter BMO as a space of discrete sequences instead of functions, while they also discuss a more general two-weight situation. Thus, our setting has some points of contact with
[
101
]
, but largely goes to a different direction.

The following theorem shows that
\operatorname{BMO}(V)
is a special case of the space
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
.

Theorem 9.10
.

For
V\in\mathscr{A}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, we have

\dot{F}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}_{2,2}(V)=\mathrm{BMO}(V)

in the following sense:

(i)

Every
b\in\mathrm{BMO}(V)
defines a distribution
f\in\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, and this satisfies

\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}_{2,2}(V)}\sim\|b\|_{\mathrm{BMO}(V)}.

(ii)

For every
f\in\dot{F}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}_{2,2}(V)\subset\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, there is a function
b\in\mathrm{BMO}(V)
that agrees with
f
as an element of
\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, and

\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}_{2,2}(V)}\sim\|b\|_{\mathrm{BMO}(V)}.

Here, all the positive equivalence constants are independent of
b
and
f
.

Proof.

Comparing Definitions
6.1
and
9.8
, and Remark
9.9
, it is immediate that

\|b\|_{\operatorname{BMO}(V)}=\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\Big\|\{(b,\theta_{R}^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})})\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}}\Big\|_{\dot{f}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}_{2,2}(V)}\sim\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\Big\|\{(b,\theta_{R}^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})})\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}}\Big\|_{\dot{f}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}_{2,2}([V]_{2})}.

(9.10)

By Theorem
8.20
, we find that,
for every
b\in\mathrm{BMO}(V)
,

f:=\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\Big\langle b,\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{R}\Big\rangle\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{R}\in\dot{F}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}_{2,2}(V)

and
\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}_{2,2}(V)}\sim\|b\|_{\mathrm{BMO}(V)}
.
Conversely, for every
f\in\dot{F}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}_{2,2}(V)
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
, let

\begin{split}b(x)&:=\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\langle f,\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{R}\rangle\prod_{\nu=1}^{k}\Bigg[\theta^{(i_{\nu})}_{R_{\nu}}(x_{\nu})-\mathbf{1}_{\ell(R_{\nu})\geq 1}\sum_{\gamma_{\nu}\in\mathbb{N}^{n_{\nu}},\,|\gamma_{\nu}|\leq N}\frac{(\partial^{\gamma_{\nu}}\theta^{(i_{\nu})}_{R_{\nu}})(\mathbf{0})}{\gamma_{\nu}!}x_{\nu}^{\gamma_{\nu}}\Bigg]\\
&\phantom{:}=:\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\langle f,\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{R}\rangle\prod_{\nu=1}^{k}g_{R_{\nu}}(x_{\nu}).\end{split}

We aim to prove that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}|b(x)|\prod_{\nu=1}^{k}\frac{1}{(1+|x_{\nu}|)^{K}}\,dx<\infty

(9.11)

for some
K\in(n,\infty)
. To this end, we estimate

\langle g_{R_{\nu}},(1+|\cdot|)^{-K}\rangle

by consider two cases for
j_{\nu}:=-\log_{2}\ell(R_{\nu})
.

Case (1)

j_{\nu}>0
. In this case, the second term in
g_{R_{\gamma}}
vanishes.
Then, since
\theta^{(i_{\nu})}\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}})
, we find that, for every
M_{1}\in(\frac{3}{2}n,\infty)
,

|g_{R_{\nu}}(x_{\nu})|\lesssim 2^{j_{\nu}\frac{n_{\nu}}{2}}\frac{1}{(1+2^{j_{\nu}}|x_{\nu}-a_{R_{\nu}}|)^{M_{1}}}\sim 2^{-j_{\nu}n_{\nu}}\frac{2^{\frac{3}{2}j_{\nu}n_{\nu}}}{(1+2^{\frac{3}{2}j_{\nu}}|x_{\nu}-a_{R_{\nu}}|)^{\frac{2}{3}M_{1}}},

where the implicit positive constants depend only on
M_{1}
and
\theta^{(i_{\nu})}
.
Thus, for every
K\in(n,\infty)
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}}\frac{|g_{R_{\nu}}(x_{\nu})|}{(1+|x_{\nu}|)^{K}}\,dx_{\nu}\leq 2^{-j_{\nu}n_{\nu}}(1+|a_{R_{\nu}}|)^{-\min(\frac{2}{3}M_{1},K)}.

(9.12)

Case (2)

j_{\nu}\leq 0
. In this case,
from the mean value theorem and
\theta^{(i_{\nu})}\in\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}})
,
we infer that, for every
M_{2}\in(n,\infty)
,

\begin{split}|g_{R_{\nu}}(x_{\nu})|&\lesssim|x_{\nu}|^{N+1}\sup_{\gamma_{\nu}\in\mathbb{N}^{n_{\nu}},\,|\gamma_{\nu}|=N+1}\sup_{t\in(0,1)}\Big|\partial^{\gamma_{\nu}}\theta^{(i_{\nu})}_{R_{\nu}}(tx_{\nu})\Big|\\
&\lesssim|x_{\nu}|^{N+1}2^{j_{\nu}(\frac{n_{\nu}}{2}+N+1)}\sup_{t\in(0,1)}\frac{1}{(1+2^{j_{\nu}}|tx_{\nu}-a_{R_{\nu}}|)^{M_{2}}},\end{split}

where the implicit positive constants depend only on
M_{2}
,
N
, and
\theta^{(i_{\nu})}
.
Note that

\frac{1}{1+2^{j_{\nu}}|tx_{\nu}-a_{R_{\nu}}|}\leq\frac{1+(1-t)2^{j_{\nu}}|x_{\nu}|}{1+2^{j_{\nu}}|tx_{\nu}-a_{R_{\nu}}|+(1-t)2^{j_{\nu}}|x_{\nu}|}\leq\frac{1+|x_{\nu}|}{1+2^{j_{\nu}}|x_{\nu}-a_{R_{\nu}}|}

and hence

|g_{R_{\nu}}(x_{\nu})|\lesssim(1+|x_{\nu}|)^{N+M_{2}+1}2^{j_{\nu}(\frac{n_{\nu}}{2}+N+1)}\frac{1}{(1+2^{j_{\nu}}|x_{\nu}-a_{R_{\nu}}|)^{M_{2}}}.

Thus, for every
K\in(N+M_{2}+n+1,\infty)
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}}\frac{|g_{R_{\nu}}(x_{\nu})|}{(1+|x_{\nu}|)^{K}}\,dx_{\nu}\leq 2^{j_{\nu}(\frac{n_{\nu}}{2}+N+1)}(1+2^{j_{\nu}}|a_{R_{\nu}}|)^{-\min(M_{2},K-N-M_{2}-1)}.

(9.13)

Using (
9.12
) and (
9.13
), we obtain

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}|b(x)|\prod_{\nu=1}^{k}\frac{1}{(1+|x_{\nu}|)^{K}}\leq\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}\sum_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|\langle f,\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{R}\rangle|b_{[0,1)^{\vec{n}},R},

where
b_{[0,1)^{\vec{n}},R}:=\prod_{\nu=1}^{k}b_{[0,1)^{n_{\nu}},R_{\nu}}
with

b_{[0,1)^{n_{\nu}},R_{\nu}}:=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\ell(R_{\nu})^{-(\frac{n_{\nu}}{2}+N+1)}(1+\frac{|a_{R_{\nu}}|}{\ell(R_{\nu})})^{-\min(M_{2},K-N-M_{2}-1)},&\text{if }\ell(R_{\nu})\geq 1,\\
\ell(R_{\nu})^{n_{\nu}}(1+|a_{R_{\nu}}|)^{-\min(\frac{2}{3}M_{1},K)},&\text{if }\ell(R_{\nu})<1.\end{cases}

Note that
t^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}:=\{t_{R}^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}:=|\langle f,\theta^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})}_{R}\rangle|\}_{R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\in\dot{f}^{2,\frac{1}{2}}_{2,2}(V)

and
B:=\{b_{P,R}:=b_{[0,1)^{\vec{n}},R}\mathbf{1}_{P=[0,1)^{\vec{n}}}\}_{P,R\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}

is
(\vec{D},\vec{E},\vec{F})
-almost diagonal, where

\begin{cases}\displaystyle D_{\nu}:=\min\Big(\frac{2}{3}M_{1},M_{2},K-N-M_{2}-1\Big),\\
\displaystyle E_{\nu}:=\frac{n_{\nu}}{2}+N+1,\quad\text{and}\quad F_{\nu}:=n_{\nu}.\end{cases}

By first fixing
M_{1},M_{2},N
large enough, and then
K
large enough, it is clear that can achieve

\vec{D}>\frac{3}{2}\vec{n},\quad\vec{E}>\vec{n},\quad\text{and}\quad\vec{F}>\frac{\vec{n}}{2}.

By Theorem
7.12
, this implies that
B
is bounded on
\dot{f}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}_{2,2}(V)
, and hence

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}|b(x)|\prod_{\nu=1}^{k}\frac{1}{(1+|x_{\nu}|)^{K}}\leq\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}}\in[2^{\vec{n}}-\vec{1}]}(Bt^{(\vec{\textit{\char 16\relax}})})_{[0,1)^{\vec{n}}}<\infty.

This finishes the proof of (
9.11
).

By (
9.11
), we find that
b
is a locally integrable function on
\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}

and
b\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.
Moreover, from the definition of
b
, we infer that

b=f
in
\mathscr{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.
Together with (
9.10
) and Theorem
8.20
, this implies that

\|b\|_{\mathrm{BMO}(V)}\sim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{2,\frac{1}{2}}_{2,2}(V)}<\infty
,
which completes the proof of Theorem
9.10
.
∎

9.4.
One-parameter Besov–Triebel–Lizorkin-type spaces

Generalising the classical Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces
\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}
,
\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}
with three indices, the notion of Besov-type and Triebel–Lizorkin-type spaces
\dot{B}^{s,\tau}_{p,q}
,
\dot{F}^{s,\tau}_{p,q}
incorporating the fourth Morrey index
\tau
was first introduced by Yang et al.
[
158
,
159
,
162
]
in the one-parameter setting of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Matrix-weighted versions of these spaces have been subsequently studied by Bu et al.
[
12
,
13
,
14
,
17
,
15
]
. We recall their definition with a slightly different notation to emphasise both similarities and differences with the spaces that we study in this work:

Definition 9.11
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,

p\in(0,\infty)
,
q\in(0,\infty]
,
\pi\in S_{[2]}
,
and
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.
The
homogeneous matrix-weighted Besov–Triebel–Lizorkin-type space associated with cubes

\dot{A}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)

is defined by setting

\dot{A}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V):=\big\{f\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{C}^{m}):\ \|f\|_{\dot{A}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)}<\infty\big\}

where

\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)}:=\sup_{P\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}|P|^{-\tau}\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{P}2^{js}V\varphi_{j}*f_{j}\Big\}_{j\geq j_{P}}\Big\|_{(L^{p}\ell^{q})_{\pi}}.

(9.14)

Note that
S_{[2]}
consists of just two permutations. We recall from Example
4.11
the notation for them suggesting their relationships to Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces, namely
F:=\operatorname{id}
and
B:=(1\ 2)
[the cycle notation for the unique non-trivial permutation of
\{1,2\}
with
B(1)=2
and
B(2)=1
].

A natural question is whether
the space
\dot{A}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)

coincides with the space
\dot{A}^{s,\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(V)
defined by taking the supremum in (
9.14
) over all open sets
\Omega
instead. This coincidence is obvious for
\tau=0
, in which case the supremum is reached in the limit of
\Omega\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
(or
P
approaching one of the quadrants that gives the largest norm); thus both cubic and open-set definitions are extensions of the classical Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces.

The following theorem provides a complete answer to this question:

Theorem 9.12
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,
p\in(0,\infty)
,

q\in(0,\infty]
,
\pi\in S_{[2]}
, and
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.
If
\tau\in(0,\frac{1}{p})
or if
\tau=\frac{1}{p}
,

q\in(0,p)
, and
\pi=B
, then

\dot{A}^{s,\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(V)\hookrightarrow\dot{A}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)\ \ \text{and}\ \ \dot{A}^{s,\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(V)\neq\dot{A}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V),

otherwise

\displaystyle\dot{A}^{s,\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(V)=\dot{A}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)

(9.15)

with equivalent quasi-norms.

Due to the
\varphi
-transform characterisation of both

\dot{A}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)
(proved in
[
12
, Theorem 3.29]
)
and
\dot{A}^{s,\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(V)
(Theorem
6.16
),
Theorem
9.12
can be proved by dealing with their corresponding sequence spaces.

Definition 9.13
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,

p\in(0,\infty)
,
q\in(0,\infty]
,
\pi\in S_{[2]}
,
and
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.
The
homogeneous matrix-weighted Besov–Triebel–Lizorkin-type sequence space associated with cubes

\dot{a}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)

is defined to be the set of all sequences

t:=\{t_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
in
\mathbb{C}^{m}
such that

\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)}:=\sup_{P\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}|P|^{-\tau}\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{P}2^{js}Vt_{j}\Big\}_{j\geq j_{P}}\Big\|_{(L^{p}\ell^{q})_{\pi}}<\infty.

The corresponding result for sequence spaces is as follows.

Theorem 9.14
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,
p\in(0,\infty)
,

q\in(0,\infty]
,
\pi\in S_{[2]}
, and
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.
If
\tau\in(0,\frac{1}{p})
or if
\tau=\frac{1}{p}
,

q\in(0,p)
, and
\pi=B
, then

\dot{a}^{s,\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(V)\hookrightarrow\dot{a}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)\ \ \text{and}\ \ \dot{a}^{s,\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(V)\neq\dot{a}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V),

otherwise

\displaystyle\dot{a}^{s,\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(V)=\dot{a}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)

(9.16)

with equivalent quasi-norms.

To prove Theorem
9.14
, we need two lemmas.
We first introduce two notations.
For every
\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}
, let

\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}:=\sup_{P\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}|P|^{-\tau}\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{P}f_{j}\Big\}_{j\geq j_{P}}\Big\|_{(L^{p}\ell^{q})_{\pi}}

and

\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\gamma}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}:=\sup_{\Omega\in\mathrm{Open}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}|\Omega|^{-\tau}\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{j}}f_{j}\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{(L^{p}\ell^{q})_{\pi}}.

Lemma 9.15
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty)
,
q\in(0,\infty]
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,
and
\pi\in S_{[2]}
.
Assume that
(p,q,\tau,\pi)
satisfies one of the following conditions:

(i)

\tau\in(\frac{1}{p},\infty)
;

(ii)

\tau=\frac{1}{p}
and
q=\infty
;

(iii)

\tau=\frac{1}{p}
,
q\in(0,\infty)
, and
\pi=F
;

(iv)

\tau=\frac{1}{p}
,
q\in[p,\infty)
, and
\pi=B
.

Then, for all
\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}
with each
f_{j}
constant on every dyadic cube in
\mathscr{D}_{j}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,

\displaystyle\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}\sim\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\gamma}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}},

where the positive equivalence constants depend only on
n
,
\tau
, and
p
.

Proof.

The inequality
\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}\leq\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\gamma}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}
is obvious.
Now, we prove the reverse inequality
by considering three cases for
(p,q,\tau,\pi)
.

Case (1)

(p,q,\tau,\pi)
satisfies (i) or (ii).
In this case, for every open set
\Omega
with
|\Omega|\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\big\|\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{j}}f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|_{(L^{p}\ell^{q})_{\pi}}\leq\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{j}}2^{-jn(\tau-\frac{1}{p})}\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{(L^{p}\ell^{q})_{\pi}}\Big\|\Big\{2^{jn(\tau-\frac{1}{p})}f_{j}\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}\ell^{\infty}}=:\mathrm{I}\times\mathrm{II}.

Let
j_{\Omega}:=\min\{j\in\mathbb{Z}:\ \Omega_{j}\neq\varnothing\}
.
Then there exists
P\in\mathscr{D}_{j_{\Omega}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

such that
P\subset\Omega
. These further imply that

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}\leq\|\mathbf{1}_{\Omega}\|_{L^{p}}\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{j\geq j_{\Omega}}2^{-jn(\tau-\frac{1}{p})}\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{\ell^{q}}\sim|\Omega|^{\frac{1}{p}}2^{-j_{\Omega}n(\tau-\frac{1}{p})}=|\Omega|^{\frac{1}{p}}|P|^{\tau-\frac{1}{p}}\leq|\Omega|^{\tau}.

Note that, for every
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and
P\in\mathscr{D}_{j}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,

\displaystyle\Big\|2^{jn(\tau-\frac{1}{p})}f_{j}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(P)}=|P|^{-\frac{1}{p}}\Big\|2^{jn(\tau-\frac{1}{p})}f_{j}\Big\|_{L^{p}(P)}=|P|^{-\tau}\|f_{j}\|_{L^{p}(P)}\leq\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}},

and hence
\mathrm{II}\leq\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}
.
Combining the above estimates, we obtain

\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\gamma}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}\lesssim\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}.

Case (2)

(p,q,\tau,\pi)
satisfies (iii).
In this case, let
\Omega
be an open set and

\{P_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be its maximal dyadic decomposition.
Then, for every
i\in I
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\mathbf{1}_{P_{i}\cap\Omega_{j}}=\mathbf{1}_{j\geq j_{P_{i}}}\mathbf{1}_{P_{i}}
,
which further implies that

\displaystyle\big\|\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{j}}f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}^{p}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in I}\int_{P_{i}}\Bigg(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{j}}|f_{j}|^{q}\Bigg)^{\frac{p}{q}}=\sum_{i\in I}\big\|\{\mathbf{1}_{P_{i}}f_{j}\}_{j\geq j_{P_{i}}}\big\|_{L^{p}\ell^{q}}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in I}|P_{i}|\,\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}^{p}=|\Omega|\,\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}^{p},

and hence
\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\gamma}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}\leq\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}
.

Case (3)

(p,q,\tau,\pi)
satisfies (iv).
In this case, let
\Omega
be an open set and

\{P_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be its maximal dyadic decomposition.
Then, for every
i\in I
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\mathbf{1}_{P_{i}\cap\Omega_{j}}=\mathbf{1}_{j\geq j_{P_{i}}}\mathbf{1}_{P_{i}}
,
which, together with Minkowski’s inequality, further implies that

\displaystyle\big\|\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{j}}f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|_{\ell^{q}L^{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Bigg(\sum_{i\in I}\int_{P_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{j}}|f_{j}|^{p}\Bigg)^{\frac{q}{p}}\Bigg]^{\frac{p}{q}}\leq\sum_{i\in I}\Bigg[\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Bigg(\int_{P_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{j}}|f_{j}|^{p}\Bigg)^{\frac{q}{p}}\Bigg]^{\frac{p}{q}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in I}\Big[\big\|\{\mathbf{1}_{P_{i}}f_{j}\}_{j\geq j_{P_{i}}}\big\|_{\ell^{q}L^{p}}^{q}\Big]^{\frac{p}{q}}\leq\sum_{i\in I}|P_{i}|\,\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}^{p}=|\Omega|\,\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}^{p},

and hence
\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\gamma}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}\leq\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}
.
This finishes the proof of Lemma
9.15
.
∎

Lemma 9.16
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty)
.
If
\tau\in(0,\frac{1}{p})
,
q\in(0,\infty]
, and
\pi\in S_{[2]}

or if
\tau=\frac{1}{p}
,
q\in(0,p)
, and
\pi=B
,
then, for all
\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}
with each
f_{j}
constant on every dyadic cube in
\mathscr{D}_{j}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,

\displaystyle\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}\leq\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\gamma}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}.

However, the reverse inequality fails, that is,
there exists
\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}
such that

\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}<\infty

but
\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\gamma}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}=\infty
.

Proof.

The inequality “
