\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}\leq\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\gamma}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}
” is obvious.
We need only to show that the reverse inequality fails.
To this end, we first construct a super-sparse sequence of dyadic cubes.
For every
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
, let
Q_{\kappa}

be a dyadic cube of edge-length
1
, contained in
[0,2^{\kappa})^{n}

and located in its “upper right” corner.
Next, we prove that the reverse inequality fails
by considering two cases for
(\tau,q,\pi)
.

Case (1)

\tau\in(0,\frac{1}{p})
,
q\in(0,\infty]
, and
\pi\in S_{[2]}
is arbitrary.
In this case, let
f_{0}:=\mathbf{1}_{\bigcup_{\kappa\in\mathbb{N}}Q_{\kappa}}

and
f_{j}:=0
for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
.
We first prove
\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}<\infty
.
By the construction of
\{Q_{\kappa}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
,
we conclude that, for every
P\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
with
|P|\geq 1
,

\#\{\kappa\in\mathbb{N}:\ P\cap Q_{\kappa}\neq\varnothing\}\leq-j_{P}+1,

which further implies that

\displaystyle|P|^{-\tau}\big\|\{\mathbf{1}_{P}f_{j}\}_{j\geq j_{P}}\big\|_{(L^{p}\ell^{q})_{\pi}}=|P|^{-\tau}\|\mathbf{1}_{P\cap(\bigcup_{\kappa\in\mathbb{N}}Q_{\kappa})}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq|P|^{-\tau}(-j_{P})^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq\sup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}2^{-in\tau}i^{\frac{1}{p}}<\infty.

Therefore,
\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}<\infty
.

Next, we show
\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\gamma}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}=\infty
.
For every
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
let
\Omega^{(N)}:=\bigcup_{\kappa=1}^{N}Q_{\kappa}
.
Then

\displaystyle|\Omega^{(N)}|^{-\tau}\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{(N)}_{j}}f_{j}\Big\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|_{(L^{p}\ell^{q})_{\pi}}=|\Omega^{(N)}|^{-\tau}\big\|\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{(N)}}\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=\big|\Omega^{(N)}\big|^{\frac{1}{p}-\tau}=N^{\frac{1}{p}-\tau}\to\infty

as
N\to\infty
.
Therefore,
\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\gamma}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}=\infty
.
Thus, the desired conclusion hold in this case.

Case (2)

\tau=\frac{1}{p}
,
q\in(0,p)
, and
\pi=B
.
In this case,
we can choose a sequence of dyadic cubes
\{R_{\kappa}\}_{\kappa\in\mathbb{N}}
that satisfies

(i)

for every
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
,

R_{\kappa}
is a subset of
Q_{\kappa}
and

(ii)

for every
j\in\mathbb{N}
,

E_{j}:=\bigcup\{R_{\kappa}:\ \ell(R_{\kappa})=2^{-j}\}

satisfies
|E_{j}|\sim j^{-a}
, where
a\in(1,\frac{p}{q}]
.

Let
f_{j}:=\mathbf{1}_{E_{j}}

for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
;

f_{j}:=0
for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\mathbb{N}
.
We first prove
\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}<\infty
.
For every
P\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
with
|P|<1
,
if
P\cap(\bigcup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}E_{j})=\varnothing
, then

|P|^{-\frac{1}{p}}\big\|\{\mathbf{1}_{P}f_{j}\}_{j\geq j_{P}}\big\|_{\ell^{q}L^{p}}=0;

otherwise there exists a dyadic cube
Q
such that

P\cap(\bigcup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}E_{j})=Q
and hence

|P|^{-\frac{1}{p}}\big\|\{\mathbf{1}_{P}f_{j}\}_{j\geq j_{P}}\big\|_{\ell^{q}L^{p}}\leq|P|^{-\frac{1}{p}}\|\mathbf{1}_{Q}\|_{L^{p}}=|P|^{-\frac{1}{p}}|Q|^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq 1.

For every
P\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
with
|P|\geq 1
,

P\cap(\bigcup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}E_{j})
is a union of at most
-j_{P}+1
dyadic cubes
from the family
\{R_{\kappa}\}_{\kappa\in\mathbb{N}}
,
which further implies that

\displaystyle|P|^{-\frac{1}{p}}\big\|\{\mathbf{1}_{P}f_{j}\}_{j\geq j_{P}}\big\|_{\ell^{q}L^{p}}

\displaystyle=|P|^{-\frac{1}{p}}\Bigg(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}|P\cap E_{j}|^{\frac{q}{p}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{j_{P}n\frac{1}{p}}\Bigg[\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}(-j_{P}+1)^{\frac{q}{p}}2^{-jn\frac{q}{p}}\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\sup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\bigg(\frac{i+1}{2^{in}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{p}}<\infty.

Therefore,
\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}<\infty
.

Next, we show
\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\gamma}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}=\infty
.
Let
\Omega:=\bigcup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}E_{j}
.
Then

|\Omega|=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}|E_{j}|\sim\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{j^{a}}<\infty

and

\displaystyle\big\|\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{j}}f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\|_{\ell^{q}L^{p}}^{q}=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}|E_{j}|^{\frac{q}{p}}=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}j^{-a\frac{q}{p}}=\infty.

Therefore,
\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\gamma}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}=\infty
.
Thus, the desired conclusions hold also in this case, which completes
the proof of Lemma
9.16
.
∎

Now, we can compare the space
\dot{a}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)

with the space
\dot{a}^{s,\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(V)
.

Proof of Theorem
9.14
.

We first prove

\dot{a}^{s,\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(V)\hookrightarrow\dot{a}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)\ \ \text{and}\ \ \dot{a}^{s,\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(V)\neq\dot{a}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)

in the cases where
\tau\in(0,\frac{1}{p})
or where
\tau=\frac{1}{p}
,

q\in(0,p)
, and
\pi=B
.
To this end, due to Theorem
6.4
and its analogue for the cubic space proved in
[
12
, Theorem 3.27]
, it suffices to show that,
for every
t:=\{t_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
with each
t_{Q}\in\mathbb{C}^{m}
,

\|\{2^{js}[V]_{j,p}t_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}\leq\|\{2^{js}[V]_{j,p}t_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\gamma}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}

(9.17)

and there exists
t:=\{t_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
such that

\|\{2^{js}[V]_{j,p}t_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}<\infty\ \ \text{but}\ \ \|\{2^{js}[V]_{j,p}t_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\gamma}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}=\infty.

(9.18)

By Lemma
9.16
, we conclude that
(
9.17
) holds and there exists
\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}
such that

\|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\beta}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}<\infty\ \ \text{but}\ \ \|\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{\dot{\gamma}^{\tau}_{p,q,\pi}}=\infty.

Choose
t:=\{t_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
such that

2^{js}[V]_{j,p}t_{j}=f_{j}
for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Then (
9.18
) holds.
For the remaining parameter ranges, that is, for all

(\tau,p,q,\pi)
not covered by the above cases,
using Lemma
9.15
with
\{f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}
replaced by

\{2^{js}[V]_{j,p}f_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}
,
and then applying
[
12
, Theorem 3.27]
and Theorem
6.4
,
we obtain equality (
9.16
).
This finishes the proof of Theorem
9.14
.
∎

Using Theorem
9.14
, we can
compare the corresponding function spaces, that is,
we can finish the proof of Theorem
9.12
.

Proof of Theorem
9.12
.

We first prove

\dot{A}^{s,\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(V)\hookrightarrow\dot{A}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)\ \ \text{and}\ \ \dot{A}^{s,\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(V)\neq\dot{A}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)

in the cases where
\tau\in(0,\frac{1}{p})
or where
\tau=\frac{1}{p}
,

q\in(0,p)
, and
\pi=B
.
The embedding is obvious.
On the other hand, Theorem
9.14
shows that
there exists
t:=\{t_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\in\dot{a}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)\setminus\dot{a}^{s,\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(V)
.
Let
\psi
be a Littlewood–Paley function. The
\varphi
-transform characterisation of the cubic space
\dot{a}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)
, namely
[
12
, Theorem 3.29]
, which is the
\dot{a}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)
analogue of Theorem
6.16
, shows that

f:=T_{\psi}t=\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}t_{Q}\psi_{Q}
converges in
\mathscr{S}^{\prime}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\mathbb{C}^{m})
with

\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)}\sim\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)}<\infty.

However, the
\varphi
-transform characterisation of
\dot{A}^{s,\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(V)
in Theorem
6.16
implies that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{A}^{s,\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(V)}\sim\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{s,\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(V)}=\infty,

and hence
f\in\dot{A}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)\setminus\dot{A}^{s,\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(V)
.

For the remaining parameter ranges, i.e., for all

(\tau,p,q,\pi)
not covered by the above cases,
equality (
9.15
) follows from Theorem
9.14
and
the same
\varphi
-transform characterisations of both spaces
\dot{A}^{s,\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(V)

and
\dot{A}^{s,\tau,\mathrm{cube}}_{p,q,\pi}(V)
.
This finishes the proof of Theorem
9.12
.
∎

9.5.
Rectangular multi-parameter Besov–Triebel–Lizorkin-type spaces

In this subsection, we will study a question similar to that in Subsection
9.4
, but in the general multi-parameter setting: What happens if, in the definition of multi-parameter Besov–Triebel–Lizorkin-type spaces, we take the supremum over all dyadic rectangles only, instead of all open sets?
We begin with a definition:

Definition 9.17
.

Let
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,

\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
,
\pi\in S_{[2k]}
,
and
V\in L^{p}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m\times m})
.
The
homogeneous matrix-weighted Besov–Triebel–Lizorkin-type sequence space associated with rectangles

\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau,\mathrm{rect}}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})

is defined to be the set of all sequences

t:=\{t_{Q}\}_{Q\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}
in
\mathbb{C}^{m}
such that

\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau,\mathrm{rect}}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)}:=\sup_{P\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|P|^{-\tau}\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{P}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}Vt_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\geq{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}_{P}}\Big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{\pi}}<\infty.

Let us compare the spaces
\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau,\mathrm{rect}}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)

and
\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
.
To limit the length of this article, we only consider the special cases
where
\vec{p}=p\cdot\vec{1}
,
\vec{q}=q\cdot\vec{1}
,

\pi\in\{B,F\}
, and
V\equiv I_{m}
.
Note that
(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{B}=\ell^{\vec{q}}L^{\vec{p}}
and
(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}})_{F}=L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{q}}
.
In the multi-parameter setting, these two spaces differ in more cases than those in the one-parameter setting.
In particular,

\mathrm{BMO}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})=\dot{F}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}_{2,2}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})\neq\dot{F}^{0,\frac{1}{2},\mathrm{rect}}_{2,2}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})

when
k\geq 2
, whereas all three spaces coincide when
k=1
.

Theorem 9.18
.

Let
k\geq 2
,
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in(0,\infty)
,

\vec{p}:=p\cdot\vec{1}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,

\vec{q}:=q\cdot\vec{1}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
, and
\pi\in\{B,F\}
.
If
\tau\in(0,\frac{1}{p})

or if
\tau=\frac{1}{p}
,
q<p
, and
\pi=B

or if
\tau=\frac{1}{p}
and
q=p
, then

\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})\hookrightarrow\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau,\mathrm{rect}}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})\ \ \text{and}\ \ \dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})\neq\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau,\mathrm{rect}}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}).

In the proof of this theorem, we will use a slight extension of the classical counterexample constructed by Carleson
[
23
]
, see Lemma
A.11
.

Proof of Theorem
9.18
.

The embedding
\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})\hookrightarrow\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau,\mathrm{rect}}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
is obvious;
it remains to prove that

\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})\neq\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau,\mathrm{rect}}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.
To this end, we consider two cases on
(p,q,\tau)
.

Case (1)

\tau\in(0,\frac{1}{p})
, or
\tau=\frac{1}{p}
,

q\in(0,p)
, and
\pi=B
.
In this case, Theorem
9.14
shows that

\dot{a}^{s_{1},\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}})\hookrightarrow\dot{a}^{s_{1},\tau,\mathrm{rect}}_{p,q,\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}})\quad\text{and}\quad\dot{a}^{s_{1},\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}})\neq\dot{a}^{s_{1},\tau,\mathrm{rect}}_{p,q,\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}}).

Thus, there exists
u:=\{u_{Q^{*}}\}_{Q^{*}\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}})}\in\dot{a}^{s_{1},\tau,\mathrm{rect}}_{p,q,\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}})

such that
u\notin\dot{a}^{s_{1},\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}})
. Let

t_{Q}:=\begin{cases}u_{Q^{*}},&\text{if }Q=Q^{*}\times[0,1)^{n_{2}+\cdots+n_{k}},\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Then

\displaystyle\|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau,\mathrm{rect}}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}=\|u\|_{\dot{a}^{s_{1},\tau,\mathrm{rect}}_{p,q,\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}})}<\infty\ \ \text{and}\ \ \|t\|_{\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}=\|u\|_{\dot{a}^{s_{1},\tau}_{p,q,\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}})}=\infty.

This finishes the proof of
\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})\neq\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau,\mathrm{rect}}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
in this case.

Case (2)

\tau=\frac{1}{p}
and
q=p
.
In this case, for every
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
let
\{R_{i}\}_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N}}
be as in Lemma
A.11

with
\varepsilon=\frac{1}{N}
and let

t_{Q}:=\begin{cases}2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}_{Q}\cdot(\vec{s}+\frac{\vec{n}}{2})},&Q=R_{i}+(N-1)\cdot\vec{1}\text{ for some }N\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\text{ and }i\in\mathscr{I}_{N},\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Let
\mathscr{I}:=\bigcup_{N\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\mathscr{I}_{N}
.
Then, for every
P\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,

\displaystyle|P|^{-\tau}\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{P}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\geq{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}_{P}}\Big\|_{(L^{\vec{p}}\ell^{\vec{p}})_{\pi}}=|P|^{-\tau}\Big[\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I},R_{i}\subset P}|R_{i}|\Big]^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq|P|^{-\tau}|P|^{\frac{1}{p}}=1,

using property (
iii
) of Lemma
A.11
in the inequality.
Hence
t\in\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau,\mathrm{rect}}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.

On the other hand, for every
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
let
\Omega^{(N)}:=\bigcup_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N}}R_{i}+(N-1)\cdot\vec{1}
. Then

\Omega^{(N)}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}:=\bigcup_{R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}:R\subset\Omega}R\supset\bigcup_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N}:R_{i}\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}R_{i}+(N-1)\cdot\vec{1}

so that

\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{(N)}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\geq\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N}:R_{i}\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}\cdot 2^{-\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot(\vec{s}+\frac{\vec{n}}{2})}\widetilde{\mathbf{1}}_{R_{i}}=\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N}:R_{i}\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}\mathbf{1}_{R_{i}}.

Since the pairwise distinct rectangles
R_{i}
in a fixed
\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
are pairwise disjoint, it follows that

\|\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{(N)}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{L^{p}}^{p}\geq\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N}:R_{i}\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|R_{i}|

and hence

\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{(N)}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{\ell^{\vec{p}}L^{\vec{p}}}^{p}\geq\sum_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N}:R_{i}\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}|R_{i}|=\sum_{i\in\mathscr{I}_{N}}|R_{i}|=1,

using property (
i
) of Lemma
A.11
in the last step. Thus

\displaystyle|\Omega^{(N)}|^{-\tau}\Big\|\Big\{\mathbf{1}_{\Omega^{(N)}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}}t_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\Big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\Big\|_{\ell^{\vec{p}}L^{\vec{p}}}\geq|\Omega^{(N)}|^{-\tau}>N^{\tau}

by property (
ii
) of Lemma
A.11
with
\varepsilon=\frac{1}{N}
in the last step.
As
N\to\infty
, this shows that
t\notin\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,
and finishes the proof of Theorem
9.18
.
∎

In
[
63
, Theorem 5.9]
, corresponding to the classical Hardy-BMO duality,
Frazier and Jawerth obtained an duality relation for Triebel–Lizorkin spaces:

[\dot{f}_{1,q}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})]^{*}=\dot{f}_{q^{\prime},q^{\prime}}^{-s,\frac{1}{q^{\prime}},\mathrm{cube}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}).

It is natural to guess that this duality also holds for multi-parameter spaces.
The following result indicates that this is true for sequence spaces associated
with open set, but not true for sequence spaces associated with cubes, which
provides another motivation for studying the space

\dot{a}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
.

Corollary 9.19
.

If
k=2
,
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and
q\in(1,\infty)
, then

\big[\dot{f}_{1,q}^{\vec{s}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})\big]^{*}=\dot{f}_{q^{\prime},q^{\prime}}^{-\vec{s},\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})\neq\dot{f}_{q^{\prime},q^{\prime}}^{-\vec{s},\frac{1}{q^{\prime}},\mathrm{rect}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}).

Proof.

By
[
50
, Theorem 4.5]
, we obtain the first “
=
”,
while the “
\neq
” follows from Theorem
9.18
.
This finishes the proof of Corollary
9.19
.
∎

10.
The
T(1)
theorem

In this section, we obtain a general criterion, in the spirit of a
T(1)
theorem, for the boundedness of multi-parameter singular integrals on the matrix-weighted multi-parameter Triebel–Lizorkin spaces
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
. We recall that the first
T(1)
theorem for the boundedness of one-parameter Calderón–Zygmund operators on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
was proved by David and Journé
[
43
]
, and the extension to multi-parameter Calderón–Zygmund (also known as Calderón–Zygmund–Journé, or simply Journé) operators on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
is due to Journé
[
98
]
. The name “
T(1)
theorem” derives from conditions of the type “
T(1)\in\operatorname{BMO}
” in the general form of these theorems, or “
T(1)=0
” in the important special case of “paraproduct-free” operators, where
T(1)
refers to the action of the operator
T
on the constant function
1
.

For Triebel–Lizorkin spaces the one-parameter setting, after a number of results in a limited range of indices (see
[
86
, pp. 222–223]
for a more complete history), extensions to the full range of indices were obtained by Frazier, Torres, and Weiss
[
68
,
144
]
, and their matrix-weighted extension was only recently by Bu et al.
[
14
]
.
In the matrix-weighted multi-parameter setting, Domelevo et al.
[
54
]
proved a paraproduct-free
T(1)
theorem in
L^{p}(V)
and a general
T(1)
theorem in
L^{2}(V)
, which was extended to a general
T(1)
theorem in
L^{p}(V)
by Vuorinen
[
151
]
.

In this section, we prove a matrix-weighted multi-parameter
T(1)
theorem on the general scale of
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
spaces, Theorem
10.30
, which essentially extends both
[
14
,
54
]
in the paraproduct-free case; for the fact that the
L^{p}(V)
spaces are contained as a special case of
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
, see Theorem
9.1
. We note that the restriction to the paraproduct-free case is made in most of the literature on
T(1)
theorems in general Triebel–Lizorkin spaces, even in the unweighted one-parameter setting. With the principal goal of extending existing theory to the matrix-weighted multi-parameter setting, we make this same restriction here. The added complication of paraproducts is left for future work and should probably be first addressed in the one-parameter Triebel–Lizorkin spaces, which is already an interesting question in its own right.

Towards the goal of stating and proving Theorem
10.30
, extending the approach of
[
14
]
from the one-parameter case, we first give an abstract criterion for extending operators

T\in\mathcal{L}\Big(\mathscr{S}\big(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\big),\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})\Big)\quad\mathrm{to}\quad\widetilde{T}\in\mathcal{L}\Big(\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)\Big)

by testing the action of
T
on suitable
atoms
. The main body of the section is then dedicated to verifying this criterion in the concrete case of multi-parameter singular integral operators.

As a first step, Subsection
10.1
is devoted to a streamlined one-parameter set-up, which allows us to
treat the multi-parameter case by induction on the number of parameters in Subsection
10.2
, where we complete the proof of the multi-parameter
T(1)
theorem.

Let
C_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
denote
the set of all functions
f\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
with compact support.

Definition 10.1
.

Let
\vec{L},\vec{N}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
.
A function
a_{Q}\in C_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})

is called an
(\vec{L},\vec{N})
-atom
on a rectangle
Q:=Q_{1}\times\cdots\times Q_{k}\in\mathrm{Rect}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
if

(i)

a_{Q}=\bigotimes_{\nu=1}^{k}a_{Q_{\nu}}
,

(ii)

for every
\nu\in[k]
,

\operatorname{supp}a_{Q_{\nu}}\subset 3Q_{\nu}
,

(iii)

for every
\nu\in[k]
and
\gamma_{\nu}\in\mathbb{N}^{n_{\nu}}
with
|\gamma_{\nu}|\leq L_{\nu}
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}}x^{\gamma_{\nu}}a_{Q_{\nu}}(x)\,dx_{\nu}=0
, and

(iv)

for every
\nu\in[k]
,

\gamma_{\nu}\in\mathbb{N}^{n_{\nu}}
with
|\gamma_{\nu}|\leq N_{\nu}
,
and
x_{\nu}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}
,

|D^{\gamma_{\nu}}a_{Q_{\nu}}(x_{\nu})|\leq|Q_{\nu}|^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{|\gamma_{\nu}|}{n_{\nu}}}
.

The following proposition is a straightforward multi-parameter extension of
[
14
, Proposition 6.5(i)]
;
we omit the details.

Proposition 10.2
.

Let
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
,
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,

\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be admissible for
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
,
and
V
be a matrix weight such that
(\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi,V)
belongs
to one of the main cases (Definition
7.3
).
Let
\vec{L},\vec{N}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
.
If
T\in\mathcal{L}(\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}),\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}))

maps
(\vec{L},\vec{N})
-atoms to
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}
-synthesis molecules (Definition
8.6
),
then there exists an operator
\widetilde{T}\in\mathcal{L}(\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V))
that agrees with
T
on
\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}};\mathbb{C}^{m})
.

If
T\in\mathcal{L}(\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}),\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}))
,
then, by the well-known Schwartz kernel theorem,
there exists a unique
K\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})

such that, for all
\varphi,\psi\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,

\langle T\varphi,\psi\rangle=\langle K,\psi\otimes\varphi\rangle.

(10.1)

This
K
is called the
Schwartz kernel
of
T
.

Thanks to the kernel theorem, in order to define a distribution
K\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, it is enough to specify
\langle K,\psi\otimes\varphi\rangle
for every pair of functions
\psi,\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
in such a way that
(\psi,\varphi)\mapsto\langle K,\psi\otimes\varphi\rangle
is bi-linear and separately continuous; indeed, this clearly defines an operator
T\in\mathcal{L}(\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}),\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}))
via (
10.1
), which then determines the full distribution
K\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
by the kernel theorem. We will use this observation without explicit mention in what follows.

10.1.
A review of the one-parameter case

Our approach to the multi-parameter results below will proceed by induction on the number
k
of parameters. In order to carry this out efficiently, it will be convenient to have a reasonably streamlined set-up for the one-parameter results available. For this purpose, we collect and reformulate the relevant results from
[
14
, Sections 6.2–6.3]

The following is a reformulation of
[
14
, Definition 6.11]
, introducing several auxiliary notions to streamline the core part of the definition.
(For the sake of simplicity, we do not make the fine distinction between two kernel classes
\operatorname{CZK}^{1}(E,F)\subset\operatorname{CZK}^{0}(E,F)
, present in
[
14
]
, but simply consider
\operatorname{CZK}(E,F):=\operatorname{CZK}^{1}(E,F)
.)

Definition 10.3
.

For
E,F\in\mathbb{R}
, let

\begin{split}\mathscr{G}(E,F):=\left.\begin{cases}(\gamma,\delta,\zeta,\rho)\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\times\mathbb{N}^{n}\times\{0,1\}\times\{0,1\}:\\
\quad|\gamma|\leq\lfloor\!\lfloor E\rfloor\!\rfloor_{+},\quad\delta=\mathbf{0},\quad(\zeta,\rho)=(0,0),&\text{or}\\
\quad|\gamma|=\lfloor\!\lfloor E\rfloor\!\rfloor\phantom{{}_{+}},\quad\delta=\mathbf{0},\quad(\zeta,\rho)=(1,0),&\text{or}\\
\quad|\gamma|\leq\lfloor\!\lfloor E\rfloor\!\rfloor_{+},\quad|\delta|=\lfloor\!\lfloor F\rfloor\!\rfloor-|\gamma|,\quad(\zeta,\rho)=(0,1),&\text{or}\\
\quad|\gamma|=\lfloor\!\lfloor E\rfloor\!\rfloor\phantom{{}_{+}},\quad|\delta|=\lfloor\!\lfloor F-E\rfloor\!\rfloor,\quad(\zeta,\rho)=(1,1)\end{cases}\right\}.\end{split}

(10.2)

For
\Gamma=(\gamma,\delta,\zeta,\rho)\in\mathscr{G}(E,F)
and
\upsilon\in(0,1)
, let

\mathbb{R}^{4n}_{\Gamma,\upsilon}:=\Big\{(X,Y)=((x,y),(u,v))\in\mathbb{R}^{4n}:\ u\neq\mathbf{0}\neq v,\ \zeta|u|+\rho|v|<\upsilon|x-y|\Big\}

and, for
U=(u,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}
,

\mathbb{D}_{U}^{\Gamma}:=\Delta_{U}^{(\zeta,\rho)}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\partial_{y}^{\delta},

where
\Delta_{U}^{(\zeta,\rho)}
is defined in
(
8.2
)
.

For
\Gamma=(\gamma,\delta,\zeta,\rho)\in\mathscr{G}(E,F)
and
(X,U)=((x,y),(u,v))\in\mathbb{R}^{4n}
, let

\operatorname{CZ}_{E,F}^{\Gamma}(X,U):=|x-y|^{n+|\gamma|+|\delta|}\bigg(\frac{|x-y|}{|u|}\bigg)^{\zeta E^{**}}\bigg(\frac{|x-y|}{|v|}\bigg)^{\rho(F-\zeta E)^{**}}.

For
K\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
, we say
K\in\operatorname{CZK}(E,F)
if
K(x,y)
and all its partial derivatives appearing below are continuous functions on
x\neq y
and the following quantity is finite:

\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}_{\upsilon}(E,F)}:=\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\Gamma\in\mathscr G(E,F)}{(X,U)\in\R^{4n}_{\Gamma,\upsilon}}}\operatorname{CZ}_{E,F}^{\Gamma}(X,U)|\mathbb{D}_{U}^{\Gamma}K(X)|.

Remark 10.4
.

The length of a multi-index
\gamma,\delta\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
is obviously non-negative.
In (
10.2
), there are several conditions of the type
|\gamma|=s
or
|\delta|=t
. If
s<0
or
t<0
, then we simply understand that there are no such multi-indices.

Remark 10.5
.

The parameter
\upsilon\in(0,1)
is usually taken simply as
\upsilon=\frac{1}{2}
. Since the differences
\Delta_{u}f(x)=f(x)-f(x-u)
can always be expressed as sums of differences of as much smaller step size as we like, namely,

\Delta_{u}f(x)=f(x)-f(x-u)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}f(x+\tfrac{i-1}{N}u)-f(x+\tfrac{i}{N}u)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Delta_{\frac{u}{N}}f(x+\tfrac{i-1}{N}u),

it is easy to see that the
\operatorname{CZK}_{\upsilon}(E,F)
norms are equivalent for all
\upsilon\in(0,1)
; thus, the kernel class
\operatorname{CZK}(E,F)
is independent of this parameter.

Remark 10.6
.

We denote
\operatorname{CZK}(E):=\operatorname{CZK}(E,0)
and

\operatorname{CZK}^{*}(F):=\{K:K^{*}\in\operatorname{CZK}(F)\}
, where
\langle K^{*},\psi\otimes\varphi\rangle:=\langle K,\varphi\otimes\psi\rangle
.
It is evident that
\operatorname{CZK}(E,F)\subset\operatorname{CZK}(E)
for all
F\in\mathbb{R}
.
While not evident, we also have
\operatorname{CZK}(E,F)\subset\operatorname{CZK}^{*}(F)
for all
E\in\mathbb{R}
; see
[
14
, (6.17)]
.

Remark 10.7
.

For technical reasons, it will be convenient to use the Calderón–Zygmund estimates in a slightly different but equivalent form. Note that, if
|x^{\prime}-x|+|y^{\prime}-y|<\hat{\upsilon}|x-y|
, then

|x-y|\leq|x-x^{\prime}|+|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|+|y^{\prime}-y|<\hat{\upsilon}|x-y|+|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|,

and thus
(1-\hat{\upsilon})|x-y|<|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|
. Hence, if
|u|,|v|<\upsilon|x-y|
, then
|u|,|v|<\upsilon^{\prime}|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|
where
\upsilon^{\prime}:=\frac{\upsilon}{1-\hat{\upsilon}}
still satisfies
\upsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1)
for
\hat{\upsilon}\in(0,1-\upsilon)
.

Thus, if
(X,U)\in\mathbb{R}^{4n}_{\Gamma,\upsilon}
, then
(X^{\prime},U)\in\mathbb{R}^{4n}_{\Gamma,\upsilon^{\prime}}
for all
X^{\prime}=(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})
as above. If
K\in\operatorname{CZK}(E,F)
and
\Gamma\in\mathscr{G}(E,F)
, it follows that

|\mathbb{D}^{\Gamma}_{U}K(X^{\prime})|\leq\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}_{\upsilon^{\prime}}(E,F)}\operatorname{CZ}^{\Gamma}_{E,F}(X^{\prime},U)^{-1}

and it is straightforward that

\operatorname{CZ}^{\Gamma}_{E,F}(X^{\prime},U)\sim\operatorname{CZ}^{\Gamma}_{E,F}(X,U)

for the ranges of the variables under consideration. Hence
\mathbb{D}^{\Gamma}_{U}K(X^{\prime})
has essentially the same bound as
|\mathbb{D}^{\Gamma}_{U}K(X)|
.

If
\phi\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is a usual bump function of integral one supported in the unit ball, and
\Phi^{X}_{\varepsilon}:=\varepsilon^{-2n}(\phi\otimes\phi)(\varepsilon^{-1}(\cdot-X))
, then
\langle\mathbb{D}_{U}^{\Gamma}K,\Phi^{X}_{\varepsilon}\rangle
, being an average of the values
\mathbb{D}_{U}^{\Gamma}K(X^{\prime})
, retains the same upper bound for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\hat{\upsilon}|x-y|)
. On the other hand,

\langle\mathbb{D}_{U}^{\Gamma}K,\Phi^{X}_{\varepsilon}\rangle\underset{\varepsilon\to 0}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{D}_{U}^{\Gamma}K(X).

Hence we see that

\begin{split}\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}_{\upsilon}(E,F)}:&=\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\Gamma\in\mathscr G(E,F)}{(X,U)\in\R^{4n}_{\Gamma,\upsilon}}}\operatorname{CZ}_{E,F}^{\Gamma}(X,U)|\mathbb{D}_{U}^{\Gamma}K(X)|\\
&\leq\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\genfrac{}{}{0pt}{}{\Gamma\in\mathscr G(E,F)}
{(X,U)\in\R^{4n}_{\Gamma,\upsilon}}}{0<\eps<\hat\upsilon\abs{x-y}}}\operatorname{CZ}_{E,F}^{\Gamma}(X,U)|\langle\mathbb{D}_{U}^{\Gamma}K,\Phi_{\varepsilon}^{X}\rangle|\lesssim\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}_{\upsilon^{\prime}}(E,F)}.\end{split}

Since the different
\operatorname{CZK}_{\upsilon}(E,F)
-norms are equivalent, we see that the quantity sandwiched between them above is also equivalent.

Fixing some values of the auxiliary parameters, say
\upsilon=\frac{1}{2}
and
\hat{\upsilon}=\frac{1}{3}<\frac{1}{2}=1-\upsilon
, we adopt this as a definition of

\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(E,F)}:=\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\Gamma\in\mathscr G(E,F)}{(X,U,\eps)\in\R^{4n+1}_{\Gamma} }}\operatorname{CZ}_{E,F}^{\Gamma}(X,U)|\langle\mathbb{D}_{U}^{\Gamma}K,\Phi_{\varepsilon}^{X}\rangle|,

where

\mathbb{R}^{4n+1}_{\Gamma}:=\Big\{(X,U,\varepsilon)\in\mathbb{R}^{4n+1}:\ (X,U)=((x,y),(u,v))\in\mathbb{R}^{4n}_{\Gamma,\frac{1}{2}},\ 0<\varepsilon<\tfrac{1}{3}|x-y|\Big\}.

Definition 10.8
.

We say that
(\chi_{j})_{j=0}^{\infty}\subset C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is a
uniform approximation to the identity
if
\sup_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\|\chi_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}}<\infty
and
for every compact
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
, there is an index
j_{K}\in\mathbb{N}
for which
\chi_{j}(x)=1
for all
x\in K
and
j\geq j_{K}
.

Lemma 10.9
.

Let
K\in\operatorname{CZK}(E,F)
. Then the limits

\langle K,f\otimes y^{\mu}\rangle:=\lim_{j\to\infty}\langle K,f\otimes\chi_{j}y^{\mu}\rangle,\qquad\langle K,x^{\lambda}\otimes g\rangle:=\lim_{j\to\infty}\langle K,\chi_{j}x^{\lambda}\otimes g\rangle

(10.3)

exist and are independent of the uniform approximation to the identity
(\chi_{j})_{j=0}^{\infty}
, for all
|\mu|\leq\lfloor\!\lfloor E\rfloor\!\rfloor
,
|\lambda|\leq\lfloor\!\lfloor F\rfloor\!\rfloor
,
and
f\in\mathcal{D}_{|\mu|}
,
g\in\mathcal{D}_{|\lambda|}
, where

\mathcal{D}_{\ell}:=\bigg\{g\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n}):\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}x^{\gamma}g(x)dx=0\text{ for all }|\gamma|\leq\ell\bigg\}.

Proof.

Since
\operatorname{CZK}(E,F)\subset\operatorname{CZK}(E)
(Remark
10.6
), the claim concerning the first limit follows from
[
14
, Lemma 6.9]
(which is a special case of
[
144
, Lemma 2.2.12]
).
Noting
\operatorname{CZK}(E,F)\subset\operatorname{CZK}^{*}(F)
(Remark
10.6
), the other claim is similar.
∎

Definition 10.10
.

For
K\in\operatorname{CZK}(E,F)
and
|\mu|\leq\lfloor\!\lfloor E\rfloor\!\rfloor
, we say that

\langle K,\cdot\otimes y^{\mu}\rangle:=0

if
\langle K,f\otimes y^{\mu}\rangle
, as defined in (
10.3
), vanishes for all
f\in\mathcal{D}_{|\mu|}
.

For
|\lambda|\leq\lfloor\!\lfloor F\rfloor\!\rfloor
, the condition
\langle K,x^{\lambda}\otimes\cdot\rangle=0
is defined analogously.

We also recall the notion of the weak boundedness property in the following formulation:

Definition 10.11
.

For functions
\eta,\xi\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and numbers
W\in\mathbb{N}
and
r\in[0,\infty)
, let

\delta(\eta,\xi):=\operatorname{diam}(\operatorname{supp}\eta\cup\operatorname{supp}\xi),\qquad P(W,\eta,r):=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq W}r^{|\alpha|}\|\partial^{\alpha}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}},

and

B_{W}(\eta,\xi):=\delta(\eta,\xi)^{n}\cdot P(W,\eta,\delta(\eta,\xi))\cdot P(W,\xi,\delta(\eta,\xi)).

Let

\mathscr{B}(W):=\{\Xi=\eta\otimes\xi:\quad\eta,\xi\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),\quad B_{W}(\eta,\xi)\leq 1\}\subset\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}).

We say that
K\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfies the
weak boundedness property
of order
W
if the following quantity is finite:

\|K\|_{\mathrm{WBP}(W)}:=\sup_{\Xi\in\mathscr{B}(W)}|\langle K,\Xi\rangle|.

Remark 10.12
.

The weak boundedness property is often defined (see e.g.
[
98
, Definition 4]
) with reference to suitable translates and dilates of pairs of functions in bounded subsets of
C_{\rm c}^{\infty}
, but the condition above is equivalent, as pointed out in
[
98
, (1.9)]
; note that
\delta(\eta,\xi)
is comparable to the edge-length of the smallest cube that contains both
\operatorname{supp}f
and
\operatorname{supp}g
appearing in
[
98
, (1.9)]
. While the parameter
W
is largely irrelevant, the advantage of the quantitative formulation of Definition
10.11
is that it is amenable to iteration in subsequent considerations.

Another detail is that the weak boundedness is often defined as a property of an
operator

T\in\mathcal{L}(\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
, instead of its
kernel

K\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
. This is quite natural in one-parameter considerations, where the weak boundedness and the kernel estimates play essentially distinct roles from each other. However, for a smoother discussion of the multi-parameter setting, it seems convenient to define both Calderón–Zygmund and weak boundedness estimates as properties of the Schwartz kernel
K
.

The following is essentially a restatement of
[
14
, Definition 6.17]
:

Definition 10.13
.

Let
E,F\in\mathbb{R}
,
G,H\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
W\in\mathbb{N}
, with
G\leq\lfloor\!\lfloor E\rfloor\!\rfloor
and
H\leq\lfloor\!\lfloor F\rfloor\!\rfloor
.
For
K\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
, we say that
K\in\operatorname{CZK}(E,F,G,H,W)
, if

(i)

K
satisfies the weak boundedness property of order
W
;

(ii)

K\in\operatorname{CZK}(E,F)
;

(iii)

\langle K,\cdot\otimes y^{\mu}\rangle=0
for all
|\mu|\leq G
,

(iv)

\langle K,x^{\lambda}\otimes\cdot\rangle=0
for all
|\lambda|\leq H
.

We define

\begin{split}\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(E,F,W)}&:=\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(E,F)}\vee\|K\|_{\operatorname{WBP}(W)},\\
\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(E,F,G,H,W)}&:=\begin{cases}\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(E,F,W)},&\text{if \eqref{it:T1=0G} and \eqref{it:T*1=0H} hold},\\
\infty,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}\end{split}

Remark 10.14
.

We have incorporated the restrictions
G\leq\lfloor\!\lfloor E\rfloor\!\rfloor
and
H\leq\lfloor\!\lfloor F\rfloor\!\rfloor
into Definition
10.13
, since conditions (
iii
) and (
iv
) are only guaranteed to make sense under these assumptions. While these restrictions were not stated in
[
14
, Definition 6.17]
, one can see that the actual results based on that definition, namely
[
14
, Theorem 6.18, Proposition 6.19, and Corollaries 6.20–6.21]
, are compatible with these restrictions.

Definition 10.15
.

For
M\in[0,\infty)
,
N\in(0,\infty)
, and
L\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq-1}
, we define

\mathscr{A}(N):=\{(\alpha,\kappa)\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\times\{0,1\}:\quad|\alpha|\leq\lfloor\!\lfloor N\rfloor\!\rfloor_{+},\ \kappa=0,\quad\text{or}\quad|\alpha|=\lfloor\!\lfloor N\rfloor\!\rfloor,\ \kappa=1\},

(10.4)

and

\|m\|_{\operatorname{Mol}(M,N)}:=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\alpha,\kappa)\in\mathscr{A}(N)\\
|h|\leq 1\end{subarray}}|h|^{-N^{**}\kappa}\Big\|(1+|\cdot|^{2})^{\frac{M}{2}}\Delta_{h}^{\kappa}\partial^{\alpha}m\Big\|_{L^{\infty}}

as well as

\|m\|_{\operatorname{Mol}(L,M,N)}:=\begin{cases}\|m\|_{\operatorname{Mol}(M,N)},&\text{if}\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}x^{\gamma}m(x)dx=0\quad\text{for all }|\gamma|\leq L,\\
\infty,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Remark 10.16
.

Comparison with Definition
8.1
shows that
\|m\|_{\operatorname{Mol}(L,M,N)}\leq 1
is essentially equivalent to the condition that
m
is an
(M,L,M,N)
-molecule on
[0,1)^{n}
.
[More precisely, for some
c\in(0,1)
the condition
\|m\|_{\operatorname{Mol}(L,M,N)}\leq c
implies that
m
is an
(M,L,M,N)
-molecule, which in turn implies that
\|m\|_{\operatorname{Mol}(L,M,N)}\leq C
for some constant
C\in(1,\infty)
.]

Note in particular that
(1+|x|^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\sim 1+|x|
. In Definition
10.15
, we choose the
C^{\infty}
factor
(1+|x|^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
for technical convenience in later considerations.

The key result that we borrow from the one-parameter theory is the following elaboration of the statement of
[
14
, Proposition 6.19]
:

Proposition 10.17
.

Let
E,F,M\in\mathbb{R}
,
G,H\in\mathbb{Z}
,
L\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq-1}
,

N\in(0,\infty)
, and
W,L^{\prime},N^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}

satisfy

\begin{cases}E\geq N,\\
E>\lfloor N\rfloor_{+},\end{cases}\begin{cases}F\geq M-n,\\
F>L,\end{cases}\begin{cases}G\geq\lfloor N\rfloor_{+},\\
H\geq L,\end{cases}\begin{cases}L^{\prime}\geq\lfloor\!\lfloor F\rfloor\!\rfloor,\\
N^{\prime}\geq\lfloor G\rfloor+1.\end{cases}

(10.5)

Let
T\in\mathcal{L}(\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
have Schwartz kernel
K\in\operatorname{CZK}(E,F,G,H,W)
. Then
T
maps
(L^{\prime},N^{\prime})
-atoms into
(M,L,M,N)
-molecules. In particular, for
(L^{\prime},N^{\prime})
-atoms
\varphi
on
[0,1)^{n}
, we have

\|T\varphi\|_{\operatorname{Mol}(L,M,N)}\lesssim\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(E,F,G,H,W)}\|\varphi\|_{\operatorname{Atom}(L^{\prime},N^{\prime})},

(10.6)

where

\|\varphi\|_{\operatorname{Atom}(L^{\prime},N^{\prime})}:=\begin{cases}\sup_{|\alpha|\leq N^{\prime}}\|\partial^{\alpha}\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}},&\text{if }\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}x^{\gamma}\varphi(x)dx=0\text{ for all }|\gamma|\leq L^{\prime},\\
\infty,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

and the implicit positive constant is independent of
T
and
\varphi
.

Remark 10.18
.

(i)

The quantitative statement (
10.6
) did not explicitly appear in
[
14
, Proposition 6.19]
, but it easily follows by inspection of the argument.

(ii)

The conditions on
L^{\prime},N^{\prime}
were not part of the statement of
[
14
, Proposition 6.19]
, but they appear right in the beginning of its proof. We have slightly simplified the conditions appearing there, namely
L^{\prime}\geq\lfloor\!\lfloor F\rfloor\!\rfloor\vee\lfloor\!\lfloor F-E\rfloor\!\rfloor
and
N^{\prime}\geq(\lfloor\!\lfloor E\rfloor\!\rfloor\wedge\lfloor G\rfloor)+1
, which is justified as follows: First, since
E>\lfloor N\rfloor_{+}\geq 0
by assumption, we see that
\lfloor\!\lfloor F\rfloor\!\rfloor\geq\lfloor\!\lfloor F-E\rfloor\!\rfloor
always dominates the maximum. Second, in Definition
10.13
(as explained in Remark
10.14
), we have incorporated the restriction that
G\leq\lfloor\!\lfloor E\rfloor\!\rfloor
, and hence also
\lfloor G\rfloor\leq\lfloor\!\lfloor E\rfloor\!\rfloor
.

For induction of the number of parameters, it will be convenient to use Proposition
10.17
in the following dualised form:

Corollary 10.19
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
10.17
, for all
\psi\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, we have

\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{(\alpha,\kappa)\in\mathscr A(N)}{\abs{h}\leq 1}}|h|^{-N^{**}\kappa}|\langle(1+|x|^{2})^{\frac{M}{2}}\Delta_{(h,0)}^{(\kappa,0)}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}K,\psi\otimes\varphi\rangle|\lesssim\|\psi\|_{L^{1}}\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(E,F,G,H,W)}\|\varphi\|_{\operatorname{Atom}(L^{\prime},N^{\prime})}.

Proof.

Using the identity

\begin{split}\langle(1+|x|^{2})^{\frac{M}{2}}\Delta_{(h,0)}^{(\kappa,0)}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}K,\psi\otimes\varphi\rangle&=\langle K,((-\partial_{x})^{\alpha}\Delta_{-h}^{\kappa}[(1+|x|^{2})^{\frac{M}{2}}\psi])\otimes\varphi\rangle\\
&=\langle T\varphi,(-\partial_{x})^{\alpha}\Delta_{-h}^{\kappa}[(1+|x|^{2})^{\frac{M}{2}}\psi]\rangle\\
&=\langle(1+|x|^{2})^{\frac{M}{2}}\Delta_{h}^{\kappa}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}T\varphi,\psi\rangle\end{split}

and the
L^{1}
–
L^{\infty}
-duality, Corollary
10.19
immediately follows from Proposition
10.17
.
∎

10.2.
The multi-parameter case

For all
\vec{n}:=(n_{\nu})_{\nu\in[k]}
and
I\subset[k]
,
let
\vec{n}_{I}:=(n_{\nu})_{\nu\in I}
.

Starting with the pioneering work of
[
98
]
, the idea of multi-parameter operators of Calderón–Zygmund type is to impose all possible combinations of Calderón–Zygmund kernel conditions (Definition
10.3
) and weak boundedness conditions (Definition
10.11
) over all complementary sets of variables:

Definition 10.20
.

Let
\vec{E},\vec{F}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
and
\vec{W}\in\mathbb{N}
.
For
K\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, we say that

K\in\mathrm{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{W})
if
K(x,y)
and all its partial derivatives appearing below are continuous functions on

x_{\nu}\neq y_{\nu}
with all
\nu\in[k]
and the following quantity is finite:

\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{W})}:=\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{[k]=I\cup J}{I\cap J=\varnothing}}\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}_{I,J}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{W})},

where

\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}_{I,J}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{W})}:=\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\Gamma_I\in\mathscr G(\vE,\vF)_I}{\genfrac{}{}{0pt}{}{(X,U,\vec\eps)\in\R^{4\vn_I+\abs{I}}_{\Gamma}}{
\Xi_{J}\in\mathscr B(\vec W)_J}}}\operatorname{CZ}_{\vec{E},\vec{F}}^{\Gamma;I}(X,U)\langle\mathbb{D}^{\Gamma;I}_{U}K,(\Phi_{\vec{\varepsilon}}^{X})_{I}\otimes\Xi_{J}\rangle

and here

\mathscr{G}(\vec{E},\vec{F})_{I}:=\prod_{\nu\in I}\mathscr{G}(\vec{E}_{\nu},\vec{F}_{\nu}),\qquad\mathbb{R}^{4\vec{n}_{I}+|I|}_{\Gamma}:=\prod_{\nu\in I}\mathbb{R}^{4n_{\nu}+1}_{\Gamma_{\nu}},

\begin{split}\mathscr{B}(\vec{W})_{J}&:=\Bigg\{\Xi_{J}=\bigotimes_{\nu\in J}\Xi_{\nu}:\Xi_{\nu}=(\eta_{\nu}\otimes\xi_{\nu}),\ \eta_{\nu},\xi_{\nu}\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}),B_{W_{\nu}}(\eta_{\nu},\xi_{\nu})\leq 1\Bigg\},\end{split}

and

\operatorname{CZ}_{\vec{E},\vec{F}}^{\Gamma;I}(X,U):=\prod_{\nu\in I}\operatorname{CZ}_{E_{\nu},F_{\nu}}^{\Gamma_{\nu}}(X_{\nu},U_{\nu}),\qquad\mathbb{D}^{\Gamma;I}_{U}:=\bigotimes_{\nu\in I}\mathbb{D}^{\Gamma_{\nu}}_{U_{\nu}},\qquad(\Phi_{\vec{\varepsilon}}^{X})_{I}:=\bigotimes_{\nu\in I}\Phi^{X_{\nu}}_{\varepsilon_{\nu}}.

Remark 10.21
.

When
k=1
, there are only two subsets
I=\varnothing
and
I=\{1\}
of
[1]=\{1\}
, and we immediately recognise

\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}_{\varnothing,\{1\}}(E,F,W)}=\|K\|_{\operatorname{WBP}(W)},\qquad\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}_{\{1\},\varnothing}(E,F,W)}=\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(E,F)},

and hence
\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(E,F,W)}
agrees with the earlier Definition
10.13
in the one-parameter case.

Definition 10.22
.

Let
K\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.
For
I\subset[k]
, and
\psi_{I},\varphi_{I}\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}_{I}})
, we define a new distribution

\langle K,\psi_{I}\otimes\varphi_{I}\rangle_{I}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}_{[k]\setminus I}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}_{[k]\setminus I}})

by

\Big\langle\langle K,\psi_{I}\otimes\varphi_{I}\rangle_{I},\psi_{[k]\setminus I}\otimes\varphi_{[k]\setminus I}\Big\rangle:=\langle K,(\psi_{I}\otimes\psi_{[k]\setminus I})\otimes(\varphi_{I}\otimes\varphi_{[k]\setminus I})\rangle

for all
\psi_{[k]\setminus I},\varphi_{[k]\setminus I}\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}_{[k]\setminus I}})
.

We record the following simple observation:

Lemma 10.23
.

Let
\psi_{\nu},\varphi_{\nu}\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}})
, let
\psi_{I}:=\bigotimes_{\nu\in I}\psi_{\nu}
and
\varphi_{I}
be defined similarly.
Assume that
K\in\mathrm{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{W})
. If
[k]=I\cup J
with
I\cap J=\varnothing
, then

\|\langle K,\psi_{I}\otimes\varphi_{I}\rangle_{I}\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E}_{J},\vec{F}_{J},\vec{W}_{J})}\leq\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{W})}\prod_{\nu\in I}B_{W_{\nu}}(\psi_{\nu},\varphi_{\nu}).

Proof.

Let
r_{\nu}:=B_{W_{\nu}}(\psi_{\nu},\varphi_{\nu})
. If any of these numbers vanishes, then one of
\psi_{\nu}
or
\varphi_{\nu}
vanishes, in which case the left-hand side is zero, and there is nothing to prove. Otherwise, let
\psi_{\nu}^{\prime}:=r_{\nu}^{-1}\psi_{\nu}
. Then
\Psi_{\nu}:=\psi_{\nu}^{\prime}\otimes\varphi_{\nu}\in\mathscr{B}(W_{\nu})
and
\Psi_{I}:=\bigotimes_{\nu\in I}\Psi_{\nu}\in\mathscr{B}(\vec{W})_{I}
. Hence

\begin{split}&\|\langle K,\psi_{I}\otimes\varphi_{I}\rangle_{I}\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E}_{J},\vec{F}_{J},\vec{W}_{J})}\\
&=\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{{J=G\cup H}}{G\cap H=\varnothing}}\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\genfrac{}{}{0pt}{}{\Gamma_G\in\mathscr G(\vE,\vF)_G}{(X,U,\vec\eps)\in\R^{4\vn_G+\abs{G}}_{\Gamma}}}{
\Xi_{H}\in\mathscr B(\vec W)_H}}\operatorname{CZ}_{\vec{E},\vec{F}}^{\Gamma;G}(X,U)|\langle\mathbb{D}^{\Gamma;G}_{U}\langle K,\psi_{I}\otimes\varphi_{I}\rangle_{I},(\Phi_{\vec{\varepsilon}}^{X})_{G}\otimes\Xi_{H}\rangle|,\end{split}

where

\langle\mathbb{D}^{\Gamma;G}_{U}\langle K,\psi_{I}\otimes\varphi_{I}\rangle_{I},(\Phi_{\vec{\varepsilon}}^{X})_{G}\otimes\Xi_{H}\rangle=\langle\mathbb{D}^{\Gamma;G}_{U}K,(\Phi_{\vec{\varepsilon}}^{X})_{G}\otimes\Psi_{I}\otimes\Xi_{H}\rangle\prod_{\nu\in I}r_{\nu}.

Here
\Psi_{I}\otimes\Xi_{H}\in\mathscr{B}(\vec{W})_{I\cup H}
and we have
G\cup(I\cup H)=I\cup J=[k]
and
G\cap(I\cup H)=\varnothing
. Hence the previous line multiplied by
\operatorname{CZ}_{\vec{E},\vec{F}}^{\Gamma;G}(X,U)
is bounded by

\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{W})}\prod_{\nu\in I}r_{\nu}
, which is the right-hand side of the claim. This completes the proof.
∎

In particular, taking
I=[k]\setminus\{\nu\}
, we end up with one-parameter operators
\langle K,\psi_{I}\otimes\varphi_{I}\rangle_{I}\in\operatorname{CZK}(E_{\nu},F_{\nu})
, for which we have Definition
10.10
about the annihilation of monomials. This immediately leads to the following extensions:

Definition 10.24
.

For
K\in\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{W})
, all
\nu\in[k]
and
|\mu_{\nu}|\leq\lfloor\!\lfloor E_{\nu}\rfloor\!\rfloor
, we say that

\langle K,\cdot\otimes y_{\nu}^{\mu_{\nu}}\rangle:=0\quad\text{if}\quad\langle\langle K,\psi_{[k]\setminus\{\nu\}}\otimes\varphi_{[k]\setminus\{\nu\}}\rangle_{[k]\setminus\{\nu\}},\cdot\otimes y^{\mu_{\nu}}\rangle=0

in the sense of Definition
10.10
, for all
\psi_{\iota},\varphi_{\iota}\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\iota}})
, where
\psi_{[k]\setminus\{\nu\}}:=\bigotimes_{\iota\in[k]\setminus\{\nu\}}\psi_{\iota}
and
\varphi_{[k]\setminus\{\nu\}}
is defined similarly.

The condition
\langle K,x_{\nu}^{\lambda_{\nu}}\otimes\cdot\rangle=0

for all
|\lambda_{\nu}|\leq\lfloor\!\lfloor F_{\nu}\rfloor\!\rfloor
is defined analogously.

Definition 10.25
.

Given
K\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\times\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, we say that
K\in\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{G},\vec{H},\vec{W})
if

(i)

K\in\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{W})
,

(ii)

\langle K,\cdot\otimes y_{\nu}^{\mu_{\nu}}\rangle=0
for all
\nu\in[k]
and all
|\mu_{\nu}|\leq G_{\mu}\leq\lfloor\!\lfloor E_{\mu}\rfloor\!\rfloor
,

(iii)

\langle K,x_{\nu}^{\lambda_{\nu}}\otimes\cdot\rangle=0
for all
\nu\in[k]
and all
|\lambda_{\nu}|\leq H_{\mu}\leq\lfloor\!\lfloor F_{\mu}\rfloor\!\rfloor
.

We define

\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{G},\vec{H},\vec{W})}:=\begin{cases}\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{W})},&\text{if \eqref{it:T1=0k} and \eqref{it:T*1=0k} hold},\\
\infty,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Lemma 10.26
.

Let
\psi_{\nu},\varphi_{\nu}\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}})
,
and let
\psi_{I}:=\bigotimes_{\nu\in I}\psi_{\nu}
and
\varphi_{I}
be defined similarly. Let
[k]=I\cup J
with
I\cap J=\varnothing
. If
K\in\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{G},\vec{H},\vec{W})
, then

\langle K,\psi_{I}\otimes\varphi_{I}\rangle_{I}\in\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E}_{J},\vec{F}_{J},\vec{G}_{J},\vec{H}_{J},\vec{W}_{J}).

Proof.

Since

K\in\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{G},\vec{H},\vec{W})\subset\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{W}),

we know from Lemma
10.23
that

\langle K,\psi_{I}\otimes\varphi_{I}\rangle_{I}\in\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E}_{J},\vec{F}_{J},\vec{W}_{J})
.
Hence it remains to verify the cancellation conditions contained in
\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E}_{J},\vec{F}_{J},\vec{G}_{J},\vec{H}_{J},\vec{W}_{J})
.

Let
\nu\in J
and
|\mu_{\nu}|\leq G_{\mu}
. Since

K\in\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{G},\vec{H},\vec{W})
, we have

\langle K,\cdot\otimes y_{\nu}^{\mu_{\nu}}\rangle=0
, which means that, for all
\psi_{\iota},\varphi_{\iota}\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}})
and
\psi_{I},\varphi_{I}
as defined in the statement of the lemma, we have

\begin{split}0&=\Big\langle\langle K,\psi_{[k]\setminus\{\nu\}}\otimes\varphi_{[k]\setminus\{\nu\}}\rangle_{[k]\setminus\{\nu\}},\cdot\otimes y^{\mu_{\nu}}\Big\rangle\\
&=\Big\langle\Big\langle\langle K,\psi_{I}\otimes\varphi_{I}\rangle_{I},\psi_{J\setminus\{\nu\}}\otimes\varphi_{J\setminus\{\nu\}}\Big\rangle_{J\setminus\{\nu\}},\cdot\otimes y^{\mu_{\nu}}\Big\rangle,\end{split}

and vanishing of the right-hand side, for all
\psi_{\iota},\varphi_{\iota}\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\iota}})
with
\iota\in J\setminus\{\nu\}
, is the definition of

\langle\langle K,\psi_{I}\otimes\varphi_{I}\rangle_{I},\cdot\otimes y^{\mu_{\nu}}\rangle=0
.

The proof of
\langle\langle K,\psi_{I}\otimes\varphi_{I}\rangle_{I},x^{\lambda_{\nu}}\otimes\cdot\rangle=0
, for all
\nu\in J
and
|\lambda_{\mu}|\leq H_{\mu}
, is similar. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 10.27
.

Let
\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{G},\vec{H},\vec{L},\vec{L}^{\prime},\vec{M},\vec{N},\vec{N}^{\prime},\vec{W}
be vectors, whose respective components satisfy the assumptions (
10.5
) of Proposition
10.17
,
and let
K\in\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{G},\vec{H},\vec{W})
.
For each
\nu\in[k]
, let
\mathscr{A}(N_{\nu})\subset\mathbb{N}^{n_{\nu}}\times\{0,1\}

be as in (
10.4
), and let

\mathscr{A}(\vec{N}):=\Big\{(\alpha,\kappa)\in\mathbb{N}^{\vec{n}}\times\{0,1\}^{k}:(\alpha_{\nu},\kappa_{\nu})\in\mathscr{A}(N_{\nu})\text{ for every }\nu\in[k]\Big\}

For
\varphi=\bigotimes_{\nu=1}^{k}\varphi_{\nu}
with
\varphi_{\nu}\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(3\cdot[0,1)^{n_{\nu}})
, and
\psi=\bigotimes_{\nu=1}^{k}\psi_{\nu}
with
\psi_{\nu}\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}})
, we then have

\Big|\Big\langle(\vec{1}+|x|_{\mathrm{vec}}^{2})^{\frac{\vec{M}}{2}}\Delta_{(h,0)}^{(\kappa,0)}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}K,{\psi\otimes\varphi}\Big\rangle\Big|\lesssim\|\psi\|_{L^{1}}\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{G},\vec{H},\vec{W})}\|\varphi\|_{\operatorname{Atom}(\vec{L}^{\prime},\vec{N}^{\prime})}|h|_{\rm{vec}}^{\vec{N}^{**}\kappa}

(10.7)

for all
(\alpha,\kappa)\in\mathscr{A}(\vec{N})
and all
h\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}

satisfying
|h|_{\rm{vec}}\leq\vec{1}
,
where the implicit positive constant is independent of
\varphi
,
\psi
, and
K
, and where
|x|_{\mathrm{vec}}^{2}:=(|x_{1}|^{2},\ldots,|x_{k}|^{2})
.

Proof.

We use induction on
k
. The base case
k=1
is contained in Corollary
10.19
. We assume for induction that the
\tilde{k}
-parameter case has been proved for all
\tilde{k}\in[k-1]
, and we proceed to prove it for
k
. For any
I\subset[k]
, we denote
\varphi_{I}:=\bigotimes_{\nu\in I}\varphi_{\nu}
, and similarly for
\psi_{I}
.

Let
[k]=I\cup J
with
I\cap J=\varnothing
be some non-trivial splitting; hence both
|I|,|J|\in[k-1]
.
Then

\begin{split}\Big\langle(\vec{1}+|x|_{\mathrm{vec}}^{2})^{\frac{\vec{M}}{2}}\Delta_{(h,0)}^{(\kappa,0)}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}K,\psi\otimes\varphi\Big\rangle=\Big\langle(\vec{1}+|x_{I}|_{\mathrm{vec}}^{2})^{\frac{\vec{M}_{I}}{2}}\Delta_{(h_{I},0)}^{(\kappa_{I},0)}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{I}}\tilde{K}_{J},\psi_{I}\otimes\varphi_{I}\Big\rangle,\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\tilde{K}_{J}&:=\Big\langle(\vec{1}+|x_{J}|_{\mathrm{vec}}^{2})^{\frac{\vec{M}_{J}}{2}}\Delta_{(h_{J},0)}^{(\kappa_{J},0)}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{J}}K,\psi_{J}\otimes\varphi_{J}\Big\rangle_{J}\\
&\phantom{:}=\Big\langle K,((-\partial_{x})^{\alpha_{J}}\Delta_{-h_{J}}^{\kappa_{J}}[(\vec{1}+|x_{J}|_{\mathrm{vec}}^{2})^{\frac{\vec{M}_{J}}{2}}\psi_{J}])\otimes\varphi_{J}\Big\rangle_{J},\end{split}

(10.8)

which belongs to
\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E}_{I},\vec{F}_{I},\vec{G}_{I},\vec{H}_{I},\vec{W}_{I})
by Lemma
10.26
. Hence, applying the induction hypothesis to
\tilde{K}_{J}
,

\begin{split}\Big|\Big\langle(\vec{1}+|x|_{\mathrm{vec}}^{2})^{\frac{\vec{M}}{2}}\Delta_{(h,0)}^{(\kappa,0)}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}K,\psi\otimes\varphi\Big\rangle\Big|\lesssim\|\psi_{I}\|_{L^{1}}\|\tilde{K}_{J}\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E}_{I},\vec{F}_{I},\vec{G}_{I},\vec{H}_{I},\vec{W}_{I})}\|\varphi_{I}\|_{\operatorname{Atom}(\vec{L}_{I}^{\prime},\vec{N}_{I}^{\prime})}|h_{I}|_{\rm{vec}}^{\vec{N}_{I}^{**}\kappa_{I}},\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\|\tilde{K}_{J}\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E}_{I},\vec{F}_{I},\vec{G}_{I}\vec{H}_{I},\vec{W}_{I})}&=\|\tilde{K}_{J}\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E}_{I},\vec{F}_{I},\vec{W}_{I})}\\
&=\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{I=A\cup B}{A\cap B=\varnothing}}\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\genfrac{}{}{0pt}{}{\Gamma_A\in\mathscr G(\vE,\vF)_A}{(X,U,\vec\eps)\in\R^{4\vn_A+\abs{A}}_{\Gamma}}}{
\Xi_{B}\in\mathscr B(\vec W)_B}}\operatorname{CZ}_{\vec{E},\vec{F}}^{\Gamma;A}(X,U)\Big|\Big\langle\mathbb{D}^{\Gamma;A}_{U}\tilde{K}_{J},(\Phi_{\vec{\varepsilon}}^{X})_{A}\otimes\Xi_{B}\Big\rangle\Big|.\end{split}

(10.9)

Substituting the formula for
\tilde{K}_{J}
from (
10.8
) and rearranging, we see that

\Big\langle\mathbb{D}^{\Gamma;A}_{U}\tilde{K}_{J},(\Phi_{\vec{\varepsilon}}^{X})_{A}\otimes\Xi_{B}\Big\rangle=\Big\langle(\vec{1}+|x_{J}|_{\mathrm{vec}}^{2})^{\frac{\vec{M}_{J}}{2}}\Delta_{(h_{J},0)}^{(\kappa_{J},0)}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{J}}\Big\langle\mathbb{D}_{U}^{\Gamma;A}K,(\Phi_{\vec{\varepsilon}}^{X})_{A}\otimes\Xi_{B}\Big\rangle_{I},\psi_{J}\otimes\varphi_{J}\Big\rangle.

Next, we plan to apply the induction hypothesis to

\tilde{\tilde{K}}_{I}:=\Big\langle\mathbb{D}_{U}^{\Gamma;A}K,(\Phi_{\vec{\varepsilon}}^{X})_{A}\otimes\Xi_{B}\Big\rangle_{I}=\pm\Big\langle K,(\mathbb{D}_{-U}^{\Gamma;A}(\Phi_{\vec{\varepsilon}}^{X})_{A})\otimes\Xi_{B}\Big\rangle_{I},

(10.10)

where the (unimportant) sign depends on the total order of partial derivatives present in
\mathbb{D}_{U}^{\Gamma;A}
. From the expression on the right, we see that
\tilde{\tilde{K}}_{I}\in\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E}_{J},\vec{F}_{J},\vec{G}_{J},\vec{H}_{J},\vec{W}_{J})
by Lemma
10.26
. Thus, using the induction hypothesis, it follows that

\Big|\Big\langle\mathbb{D}^{\Gamma;A}_{U}\tilde{K}_{J},(\Phi_{\vec{\varepsilon}}^{X})_{A}\otimes\Xi_{B}\Big\rangle\Big|\lesssim\|\psi_{J}\|_{L^{1}}\|\tilde{\tilde{K}}_{I}\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E}_{J},\vec{F}_{J},\vec{G}_{J},\vec{H}_{J},\vec{W}_{J})}\|\varphi_{J}\|_{\operatorname{Atom}(\vec{L}_{J}^{\prime},\vec{N}_{J}^{\prime})}|h_{J}|_{\rm{vec}}^{\vec{N}_{J}^{**}\kappa_{J}},

where

\begin{split}\big\|\tilde{\tilde{K}}_{I}\big\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E}_{J},\vec{F}_{J},\vec{G}_{J},\vec{H}_{J},\vec{W}_{J})}&=\big\|\tilde{\tilde{K}}_{I}\big\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E}_{J},\vec{F}_{J},\vec{W}_{J})}\\
&=\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{J=C\cup D}{C\cap D=\varnothing}}\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\genfrac{}{}{0pt}{}{\Gamma_C\in\mathscr G(\vE,\vF)_C}{(X,U,\vec\eps)\in\R^{4\vn_C+\abs{C}}_{\Gamma}}}{
\Xi_{D}\in\mathscr B(\vec W)_D}}\operatorname{CZ}_{\vec{E},\vec{F}}^{\Gamma;C}(X,U)\Big|\Big\langle\mathbb{D}^{\Gamma;C}_{U}\tilde{\tilde{K}}_{I},(\Phi_{\vec{\varepsilon}}^{X})_{C}\otimes\Xi_{D}\Big\rangle\Big|.\end{split}

(10.11)

Substituting the formula for
\tilde{\tilde{K}}_{I}
from (
10.10
) and rearranging, we find that

\Big\langle\mathbb{D}^{\Gamma;C}_{U}\tilde{\tilde{K}}_{I},(\Phi_{\vec{\varepsilon}}^{X})_{C}\otimes\Xi_{D}\Big\rangle=\Big\langle\mathbb{D}^{\Gamma;A\cup C}_{U}K,(\Phi_{\vec{\varepsilon}}^{X})_{A\cup C}\otimes\Xi_{B\cup D}\Big\rangle.

Multiplying this by the factors present in (
10.9
) and (
10.11
), namely

\operatorname{CZ}^{\Gamma;A}_{\vec{E},\vec{F}}(X,U)\times\operatorname{CZ}^{\Gamma;C}_{\vec{E},\vec{F}}(X,U)=\operatorname{CZ}^{\Gamma;A\cup C}_{\vec{E},\vec{F}}(X,U),

and combining the suprema in (
10.9
) and (
10.11
), we arrive at exactly the quantity in the definition of
\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{W})}
, just parameterised
with the new complementary sets
A\cup C
and
B\cup D
in place of the original
I
and
J
, respectively.

Hence, combining all the estimates, we have obtained

\begin{split}&\Big|\Big\langle(\vec{1}+|x|_{\mathrm{vec}}^{2})^{\frac{\vec{M}}{2}}\Delta_{(h,0)}^{(\kappa,0)}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}K,\psi\otimes\varphi\Big\rangle\Big|\\
&\quad\lesssim\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{G},\vec{H},\vec{W})}\|\psi_{J}\|_{L^{1}}\|\psi_{I}\|_{L^{1}}\|\varphi_{J}\|_{\operatorname{Atom}(\vec{L}_{J}^{\prime},\vec{N}_{J}^{\prime})}\|\varphi_{I}\|_{\operatorname{Atom}(\vec{L}_{I}^{\prime},\vec{N}_{I}^{\prime})}|h|_{\rm{vec}}^{\vec{N}_{J}^{**}\kappa}|h|_{\rm{vec}}^{\vec{N}_{I}^{**}\kappa}\\
&\quad=\|K\|_{\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{G},\vec{H},\vec{W})}\|\psi\|_{L^{1}}\|\varphi\|_{\operatorname{Atom}(\vec{L}^{\prime},\vec{N}^{\prime})}|h|_{\rm{vec}}^{\vec{N}^{**}\kappa},\end{split}

and this completes the proof of Lemma
10.27
.
∎

The following corollary of Lemma
10.27
will be more convenient to apply below:

Corollary 10.28
.

Let
\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{G},\vec{H},\vec{L},\vec{L}^{\prime},\vec{M},\vec{N},\vec{N}^{\prime},\vec{W}
be vectors, whose respective components satisfy the assumptions (
10.5
) of Proposition
10.17
.
Let
T\in\mathcal{L}(\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}),\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}))
have Schwartz kernel
K\in\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{G},\vec{H},\vec{W})
.
Let
\varphi
be an
(\vec{L}^{\prime},\vec{N}^{\prime})
-atom on the unit cube
[0,1)^{\vec{n}}
.
Then, for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{\vec{n}}
satisfying

|\alpha|_{\rm{vec}}\leq\lfloor\!\lfloor\vec{N}\rfloor\!\rfloor
, the distributional derivatives

\partial^{\alpha}T\varphi
are given by
L^{\infty}
functions that satisfy, for almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
,

|\Delta_{h}^{\kappa}\partial^{\alpha}T\varphi(x)|\lesssim|h|_{\rm{vec}}^{\vec{N}^{**}\kappa}(\vec{1}+|x|_{\mathrm{vec}}^{2})^{-\frac{\vec{M}}{2}}

(10.12)

for all
(\alpha,\kappa)\in\mathscr{A}(\vec{N})
and all
h\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}

satisfying
|h|_{\rm{vec}}\leq\vec{1}
. Moreover, for all
\nu\in[k]
, all
|\gamma_{\nu}|\leq L_{\nu}
, and almost all
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\ominus\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}}x_{\nu}^{\gamma_{\nu}}T\varphi(x_{\nu},x^{\prime})dx_{\nu}=0.

(10.13)

Proof.

We note that the conclusion (
10.12
) becomes stronger with increasing
\vec{M}
, while the only constraint on
\vec{M}
is
\vec{M}\leq\vec{F}+\vec{n}
from (
10.5
). Hence, without loss of generality, we may take

\vec{M}=\vec{F}+\vec{n}.

(10.14)

Let
\psi_{\nu}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}})
and
\psi:=\bigotimes_{\nu=1}^{k}\psi_{\nu}
. Then
\psi
and
\varphi
satisfy the assumptions of Lemma
10.27
, and hence also the conclusion (
10.7
). But

\begin{split}\mathrm{LHS}\eqref{eq:6.19k}&=\Big|\Big\langle K,(\partial^{\alpha}\Delta_{h}^{\kappa}[(\vec{1}+|x|_{\mathrm{vec}}^{2})^{\frac{\vec{M}}{2}}\psi])\otimes\varphi\Big\rangle\Big|\\
&=\Big|\Big\langle T\varphi,\partial^{\alpha}\Delta_{h}^{\kappa}[(\vec{1}+|x|_{\mathrm{vec}}^{2})^{\frac{\vec{M}}{2}}\psi]\Big\rangle\Big|\\
&=\Big|\Big\langle(\vec{1}+|x|_{\mathrm{vec}}^{2})^{\frac{\vec{M}}{2}}\Delta_{h}^{\kappa}\partial^{\alpha}(T\varphi),\psi\Big\rangle\Big|.\end{split}

With usual bump functions
0\leq\phi_{\nu}\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
of integral one,
consider

\psi_{\nu}=\varepsilon_{\nu}^{-n_{\nu}}\phi_{\nu}(\varepsilon_{\nu}^{-1}(\cdot-x_{\nu})).

Noting that
\|\psi_{\nu}\|_{L^{1}}=\|\phi_{\nu}\|_{L^{1}}=1
, we see that

\mathrm{RHS}\eqref{eq:6.19k}\lesssim|h|_{\rm{vec}}^{\vec{N}^{**}\kappa}

for all these
\psi
, also using the assumptions on
K
and
\varphi
. Considering the limit
\varepsilon_{\nu}\to 0
, we infer that

\Big\|(\vec{1}+|x|_{\mathrm{vec}}^{2})^{\frac{\vec{M}}{2}}\Delta_{h}^{\kappa}\partial^{\alpha}(T\varphi)\Big\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim|h|_{\rm{vec}}^{\vec{N}^{**}\kappa},

which is the same as (
10.12
). With
(\alpha,\kappa)=(\mathbf{0},\vec{0})
, we have in particular
|T\varphi|\lesssim(\vec{1}+|x|_{\mathrm{vec}}^{2})^{-\frac{\vec{M}}{2}}
. From (
10.14
) and
\vec{F}>\vec{L}
, which is part of (
10.5
), it then follows that, for all
|\gamma_{\nu}|\leq L_{\nu}
,

x_{\nu}^{\gamma_{\nu}}T\varphi\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}),

(10.15)

and thus
[x_{\nu}\mapsto x_{\nu}^{\gamma_{\nu}}T\varphi_{[k]}(x_{\nu},x^{\prime})]\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}})
for a.e.
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\ominus\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}
by Fubini’s theorem. Thus, the claimed identity (
10.13
) at least makes sense.

We next use the condition corresponding to the parameter
\vec{H}
in
K\in\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{G},\vec{H},\vec{W})
and the cancellation of the
(\vec{L}^{\prime},\vec{N}^{\prime})
-atom
\varphi
. By (
10.5
), we know that
\vec{H}\geq\vec{L}
and also that
\vec{L}^{\prime}\geq\lfloor\!\lfloor\vec{F}\rfloor\!\rfloor\geq\vec{L}
(using
\vec{F}>\vec{L}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
for the last inequality). By the
\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{G},\vec{H},\vec{W})
condition, for a uniform approximation to the identity
(\chi_{j})_{j=0}^{\infty}
(Definition
10.8
) and arbitrary
\psi_{\iota}\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\iota}})
with
\psi_{[k]\setminus\{\nu\}}:=\bigotimes_{\iota\in[k]\setminus\{\nu\}}\psi_{\iota}
, we have

\langle K,(\psi_{[k]\setminus\{\nu\}}\otimes\chi_{j}x_{\nu}^{\gamma_{\nu}})\otimes\varphi\rangle=\langle K,(\psi_{[k]\setminus\{\nu\}}\otimes\chi_{j}x_{\nu}^{\gamma_{\nu}})\otimes(\varphi_{[k]\setminus\{\nu\}}\otimes\varphi_{\nu})\rangle\underset{j\to\infty}{\longrightarrow}0,

(10.16)

for all
|\gamma_{\nu}|\leq H_{\nu}
, provided that
\varphi_{\nu}\in\mathcal{D}_{|\gamma_{\nu}|}
. In particular, this holds for all
|\gamma_{\nu}|\leq L_{\nu}\leq L_{\nu}^{\prime}\wedge H_{\nu}
, as we just observed. On the other hand, we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{LHS}\eqref{eq:Kx}&=\langle K,(\psi_{[k]\setminus\{\nu\}}\otimes\chi_{j}x_{\nu}^{\gamma_{\nu}})\otimes\varphi\rangle\\
&=\langle T\varphi,\psi_{[k]\setminus\{\nu\}}\otimes\chi_{j}x_{\nu}^{\gamma_{\nu}}\rangle\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\ominus\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}}\psi_{[k]\setminus\{\nu\}}(x^{\prime})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}}(T\varphi)(x)\chi_{j}(x^{\prime})x_{\nu}^{\gamma_{\nu}}dx_{\nu}dx^{\prime}.\end{split}

By (
10.15
), the fact that
\psi_{[k]\setminus\{\nu\}}\in L^{\infty}
, and the pointwise limit
\chi_{j}(x^{\prime})\to 1
, dominated convergence shows that

0=\lim_{j\to\infty}\mathrm{LHS}\eqref{eq:Kx}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\ominus\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}}\psi_{[k]\setminus\{\nu\}}(x^{\prime})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}}(T\varphi)(x)x_{\nu}^{\gamma_{\nu}}dx_{\nu}dx^{\prime}.

Since this holds for
\psi_{[k]\setminus\{\nu\}}=\bigotimes_{\iota\in[k]\setminus\{\nu\}}\psi_{\iota}
with arbitrary
\psi_{\iota}\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n_{\iota}})
, we must have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}}(T\varphi)(x)x_{\nu}^{\gamma_{\nu}}dx_{\nu}=0

for almost every
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}\ominus\mathbb{R}^{n_{\nu}}
. This is exactly (
10.13
) that we wanted to prove. Since we already proved (
10.12
), this completes the proof of Corollary
10.28
.
∎

Corollary 10.29
.

Let
\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{G},\vec{H},\vec{L},\vec{L}^{\prime},\vec{M},\vec{N},\vec{N}^{\prime},\vec{W}
be vectors, whose respective components satisfy the assumptions (
10.5
) of Proposition
10.17
.
Let
T\in\mathcal{L}(\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}),\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}))
have Schwartz kernel
K\in\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{G},\vec{H},\vec{W})
.
Then
T
maps
(\vec{L}^{\prime},\vec{N}^{\prime})
-atoms to a
uniform constant multiple of
(\vec{M},\vec{L},\vec{M},\vec{N})
-molecules.

Proof.

Corollary
10.28
states exactly this in the special case of atoms on the unit cube
[0,1)^{\vec{n}}
. Due to the behaviour with respect to translations and dilations of atoms (Definition
10.1
), molecules (Definition
8.1
), and the
\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{G},\vec{H},\vec{W})
conditions alike, the general case follows from this by changes of variables.
∎

We now come to the main theorem of this section on the boundedness of Calderón–Zygmund operators on matrix-weighted multi-parameter Besov–Triebel–Lizorkin-type spaces.

Theorem 10.30

(The
T(1)
theorem for
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
)
.

Let
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
,
\vec{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\tau\in[0,\infty)
,

r:=r(\pi,\vec{p},\vec{q})\wedge 1
,

\pi\in S_{[2k]}
be admissible for
(\vec{p},\vec{q})
,
and
V
be a matrix weight such that
(\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi,V)
belongs
to one of the main cases (Definition
7.3
).
Let
T\in\mathcal{L}(\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}),\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}))
have Schwartz kernel

K\in\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{G},\vec{H},\vec{W})
for some
\vec{W}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
, where

\vec{E},\vec{F}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
satisfy

\vec{E}>(\tau\vec{n}+\vec{s})_{+},\quad\vec{F}>\Big[\Big(\tfrac{1}{r}-1\Big)\vec{n}-\vec{s}\Big]_{+}\vee\Big(\tfrac{1}{r}+\tau-1\Big)\vec{n}

(10.17)

and
\vec{G},\vec{H}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq-1}^{k}
satisfy

\vec{G}\geq\lfloor\tau\vec{n}+\vec{s}\rfloor_{+},\qquad\vec{H}\geq\lfloor(\tfrac{1}{r}-1)\vec{n}-\vec{s}\rfloor.

(10.18)

Then there exists an operator
\widetilde{T}\in\mathcal{L}(\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V))

that agrees with
T
on
\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.

Proof.

By Proposition
10.2
, the conclusion will follow if we can find

(\vec{L}_{a},\vec{N}_{a})\in(0,\infty)^{2k}
such that
T
maps
(\vec{L}_{a},\vec{N}_{a})
-atoms
to
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}
-synthesis molecules. Recall from Definition
8.6
that
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}
-synthesis molecules are
(\vec{K}^{\prime},\vec{L}^{\prime},\vec{M}^{\prime},\vec{N}^{\prime})
-molecules, where these parameters satisfy (
8.22
) and (
8.23
). By Corollary
10.29
,

T
with kernel
K\in\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{G},\vec{H},\vec{W})

maps
(\vec{L}_{a},\vec{N}_{a})
-atoms to
(\vec{K}^{\prime},\vec{L}^{\prime},\vec{M}^{\prime},\vec{N}^{\prime})
-molecules
(with
\vec{K}^{\prime}=\vec{M}^{\prime}
) under the conditions (
10.5
)
with
(\vec{L}^{\prime},\vec{M}^{\prime},\vec{N}^{\prime})
in place of
(\vec{L},\vec{M},\vec{N})

and
(\vec{L}_{a},\vec{N}_{a})
in place of
(\vec{L}^{\prime},\vec{N}^{\prime})
, respectively.
Combining these results, in order to map certain atoms
to
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}
-synthesis molecules, we need the following:

\begin{cases}\vec{E}\geq\phantom{\lfloor}\vec{N}^{\prime}\phantom{\rfloor}>\tau\vec{n}+\vec{s},\\
\vec{E}>\lfloor\vec{N}^{\prime}\rfloor_{+},\end{cases}\quad\begin{cases}\vec{F}\geq\vec{M}^{\prime}-\vec{n}=\vec{K}^{\prime}-\vec{n}>[(\frac{1}{r}-1)\vec{n}-\vec{s}]_{+}\vee(\frac{1}{r}+\tau-1)\vec{n},\\
\vec{F}>\vec{L}^{\prime}>(\frac{1}{r}-1)\vec{n}-\vec{s}-\vec{1},\end{cases}

and

\begin{cases}\vec{G}\geq\lfloor\vec{N}^{\prime}\rfloor_{+},\quad\vec{N}^{\prime}>\tau\vec{n}+\vec{s},\\
\vec{H}\geq\vec{L}^{\prime}>(\frac{1}{r}-1)\vec{n}-\vec{s}-\vec{1}.\end{cases}

From these, we readily verify that such
\vec{M}^{\prime}\in[0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{N}^{\prime}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
, and
\vec{L}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq-1}^{k}
can be found provided that
\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{G},\vec{H}
satisfy the assumptions of the theorem. This completes the proof.
∎

As a very special case, we obtain a new proof of the following result of Domelevo et al.
[
54
]
:

Corollary 10.31

(
[
54
]
, Theorem 1.1(1))
.

Let
p\in(1,\infty)
,
\vec{E}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
,
\vec{F}\in(0,\infty)^{k}
, and
V\in\mathscr{A}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.
Let
T\in\mathcal{L}(\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}),\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}))
have Schwartz kernel

K\in\operatorname{CZK}(\vec{E},\vec{F},\vec{0},\vec{0},\vec{W})
for some
\vec{W}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
.
Then there exists an operator
\widetilde{T}\in\mathcal{L}(L^{p}(V))

that agrees with
T
on
\mathscr{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.

Proof.

By Theorem
9.1
, we have

L^{p}(V)=\dot{F}^{\vec{0},0}_{p,2}(V)=\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V),

where
\vec{s}=\vec{0}
,
\tau=0
,
\vec{p}=p\cdot\vec{1}
,
\vec{q}=2\cdot\vec{1}
, and
\pi=F
be the identity permutation. For these parameters, we
r(\pi,\vec{p},\vec{q})=\min(p,2)>1
and then
r:=1\wedge r(\pi,\vec{p},\vec{q})=1
.
Thus, the conditions (
10.17
) and (
10.18
) of Theorem
9.1
for this space
become
\vec{E}>\vec{0}
,
\vec{F}>\vec{0}
,
\vec{G}\geq\vec{0}
,
and
\vec{H}\geq\vec{0}
, which are precisely the assumptions of the corollary.
Theorem
10.30
thus applies to prove the existence of a desired

\widetilde{T}\in\mathcal{L}(\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V))=\mathcal{L}(L^{p}(V)),

and this completes the proof of Corollary
10.31
.
∎

Remark 10.32
.

(i)

Theorem
10.30
is completely new in the multi-parameter case.
In the one-parameter case, it essentially reduces to
[
14
, Theorem 6.18]
(with a slightly different range of indices) for the usual Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces with
\tau=0
, and slightly more generally, while in general it deals with a different scale of Besov–Triebel–Lizorkin-type space (see Theorem
9.12
).

(ii)

The paraproduct-free multi-parameter
T(1)
theorem in
L^{p}(V)

was first established in
[
54
]
.
As a special case when
k=2
, Corollary
10.31

reproduces the first assertion of

[
54
, Theorem 1.1(1)]
.
Non-paraproduct-free situations, that is, assuming
T(1)\in\mathrm{BMO}

instead of
T(1)=0
, were also studied in
[
54
,
151
]
.
Here, we only treat paraproduct-free cases, as in the majority of works
on
T(1)
theorems in matrix-weighted Besov–Triebel–Lizorkin spaces.
Obtaining a general
T(1)
theorem for these spaces is left for future work, and should probably first be addressed in the one-parameter case, since it seems to be open even there.

(iii)

Using the identification of
\dot{A}^{\vec{s},\tau}_{\vec{p},\vec{q},\pi}(V)
with other known function spaces, the model of Corollary
10.31
can be used to obtain similar corollaries in other concrete spaces of interest. As an example, we mention the matrix-weighted Sobolev spaces
\dot{W}^{\vec{l}}_{p}(V)=\dot{F}^{\vec{l},0}_{p,2}(V)
(see Theorem
9.4
for this identity), for which Theorem
10.30
is already new. While it is conceivable that this special case could be handled without the full theory behind Theorem
10.30
, we note that it probably cannot be easily deduced from the
L^{p}(V)
results and methods of
[
54
]
. Namely, the approach of
[
54
]
is based the dyadic representation of
[
118
]
involving expansions in terms of Haar functions, which are ill-adapted to the analysis of higher order smoothness spaces, unlike the techniques of Theorem
10.30
. This discussion also shows that our proof of Corollary
10.31
is genuinely different from that in
[
54
]
.

11.
Sobolev-type embeddings

Sobolev-type embedding theorems express a fundamental trade-off between smoothness and integrability of functions. Starting with the pioneering contributions of Sobolev
[
138
]
on embeddings of the form
W^{m}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\hookrightarrow W^{m-l}_{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
with
\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{p}-\frac{l}{n}
, many extensions to different settings are available by now. For Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces in the unweighted one-parameter setting, a Sobolev-type embedding theorem in the full range of indiceswas first obtained by Jawerth
[
94
]
, and another proof is due to Johnsen and Sickel
[
97
]
. Its matrix-weighted one-parameter version is recently due to Bu et al.
[
18
]
, relying on the same interpolation inequality as
[
97
]
.

In this section, we obtain a matrix-weighted multi-parameter extension of these results.
Subsection
11.1
deals with Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces, while Subsection
11.2
derives some consequences in
L^{p}(V)
and
\dot{W}^{\vec{l}}_{p}(V)
spaces.

11.1.
Embeddings of Besov and Triebel–Lizorkin spaces

In this subsection, we characterise several embeddings between two different matrix-weighted spaces in terms of a relatively simply condition (
11.7
); the main result is stated in Theorem
11.2
.
Instead of the interpolation approach of
[
18
,
97
]
used for related results in the one-parameter case, our proof is closer to the original one of Sobolev
[
138
]
, based on the boundedness of fractional integrals, which extends well to the multi-parameter setting. We will also explain the failure of a naïve extension of the method of
[
18
,
97
]
at the end of the subsection. The technical core of our approach is contained in the following:

Proposition 11.1
.

Let
\vec{s}_{0},\vec{s}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
.
Let
0<p_{0}<p_{1}<\infty
and
\vec{q}\in(0,\infty]^{k}
.
Assume that function sequences
\{f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}

and
\{g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}
satisfy,
for every
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
,

2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot(\vec{s}_{1}-\frac{\vec{n}}{p_{1}})}|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x)|\leq 2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot(\vec{s}_{0}-\frac{\vec{n}}{p_{0}})}|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x)|.

(11.1)

If we further assume that
\{g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}

is constant on each
\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
,
then, for every
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
,

\big\|2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}_{1}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\|_{L^{p_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}\leq\big\|2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}_{0}}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\|_{L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}

(11.2)

and there exists a positive constant
C
,
depending only on
s_{0}
,
p_{0}
,
p_{1}
, and
\vec{q}
, such that

\big\|\big\{2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}_{1}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p_{1}}\ell^{\vec{q}}}\leq C\big\|\big\{2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}_{0}}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p_{0}}\ell^{\infty}}.

(11.3)

Proof.

We first prove (
11.2
).
For every
\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
,

\displaystyle\big\|2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}_{1}}f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\|_{L^{p_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}^{p_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}}2^{(\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n})(1-\frac{p_{1}}{p_{0}})}(2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}_{0}}|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|)^{p_{1}}=\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|Q|^{\frac{p_{1}}{p_{0}}}(2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}_{0}}|g_{Q}|)^{p_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg[\sum_{Q\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}|Q|(2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}_{0}}|g_{Q}|)^{p_{0}}\Bigg]^{\frac{p_{1}}{p_{0}}}=\|2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}_{0}}g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\|_{L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})}^{p_{1}},

where
g_{Q}
denotes the constant value of
g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}

on
Q\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
.
This finishes the proof of (
11.2
).

The main part of the proof consists of showing (
11.3
).
For every
\nu\in[k]
, the left-hand side of (
11.3
) increases as
q_{\nu}
decreases; hence it is enough to prove (
11.3
) for smaller values of each
q_{\nu}
. Without loss of generality, we may hence assume that
q_{\nu}\equiv q<p_{0}
for each
\nu\in[k]
.

The left-hand side of (
11.3
) also increases as any of the
|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x)|
increases pointwise. Hence, without loss of generality, we may further assume that (
11.1
) holds as an equality. It then follows that

2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}_{1}}|f_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x)|=2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot(\frac{\vec{n}}{p_{1}}-\frac{\vec{n}}{p_{0}})}2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{s}_{0}}|g_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x)|=:2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}(\frac{1}{p_{1}}-\frac{1}{p_{0}})}|\widetilde{g}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x)|.

Thus, after these reductions, in place of (
11.3
), it is enough to prove that

\big\|\big\{2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}(\frac{1}{p_{1}}-\frac{1}{p_{0}})}\widetilde{g}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{p_{1}}\ell^{q}}\lesssim\|\{\widetilde{g}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{L^{p_{0}}\ell^{\infty}},\qquad 0<q<p_{0}<p_{1}<\infty.

(11.4)

Moreover, denoting

r_{i}:=\frac{p_{i}}{q}\in(1,\infty)\quad(i=0,1),\qquad h_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x):=|\tilde{g}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x)|^{q},\qquad H(x):=\|\{h_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(x)\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\|_{\ell^{\infty}},

we find that

\begin{split}\operatorname{LHS}\eqref{Lplq2}^{q}&=\big\|\big\{2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}(\frac{q}{p_{1}}-\frac{q}{p_{0}})}|\tilde{g}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{q}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{\frac{p_{1}}{q}}\ell^{1}}=\big\|\big\{2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}(\frac{1}{r_{1}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}})}h_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{r_{1}}\ell^{1}},\\
\operatorname{RHS}\eqref{Lplq2}^{q}&=\big\|\{|\tilde{g}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}|^{q}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{\frac{p_{0}}{q}}\ell^{\infty}}=\big\|\{h_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{r_{0}}\ell^{\infty}}=\|H\|_{L^{r_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})},\end{split}

here and thereafter, LHS (
11.4
) [resp. RHS (
11.4
)] means the left-hand
(resp. reight-hand) side of (
11.4
).
Hence (
11.4
) is further equivalent to

\big\|\big\{2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}(\frac{1}{r_{1}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}})}h_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\big\}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}}\big\|_{L^{r_{1}}\ell^{1}}\lesssim\|H\|_{L^{r_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})},\qquad 1<r_{0}<r_{1}<\infty.

(11.5)

Let us denote the constant value of
h_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}
on
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
by
h_{R}
. Then

h_{R}=\langle h_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}\rangle_{R}\leq\langle H\rangle_{R}.

Noting that
2^{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}\cdot\vec{n}}=|R|^{-1}
for
R\in\mathscr{D}_{\vec{\textit{\char 17\relax}}}(\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}})
, we find that,
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{\vec{n}}
,
